4. Within any pack: `CORRECT` overrides superseded body/base wording; `OMIT` must not enter final notes; `VERIFY` must remain unresolved unless the supplied pack itself resolves it.

6. Source URLs/citation labels are evidence metadata. Do not reproduce them in learner-facing notes unless the global prompt explicitly requests citations.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Political Theory & Constitutional Concepts

- **State:** population + defined territory + government + sovereignty. **State ≠ Nation**; nation may rest on shared identity/history/culture without necessarily possessing sovereign governmental organisation.
- **Constitution:** fundamental legal framework creating, allocating and limiting public power. **Constitutionalism / constitutional government:** limited government; governmental authority constrained by constitutional rules, rule of law and enforceable institutional checks. Constitution is supreme over legislative, executive and judicial organs. [Supreme Court of India](https://www.sci.gov.in/constitution/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Rule of Law:** supremacy of law; equality before law; limits on arbitrary public power; protection of liberty/civil rights. **Rule of law ≠ unrestricted parliamentary sovereignty.**
- **Liberty:** absence of unjustified restraint **+** opportunity for development of personality; liberty is compatible with law—`no law → not necessarily liberty`.
- **Equality:** absence of special privilege; equality before law **≠ mechanical identical treatment in unequal circumstances**.
- **Rights ↔ Duties:** correlative; rights create corresponding obligations.
- **Separation of powers:** safeguard against concentration of power; itself recognised as a basic-structure feature. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2012/35071/35071_2012_Judgement_26-Sep-2018.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Due process:** Article 21 text uses **“procedure established by law”**; post-*Maneka Gandhi*, deprivation of life/liberty must follow procedure that is **fair, just and reasonable**, bringing substantive/procedural due-process reasoning into Article 21 jurisprudence. [Science Gateway](https://webapi.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2012/35071/35071_2012_Judgement_26-Sep-2018.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Democracy:** popular sovereignty; representative government in India. Direct-democracy devices: referendum / initiative / recall / plebiscite.
- **Ideology traps:** Marx → class struggle/dialectical materialism; Gandhism & Marxism → common ultimate ideal of stateless society in UPSC framing.

**Parliamentary vs Presidential**

| Parliamentary | Presidential |
|---|---|
| Nominal + real executive; PM/CoM = real executive | President = head of State + head of Government |
| Executive drawn from/fused with legislature | Institutional separation of executive and legislature |
| CoM collectively responsible to popular House | Executive not collectively responsible to legislature |
| Tenure depends on legislative confidence | Fixed executive tenure |
| Lower House may be dissolved under constitutional system | President does not dissolve US House of Representatives |
| India: CoM collectively responsible to Lok Sabha | US = standard comparison |

Base structure preserved from user notes. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…

**India ≠ UK parliamentary sovereignty:** British Parliament traditionally sovereign; Indian Parliament operates under a written supreme Constitution, federal division, Fundamental Rights and judicial review. [Supreme Court of India](https://www.sci.gov.in/constitution/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Eighth Schedule**
- **22 languages**.
- 21st Amendment → Sindhi.
- 71st → Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali.
- 92nd → Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali.
- 96th → Oriya renamed **Odia**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Scheduled languages ≠ “22 official languages of India.”** Article 343 specifies Hindi in Devanagari script as official language of the Union; the Eighth Schedule separately enumerates languages. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Classical-language declarations → dynamic layer; exclude.

**Ninth Schedule**
- Added by **1st Amendment, 1951** with Article 31B.
- Protection **≠ absolute immunity from judicial review**.
- Additions made after **24 April 1973** are open to basic-structure review; *Waman Rao* + *I.R. Coelho* reinforcement. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20… [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/31353.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Tenth Schedule / anti-defection**
- Added by **52nd Amendment, 1985**.
- Party member: disqualified if voluntarily gives up party membership OR votes/abstains against authorised direction without prior permission and conduct is not condoned within 15 days.
- Independent elected member → disqualified if joins political party after election.
- Nominated member → may join a party within first **6 months** after taking seat; joining after that attracts disqualification.
- Merger exception → **≥2/3** members of legislature party agree to merger.
- Old **1/3 split exemption deleted** by 91st Amendment.
- Decision → Speaker/Chairman; Constitution prescribes **no decision deadline**.
- Presiding-officer decision remains subject to judicial review (*Kihoto Hollohan*). [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/cb1b190ea633a1746368ed1fac35fb30.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### C. Amendments & Basic Structure

**Article 368 — amendment procedure**
- Constituent power: **addition / variation / repeal**.
- Bill → **either House of Parliament**; not State Legislature.
- No constitutional requirement of prior Presidential recommendation.
- Each House passes **separately**: majority of total membership **AND** ≥2/3 of members present & voting.
- **No joint sitting** for deadlock.
- President **shall give assent**.
- State ratification, where required → legislatures of **not less than one-half of States**, before Presidential assent. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/cb1b190ea633a1746368ed1fac35fb30.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Ratification category: Arts **54, 55, 73, 162, 241, 279A**; Chapter IV Part V; Chapter V Part VI; Chapter I Part XI; any Seventh Schedule List; representation of States in Parliament; Article 368 itself. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/cb1b190ea633a1746368ed1fac35fb30.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- 24th Amendment expressly affirmed constituent amending power and made Presidential assent mandatory. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/2a321e3e1deabf4a8e1b878b640414d7.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Arts 2–4 State-reorganisation laws are expressly **outside Article 368** and follow ordinary legislative process. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Basic Structure**
- Judicial doctrine; **not an enumerated closed list in constitutional text**.
- *Kesavananda Bharati* (1973): Parliament's amendment power does not extend to destruction of basic structure. [Supreme Court of India](https://www.sci.gov.in/document/his-holiness-kesavananda-bharati-v-state-of-kerala-1973-supp-scr-1/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Representative features recognised through case law: supremacy of Constitution; republican & democratic form; secularism; federalism; separation of powers; rule of law; judicial review; independence of judiciary; free & fair elections; equality; limited amending power; balance/harmony between FR and DPSP. **List is illustrative, not exhaustive.** [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2012/35071/35071_2012_Judgement_26-Sep-2018.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Case sequence**
`Shankari Prasad (1951) → Sajjan Singh (1965) → Golaknath (1967) → 24th Amendment (1971) → Kesavananda Bharati (1973) → 42nd Amendment (1976) → Minerva Mills/Waman Rao (1980) → I.R. Coelho (2007)`

**Landmark amendment hooks**
- **1st, 1951** → Art 15(4); Arts 31A/31B; Ninth Schedule. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/f3789482ad0dfee795e54fb88c0401e6.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **24th, 1971** → explicit constituent power; mandatory Presidential assent. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/2a321e3e1deabf4a8e1b878b640414d7.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **42nd, 1976** → Preamble: Socialist, Secular, Integrity; Fundamental Duties/Part IV-A; Tribunals/Part XIV-A; attempted expansion of amending power.
- **44th, 1978** → Right to Property removed from FR structure; Article 300A route; major Emergency safeguards. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/376b37bf0ae18f1d9276ce226d0e67a1.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **52nd, 1985** → Tenth Schedule.
- **71st, 1992** → Konkani, Manipuri, Nepali.
- **91st, 2003** → strengthened anti-defection law; deleted split exception; CoM ceiling 15%; defector-to-minister restriction. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/1bc0ff014a6cd9946d181eb268f1259b.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **92nd, 2003** → Bodo, Dogri, Maithili, Santhali.
- **96th, 2011** → Oriya → Odia. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

---

### D. Preamble & Constitutional Status

- Source/philosophical anchor → **Objectives Resolution**; authority → **“We, the People of India.”** PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- Current status words → **Sovereign Socialist Secular Democratic Republic**.
- 42nd Amendment substituted original **“Sovereign Democratic Republic”** with present formulation and changed “unity” → “unity and integrity”. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/ca7ce5c746fa7480804bbdeb6cb704f0.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **On 26 January 1950:** India = **Sovereign Democratic Republic**; `Socialist` + `Secular` came later.
- Justice → **3**: social, economic, political.
- Liberty → **5**: thought, expression, belief, faith, worship.
- Equality → **2**: status, opportunity.
- Fraternity → dignity of individual + unity/integrity of Nation.
- **Economic liberty is not a Preamble expression.**
- Preamble **is part of the Constitution** (*Kesavananda Bharati*); earlier *Berubari* view treated it as not part. It aids constitutional interpretation but is not an independent source of legislative power. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/29981.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Preamble is amendable subject to basic-structure limitation; amended only once so far through 42nd Amendment.

**Part XXII — 2026 reinforcement**
- Art 393 → short title: **Constitution of India**.
- Art 394 → specified provisions came into force immediately; remaining provisions on **26 January 1950**.
- Art 394A → authoritative Hindi text.
- Art 395 → repealed Indian Independence Act, 1947 + Government of India Act, 1935 and amending/supplementing enactments, subject to stated exception. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

---

### E. Citizenship & States

**Citizenship — constitutional core**
- **Part II = Articles 5–11**, not Part III. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Art 5 → citizenship at commencement: Indian domicile + specified birth/parentage/residence conditions.
- Art 6 → migrants **from Pakistan to India**.
- Art 7 → specified migrants **to Pakistan**, including return under permit conditions.
- Art 8 → persons of Indian origin residing abroad.
- Art 9 → voluntary acquisition of foreign citizenship defeats citizenship under Arts 5/6/8.
- Art 10 → continuance subject to parliamentary law.
- Art 11 → Parliament may legislate on acquisition, termination and all other citizenship matters. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Citizenship Act, 1955: acquisition routes → birth / descent / registration / naturalisation / incorporation of territory; loss → renunciation / termination / deprivation. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/handle/123456789/19760?sam_handle=123456789%2F2505\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Birth on/after commencement of Citizenship (Amendment) Act, 2003: citizen by birth where **both parents are citizens OR one is citizen and the other is not an illegal migrant**, subject to statutory exceptions. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1522/1/A1955-57Eng.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- India → **single citizenship**; no US-style Union + State citizenship.
- Constitution imposes no “natural-born citizen” requirement for Presidency; naturalised Indian citizen is not excluded merely by mode of citizenship.
- **Aadhaar ≠ proof of citizenship/domicile** — retain only as PYQ elimination cross-link.

**Union / States — Articles 1–4**
- Art 1 → **India, that is Bharat, shall be a Union of States**.
- Art 2 → Parliament: admit into Union / establish new States.
- Art 3 → Parliament: form new State; increase/diminish area; alter boundaries; alter name.
- Art 3 Bill → Presidential recommendation; where State area/boundary/name affected, President refers it to concerned State Legislature for its **views**. State consent/veto **not required**.
- Art 4 → consequential First/Fourth Schedule changes; laws under Arts 2/3 **not deemed constitutional amendments under Art 368**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/cb1b190ea633a1746368ed1fac35fb30.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Reorganisation chronology — durable PYQ set**

| Year | Anchor |
|---|---|
| 1948 | Dhar Commission → administrative convenience preferred over language |
| 1948–49 | JVP Committee → initially rejected linguistic reorganisation |
| **1953** | **Andhra State** → first linguistic State |
| 1953 | States Reorganisation Commission: **Fazl Ali + K.M. Panikkar + H.N. Kunzru** |
| **1956** | States Reorganisation Act + 7th Amendment; Andhra Pradesh formed |
| **1963** | Nagaland → State of Nagaland Act, 1962; **not created by Constitutional Amendment** |
| **1966** | Punjab reorganisation → Haryana |
| **1974** | Sikkim → associate-State phase, 35th Amendment |
| **1975** | Sikkim → full-fledged State, **36th Amendment** |
| **1987** | Goa, Mizoram, Arunachal Pradesh Statehood |
| **2000** | Chhattisgarh → Uttarakhand → Jharkhand |
| **2014** | Telangana |

Nagaland/Arunachal Statehood rests on parliamentary reorganisation statutes; the 36th Amendment made Sikkim a full-fledged State. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/06/be778e1cd557ad1e2e8fb67dc24e7d43.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

**Sources of Constitution — recall table**

| Source | High-yield borrowings |
|---|---|
| Government of India Act, 1935 | federal scheme; Governor; judiciary; PSCs; emergency/admin details |
| Britain | parliamentary government; rule of law; cabinet; legislative procedure; single citizenship; writs; privileges; bicameralism |
| USA | Fundamental Rights; judicial independence/review; Presidential impeachment; removal of SC/HC judges; Vice-President |
| Ireland | DPSP; nomination to Rajya Sabha; method of Presidential election |
| **Canada** | strong-centre federation; **residuary powers with Centre**; appointment of Governors; SC advisory jurisdiction |
| Australia | Concurrent List; freedom of trade/commerce/intercourse; joint sitting |
| Weimar Germany | emergency-related suspension of Fundamental Rights |
| USSR | Fundamental Duties; social/economic/political justice ideal |
| France | Republic; liberty/equality/fraternity |
| South Africa | constitutional-amendment procedure; Rajya Sabha election |
| Japan | procedure established by law |

Base source table substantially retained. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…

**UPPCS recall priorities**
- `Part II = Citizenship`; `Part XX = Art 368`; `Part IX-A = Municipalities`; `Part XIV-A = Tribunals`.
- `4th = RS seats`; `7th = Lists`; `8th = languages`; `9th = protected Acts/Regulations`; `10th = defection`.
- 42nd → 1976; 52nd → 1985; 86th → 2002; 91st/92nd → 2003; 96th → 2011.
- State chronology traps: `Nagaland 1963 → Haryana/Punjab reorganisation 1966 → Sikkim 1975 → Arunachal 1987`; `Haryana 1966 → Goa 1987 → Jharkhand 2000 → Telangana 2014`.
- SRC personnel: Fazl Ali ≠ K.T. Shah/Pattabhi Sitaramayya; members = Panikkar + Kunzru.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist

- Constitution **≠** constitutionalism; constitutional government = **limited government**.
- Parliamentary executive responsible to legislature **≠** fixed independent executive.
- British parliamentary sovereignty **≠** Indian parliamentary supremacy.
- Article 21 text: procedure established by law **≠** a purely formal procedure after *Maneka*.
- Equality **≠** identical treatment regardless of circumstances.
- Citizenship = **Part II**, not Part III.
- Fourth Schedule = RS seats **≠** Ninth Schedule.
- Eighth Schedule languages **≠** “national languages” / all “official languages”.
- Ninth Schedule **≠** blanket judicial immunity.
- Nominated legislator: party joining **within 6 months ≠ after 6 months**.
- Merger exception = **≥2/3**; old split exception = **1/3 deleted**.
- Tenth Schedule decision → Speaker/Chairman **≠ President + CoM**.
- Constitutional-amendment bill → either House **≠ Lok Sabha only / State Legislature**.
- Amendment bill: no prior Presidential recommendation; **no joint sitting**; final assent mandatory.
- Special majority **≠** State ratification in every amendment.
- Ratification = **not less than half**, not all States.
- Basic structure = judicial doctrine **≠ enumerated constitutional list**.
- Preamble part of Constitution **≠ independently enforceable source of power**.
- 26 Jan 1950 status = **Sovereign Democratic Republic** ≠ current five status words.
- 42nd added **Socialist + Secular + Integrity**.
- Art 2 admission/establishment **≠** Art 3 reorganisation of existing States.
- State Legislature's Art 3 **views ≠ consent/veto**.
- Andhra **State 1953** ≠ Andhra **Pradesh 1956**.
- Sikkim **associate phase 1974 ≠ full State 1975**.
- Nagaland Statehood = ordinary parliamentary Act **≠ Constitutional Amendment**.
- Naturalised citizen **≠ constitutionally barred from Presidency**.
- Aadhaar **≠ citizenship/domicile proof**.
- Residuary powers source → **Canada ≠ Australia**.

### CORRECT

- Base: **“Articles 5–11 in Part III”** → **Articles 5–11 are Part II: Citizenship** → official Constitution. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Base: **post-2004 citizen by birth only if both parents citizens** → **both citizens OR one citizen + other not illegal migrant**, subject to §3 exceptions → Citizenship Act, 1955. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1522/1/A1955-57Eng.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Base: **Sikkim full-fledged State / 36th Amendment = 1974** → **35th Amendment 1974 = associate State; 36th Amendment 1975 = full-fledged State**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/2a875dab390ddae1419f7f050ccd6ed0.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Base Part XV range: **324–329A** → **Arts 324–329; Article 329A stands omitted**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Base wording: **“22 official languages of India”** → **22 languages listed in Eighth Schedule; Article 343 separately specifies official language of Union**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Anti-defection shorthand **“<2/3 switching = defection”** → individual disqualification grounds apply independently; **≥2/3 is the merger exception**, not the definition of defection. ## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. State, Nation, Sovereignty

| Concept | Payload |
|---|---|
| **State** | Political/legal organisation with **population + definite territory + organised government + sovereignty** |
| **Sovereignty** | Supreme authority internally + independence from external control |
| **Nation** | Primarily socio-cultural/historical community; does **not** necessarily possess sovereign statehood |
| **UPSC best-fit State** | Permanent community + definite territory + independence from external control + organised government |
| **Trap** | International recognition ≠ one of the classic four essential elements used in these PYQs |

Base notes already distinguish Nation from State but need the classic four-element formulation tightened. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…

### B. Constitution vs Constitutionalism

| Constitution | Constitutionalism |
|---|---|
| Establishes basic legal-political framework | Controls **how governmental power may be exercised** |
| Creates/allocates governmental authority | **Limits** governmental authority |
| May exist even under authoritarian government | Requires effective restraints against arbitrary power |
| Document/institutional framework | Principle of **limited government + Rule of Law** |

- Chief PYQ formula: **Constitution → defines AND limits governmental power.**
- **Constitutional government = limited government.**
- Power must operate under constitutional rules, not unrestricted will.
- Effective restraints protect individual liberty.
- Mere possession of a constitution ≠ constitutionalism.
- Decentralisation/dispersal of power + institutional restraints support constitutionalism. Laxmikanth explicitly treats constitutionalism as limited government and the antithesis of arbitrary power. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

### C. Parliamentary vs Presidential Government

| Axis | Parliamentary | Presidential |
|---|---|---|
| Executive | nominal head + real political executive | President = head of State + government |
| Legislature–executive relation | fusion/co-operation | institutional separation |
| Political responsibility | executive responsible to legislature | executive not collectively responsible to legislature |
| Core Indian rule | CoM collectively responsible to **Lok Sabha** | — |
| Tenure | ministry survives while retaining confidence | executive has fixed tenure |
| Removal | loss of confidence/no-confidence | impeachment-type constitutional removal |
| Ministers | commonly legislators; Indian minister may remain non-MP only **6 consecutive months** | US secretaries are outside Congress |
| Lower House | may be dissolved within constitutional system | US President cannot dissolve House of Representatives |
| Exam marker | **responsibility/accountability** | **separation + fixed tenure** |

Article 75(3) expressly makes the Union Council of Ministers collectively responsible to the House of the People; Article 75(5) supplies the six-month non-member qualification. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/06/a714fb6b673825b7170f2f0c979a913c.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) The reference text likewise identifies collective responsibility as the bedrock parliamentary principle. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

**India vs Britain**
- Both → parliamentary/Cabinet government.
- Britain → parliamentary sovereignty.
- India → parliamentary legislative power limited by written Constitution, federal distribution, Fundamental Rights and judicial review.
- Britain → hereditary head; India → elected republican head.
- UPSC 2021 also treats constitutional challenges to Parliamentary enactments/amendments as Constitution Bench matters in the stated context.

### D. Liberty

- **Best-fit:** opportunity/conditions for **full development of the individual**.
- Not:
  - mere absence of restraint;
  - unrestricted choice;
  - licence to do anything.
- **Law and liberty:** absence of law does **not** automatically produce liberty; ordered liberty requires lawful restraint.
- **Negative liberty:** freedom **from** coercion/interference.
- **Positive liberty:** enabling conditions/opportunities for development.
- **Best institutional safeguard in liberal democracy:** **separation of powers**.
- Liberty = qualified, not absolute. Base-note formulation correctly captures both absence of restraint and opportunity for personal development. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…

### E. Equality

- Core meaning:
  - **absence of special privileges**;
  - equality of status/opportunity;
  - like cases treated alike.
- Equality ≠ mechanical identical treatment of unequal situations.
- Political-theory distinction only here; detailed Articles 14–18 → Fundamental Rights node.

### F. Rights & Duties

- PYQ formula: **Rights are correlative with Duties.**
- Rights ≠ purely personal claims independent of society.
- Rights and duties are mutually connected: enjoyment/protection of rights creates corresponding obligations on others/institutions.
- Detailed Fundamental Duties list → separate DPSP & Fundamental Duties node.

### G. Rule of Law

**PYQ-tested core**
1. **Limitation of powers**
2. **Equality before law**
3. **Liberty and civil rights**

**NOT a feature:** “people’s responsibility to Government.”

**Diceyan formulation**
- absence of arbitrary power;
- equality before ordinary law;
- primacy of individual rights in the British formulation.
- **India trap:** individual rights derive from the Constitution; Dicey’s third formulation does not transplant literally.

### H. Due Process vs Procedure Established by Law

- **Constitutional text — Article 21:** life/personal liberty may be deprived only according to **procedure established by law**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Due Process of Law — UPSC 2023 best-fit:** **principle of natural justice**.
- Historical sequence:
  - **A.K. Gopalan (1950):** narrow reading; existence of legally prescribed procedure was central.
  - **Maneka Gandhi (1978):** procedure affecting liberty must be **just, fair and reasonable**, not arbitrary/oppressive.
- Therefore:
  - Constitution text ≠ American-style explicit “due process” clause.
  - Indian Article 21 jurisprudence subsequently incorporated substantive/procedural fairness associated with due process. The Supreme Court continues to state that Article 21 requires a just, fair and reasonable procedure. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2017/22086/22086_2017_Judgement_20-Mar-2018.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Natural justice core:** unbiased decision-maker + fair opportunity of hearing + fair procedure.

### I. Democracy

- **Popular sovereignty:** ultimate political authority rests with people.
- **Representative/indirect democracy:** elected representatives exercise governmental power → Indian model.
- **Direct-democracy devices:**
  - Referendum → electorate decides proposal/question.
  - Initiative → citizens initiate legislative proposal.
  - Recall → voters remove representative before normal term ends.
  - Plebiscite → popular vote/opinion, often on major political/territorial question.
- UPSC conceptual angle: democratic virtue lies in activating the intelligence and character of ordinary citizens.

### J. Political Ideology — PYQ-Limited Payload

| PYQ distinction | Fact |
|---|---|
| Marx: explanation of class struggle | **Dialectical materialism** |
| Gandhism + Marxism: common point | ultimate ideal of **stateless society** |
| Not common to both | class struggle; economic determinism; abolition of private property |

- Do **not** expand into ideological theory beyond these tested distinctions.

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer
- Parliamentary form historically developed first in **Great Britain/UK** — `UPPCS-2018-GS1-Q019`.
- Parliamentary-government recognition test: **executive responsibility to legislature** — `UPPCS-2025-GS1-Q054`.
- Rule of Law application angle: **effective access to justice**, especially for socially/economically weaker groups; PIL tested as one mechanism — `UPPCS-2025-GS1-Q047`.
- Preserve direct one-line factual formulations alongside UPSC’s best-fit conceptual definitions.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- **Constitution ≠ constitutionalism**
- Constitution present ≠ government necessarily limited
- Constitutional government = **limited government**, not merely elected/popular government
- Parliamentary government → executive **responsible to legislature**
- Presidential government → executive **not collectively responsible** to legislature
- Head of State ≠ necessarily head of government
- Parliamentary minister ≠ necessarily sitting MP at every instant; India allows **6-month** window
- Indian Parliament ≠ British Parliament in sovereignty
- Liberty ≠ absence of all restraints
- Liberty ≠ licence/unrestricted choice
- Equality ≠ identical treatment in every circumstance
- Equality → absence of **privileges**
- Rights ≠ independent of duties; **rights ↔ duties**
- Rule of Law → limitation of power + equality + civil liberty
- Rule of Law ≠ responsibility of people to government
- “Procedure established by law” ≠ textual “due process of law”
- Post-*Maneka*: procedure must still be **fair, just, reasonable**
- Due process → **natural justice**
- State ≠ Nation
- State → population + territory + government + sovereignty
- Marx class struggle → **dialectical materialism**
- Gandhism–Marxism commonality → **stateless society**, not class struggle

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT
- **Base:** State requires “recognised and organised government” → **Core four:** population + definite territory + organised government + sovereignty; external recognition should not be treated as a fifth indispensable element for this PYQ framework → UPSC 2021 State-definition demand.
- **Base:** PM = leader of majority party → **Qualify:** PM heads government; government may be single-party or coalition and must command/retain Lok Sabha confidence.
- **Base:** CoM members share same political ideology → **Qualify:** typical in single-party ministry; coalition ministries rest on political agreement/consensus.
- **Base:** ministers are members of executive + legislature → **Qualify:** Article 75(5) permits a minister to remain outside both Houses for up to six consecutive months. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/06/a714fb6b673825b7170f2f0c979a913c.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Base:** presidential model has “strict” separation of powers → use **separation of powers with institutional checks/balances**; avoid suggesting completely isolated organs.
- **Base:** President rules through a “Kitchen Cabinet” → do **not** treat Kitchen Cabinet as a defining constitutional institution; retain only the durable distinction that presidential advisers/secretaries are not collectively responsible to Congress.
- **Base:** Gopalan formulation can read like present law → mark explicitly **historical/narrow**; current Article 21 interpretation requires fair, just and reasonable procedure after *Maneka Gandhi*. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2017/22086/22086_2017_Judgement_20-Mar-2018.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### VERIFY
- None.

### OMIT
- Detailed merits/demerits catalogue of parliamentary government beyond PYQ-relevant distinctions.
- Semi-presidential country-list examples; not needed for identified canonical demand.
- Detailed socialism/secularism/Preamble discussion → owned by **Preamble & Constitutional Status**.
- Detailed Article 14–22 provisions/case catalogue → owned by **Fundamental Rights & writs**.
- Basic-structure case chronology → owned by **Amendments & Basic Structure**.
- Dynamic office-holder/current political examples.
- Encyclopaedic ideological theory beyond the two canonical political-ideology demands.

Current taxonomy + complete canonical PYQ gateway + base notes + Laxmikanth/reference verification answer all identified PYQ demands. No test-series/topper/coaching supplement required.

### C. Eighth Schedule / language traps
- **22 languages:** Assamese, Bengali, Bodo, Dogri, Gujarati, Hindi, Kannada, Kashmiri, Konkani, Maithili, Malayalam, Manipuri, Marathi, Nepali, Odia, Punjabi, Sanskrit, Santhali, Sindhi, Tamil, Telugu, Urdu.
- Originally **14**.
- Additions:
  - **21st Amendment (1967):** Sindhi
  - **71st Amendment (1992):** Konkani + Manipuri + Nepali
  - **92nd Amendment (2003):** Bodo + Dogri + Maithili + Santhali
  - **96th Amendment (2011):** Oriya → **Odia**. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…
- **Bhojpuri ≠ Eighth Schedule**.
- Eighth Schedule languages ≠ “official languages of India”.
- Union official language provision: **Art. 343**.
- **Classical-language status ≠ Eighth Schedule inclusion.**
  - Treat classical-status declarations as a **replaceable current layer**.
  - Historical PYQ anchors only: Kannada + Telugu were classical languages in UPSC-2014 context; Odia obtained classical status in 2014.

### D. Ninth Schedule
- **Art. 31B + Ninth Schedule** → inserted by **1st Constitutional Amendment Act, 1951**. [India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_AN_91_150_00002_00002_1665722041433\&filename=constitution_of_india.pdf\&type=actfile\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Purpose/background → constitutional protection especially associated initially with land-reform/Zamindari-abolition legislation.
- A law is placed in Ninth Schedule **through constitutional amendment**.
- Protection is **not absolute / not blanket judicial immunity**.
- **24 April 1973 cut-off**:
  - Waman Rao → post-Kesavananda inclusions subject to basic-structure limitation.
  - **I.R. Coelho (2007)** → post-24 April 1973 Ninth-Schedule insertions remain reviewable if they damage/destroy basic structure; judicial review cannot be eliminated by mere placement in Ninth Schedule. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/judgment/judis/28469.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Do not freeze current count of Acts/Regulations**.

### E. Tenth Schedule / Anti-defection
- Added by **52nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1985**.
- **Party member disqualified if:**
  - voluntarily gives up party membership; **formal resignation not necessary**;
  - votes/abstains contrary to party direction without prior permission **and action not condoned within 15 days**.
- **Independent member:** joining a political party after election → disqualification.
- **Nominated member:** may join a political party **within 6 months of taking seat**; joining **after 6 months** attracts disqualification. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…
- **Merger exception:** ≥ **2/3 of legislature party** agree to merger.
- Presiding-officer exception: member elected Speaker/Chairman etc. may leave party while holding office and may rejoin after ceasing to hold it.
- **91st Amendment (2003):** deleted the old **one-third split exemption**; one-third was **split**, not merger. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…
- Decision → **Speaker/Chairman**, not President/Governor.
- Speaker/Chairman's decision → subject to judicial review.
- Tenth Schedule itself gives **no decision deadline**.
- **Keisham Meghachandra Singh (2020):** SC indicated disqualification petitions should ordinarily be decided within **3 months**, absent exceptional circumstances. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2019/27562/27562_2019_4_1501_19772_Judgement_21-Jan-2020.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Constitution/Tenth Schedule expressly uses **“political party”** → UPSC-2025 trap.

### F. Seventh Schedule — high-yield entries

| Union List | State List | Concurrent List |
|---|---|---|
| Defence | Public order | Criminal law/procedure |
| Foreign affairs | Police | Marriage/divorce |
| Railways | Public health & sanitation | Succession |
| Banking | Agriculture | Education |
| Stock exchanges/futures markets | Land | Forests |
| Census | Local government | Protection of wild animals & birds |
| **Inter-State trade & commerce** | Trade & commerce **within State** subject to Concurrent Entry 33 | Economic & social planning |
| **Inter-State migration & quarantine** | — | Population control & family planning |
| Corporation tax | — | Adulteration of foodstuffs/other goods |
| — | — | Prevention of cruelty to animals |

- **Art. 246:** core legislative-distribution anchor.
- **Art. 248:** residuary legislative power → Parliament.
- UPSC-2024 trap: **inter-State quarantine = Union List**. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…
- UPPCS-2024 trap: **succession + adulteration of goods = Concurrent List**; generic “trade & commerce” cannot be classified without the inter-State/within-State qualifier.
- **42nd Amendment — State → Concurrent:** education; forests; protection of wild animals & birds; weights & measures; administration of justice/constitution & organisation of courts except SC/HC.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- Eighth-Schedule language **≠** official language **≠** classical language.
- **Dogri**, not `Dongri`.
- Bhojpuri **not** in Eighth Schedule.
- Third Schedule oaths **≠** President's oath → Art. 60.
- Fourth Schedule = Rajya Sabha seats **≠** Second Schedule.
- Fifth Schedule Scheduled Areas **≠** Sixth Schedule tribal areas of ATM2.
- Part VII **repealed**; Union Territories = **Part VIII**.
- Municipalities = **Part IX-A**, not Part XI.
- Tribunals = **Part XIV-A**, not Part XI/X.
- Elections = Part XV; Emergency = XVIII; Amendment = XX.
- Art. 43A workers **≠** Art. 43B co-operatives.
- Art. 338B NCBC **≠** Art. 340 backward-class commission power.
- Art. 155 appointment of Governor **≠** removal.
- Art. 162 = **extent**, not “extension”, of State executive power.
- Ninth Schedule protection **≠** immunity from basic-structure review.
- Ninth Schedule placement requires constitutional amendment **≠** ordinary statutory insertion.
- Nominated legislator: may join party **within** 6 months; disqualification for joining **after** 6 months.
- Independent elected member joining party → disqualified.
- Anti-defection decision: Speaker/Chairman **≠** President/Governor.
- Tenth Schedule has no statutory decision deadline **≠** SC's ordinary 3-month judicial direction.
- **1/3 split exemption deleted** ≠ **2/3 merger exception retained**.
- Inter-State trade/migration/quarantine → Union List **≠** State List.
- Trade/commerce within State → State List, subject to Concurrent Entry 33.
- Education/forest/wildlife → Concurrent List after 42nd Amendment.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Amendment architecture

**Article 368 — Part XX**

- Parliament exercises **constituent power**.
- Amendment by **addition / variation / repeal**.
- Constitutional Amendment Bill → **either House of Parliament**; State legislatures cannot initiate it.
- Minister **or private member** may introduce.
- **No prior recommendation of President**.
- Each House passes **separately**:
  - majority of **total membership** of that House **AND**
  - ≥ **⅔ of members present and voting**.
- **No joint sitting** on disagreement.
- Where proviso to Art. 368(2) applies → ratification by legislatures of **not less than one-half of States** before presentation to President.
- President **shall give assent**; cannot return/withhold a duly passed Constitutional Amendment Bill.
- Art. 368 technically provides **two** amendment methods; simple-majority alterations under other Articles form a **third practical category**, but are outside Art. 368. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

Base notes already capture most of this procedure. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… Reference text independently confirms the sequence. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

### B. Three practical routes

| Route | Requirement | High-yield examples |
|---|---|---|
| **Simple majority — outside Art. 368** | ordinary legislative majority | admission/establishment/formation of States; alteration of State areas/boundaries/names; creation/abolition of State Legislative Councils; citizenship; Second Schedule; parliamentary procedure/privileges; official language; Fifth & Sixth Schedules |
| **Special majority of Parliament** | majority of total membership + ≥⅔ present & voting, **each House separately** | Fundamental Rights; DPSPs; residual constitutional provisions not in categories 1 or 3 |
| **Special majority + State ratification** | above parliamentary majority + resolutions of ≥½ State legislatures | specified federal provisions below |

### C. State-ratification list — exact Art. 368(2) proviso

Requires ratification by **not less than one-half of States**:

- **Articles 54, 55** → President's election.
- **Articles 73, 162** → extent of Union/State executive power.
- **Article 241** → High Courts for UTs.
- **Article 279A** → GST Council.
- **Chapter IV, Part V** → Union Judiciary.
- **Chapter V, Part VI** → High Courts in States.
- **Chapter I, Part XI** → legislative relations.
- **Any List in Seventh Schedule**.
- **Representation of States in Parliament**.
- **Article 368 itself**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

**Elimination:** Article **158—conditions of Governor's office** is not in this list; Article **162—State executive power** is.

### D. Basic Structure doctrine

- **Judicial doctrine; Constitution contains no exhaustive textual definition/list.**
- **Kesavananda Bharati (1973):** Parliament may amend any part, including Fundamental Rights, but cannot **damage/destroy/alter the Basic Structure**.
- Therefore: **constitutional provision amendable ≠ Parliament has unlimited amending power**.
- **Judicial review** and **limited amending power** themselves form part of Basic Structure.
- Basic Structure elements have accumulated **case-by-case**; no closed enumeration. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2013/21321/21321_2013_32_1501_28728_Judgement_20-Jul-2021.pdf)

Reference source traces the doctrine from Shankari Prasad through Waman Rao. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

### E. Consolidated Basic Structure payload

| Cluster | Established elements |
|---|---|
| **Constitutional identity** | supremacy of Constitution; sovereign-democratic-republican character; secularism; federalism; unity & integrity |
| **Institutional architecture** | separation of powers; parliamentary system; rule of law; judicial review; independence of judiciary; limited amending power |
| **Democratic/rights core** | equality; free & fair elections; freedom & dignity of individual; essence/principles underlying FRs; effective access to justice |
| **Judicial powers** | SC powers under Arts. 32, 136, 141, 142; HC powers under Arts. 226, 227 |
| **Social constitutionalism** | welfare state/social justice; harmony & balance between FRs and DPSPs |

Reference compilation records these elements across successive Supreme Court decisions. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20… The Supreme Court also expressly identifies independence of judiciary as part of Basic Structure. [Supreme Court of India](https://www.sci.gov.in/constitution/)

### F. Judgment ↔ Amendment chain

| Sequence | Durable proposition |
|---|---|
| **Shankari Prasad (1951)** | Constitutional amendment ≠ “law” under Art. 13; Parliament could amend FRs; challenge to 1st Amendment failed |
| **Sajjan Singh (1964 judgment)** | Reaffirmed Shankari Prasad position |
| **Golak Nath (1967)** | Reversed course: constitutional amendment treated as “law” for Art. 13; Parliament could not abridge FRs |
| **24th Amendment (1971)** | Response to Golak Nath → Arts. 13 & 368 amended; constituent power expressly stated; Art. 13 excluded from Art. 368 amendments; President **shall assent**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/2a321e3e1deabf4a8e1b878b640414d7.pdf) |
| **Kesavananda Bharati (1973)** | Upheld 24th Amendment; FRs amendable, **subject to Basic Structure limitation** |
| **39th Amendment (1975) → Indira Nehru Gandhi case (1975)** | attempt to insulate specified high-office election disputes; relevant provision struck for violating Basic Structure |
| **42nd Amendment (1976)** | inserted Art. 368(4),(5): attempted to bar judicial challenge and declare unlimited amending power. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/9f820a53b6e6840a459bde27eb5d24e5.pdf) |
| **Minerva Mills (1980)** | Art. 368(4),(5) invalid; **limited amending power + judicial review** = Basic Structure; harmony between FRs & DPSPs also protected. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/judgment/judis/28469.pdf) |
| **Waman Rao (1980)** | reaffirmed Basic Structure; 24 April 1973 became critical cutoff for post-Kesavananda Ninth-Schedule insertions |
| **I.R. Coelho (2007)** | Ninth Schedule ≠ blanket immunity where post-24 April 1973 insertion damages Basic Structure |
| **99th Amendment/NJAC case** | amendment struck; **independence of judiciary** held Basic Structure. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/43070.pdf) |

### G. Landmark amendments

| Amendment | Core payload |
|---|---|
| **1st CA, 1951** | Added **Art. 15(4)**; altered Art. 19 restrictions/state-monopoly protection; inserted **Arts. 31A, 31B + Ninth Schedule**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/f3789482ad0dfee795e54fb88c0401e6.pdf) |
| **24th CA, 1971** | Post-Golak Nath; Art. **13(4)**; Art. **368(1),(3)**; expressly recognised Parliament's constituent power; President **shall assent**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/2a321e3e1deabf4a8e1b878b640414d7.pdf) |
| **42nd CA, 1976 — “Mini-Constitution”** | Preamble: **Socialist, Secular, Integrity**; Part IVA/Fundamental Duties; Arts. **39A, 43A, 48A**; Part XIVA/Tribunals; moved **administration of justice, forests, protection of wild animals/birds, education, weights & measures** from State to Concurrent domain; extended Art. 31C to all DPSPs; inserted Art. 368(4),(5). The Art. 31C extension and Art. 368(4),(5) did **not survive** Minerva Mills. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/9f820a53b6e6840a459bde27eb5d24e5.pdf) |
| **44th CA, 1978** | Removed property from FR framework; inserted **Art. 300A**; restored LS term from 6→5 years; President may require CoM advice to be reconsidered once; omitted **Art. 329A**; emergency safeguards—`internal disturbance → armed rebellion`, written Cabinet decision, parliamentary approval within one month by constitutional special majority, six-month renewals. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/376b37bf0ae18f1d9276ce226d0e67a1.pdf) |
| **91st CA, 2003** | Union CoM incl. PM ≤ **15% of Lok Sabha**; State CoM incl. CM ≤ **15% of Assembly**, minimum **12**; defectors disqualified from ministership/remunerative political posts for specified period; **1/3 split exception deleted**; 2/3 merger protection remains. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/1bc0ff014a6cd9946d181eb268f1259b.pdf) |

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

### High-value direct recall

| Amendment / case | Recall hook |
|---|---|
| **1st** | 1951 → 15(4), 31A, 31B, Ninth Schedule |
| **24th** | 1971 → answer to Golak Nath |
| **39th** | 1975 → Indira Gandhi election-case link |
| **42nd** | 1976 → “Mini-Constitution”; duties/basic-structure confrontation |
| **44th** | 1978 → rollback/emergency safeguards/property |
| **91st** | 2003 → CoM cap + anti-defection strengthening |
| **99th** | 2014 → NJAC; struck on Basic Structure |

- **Canonical chronology:** `Shankari Prasad → Sajjan Singh → Golak Nath → 24th CA → Kesavananda → 39th/Indira Gandhi → 42nd CA → Minerva Mills`.
- **Q127 bridge-only recall:** 52nd–1985; 86th–2002; 96th–2011. Do not import their full subject matter into this pack.
- UPPCS repeatedly asks **case name/year/amendment number directly**, unlike UPSC's heavier qualifier/statement style.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist

- **Can amend any provision** ≠ can **destroy Basic Structure**.
- Basic Structure **judicially evolved** ≠ constitutionally defined exhaustive list.
- Constitutional Amendment Bill → **either House**, not Lok Sabha only.
- State legislatures **ratify specified amendments** ≠ initiate Constitutional Amendment Bills.
- **No prior Presidential recommendation** ≠ President has discretionary veto; after passage, President **shall assent**.
- Constitutional Amendment Bill → **no joint sitting**.
- Special majority = **majority of total membership + ≥⅔ present & voting** ≠ ⅔ of total membership.
- State ratification = **≥½ States**, not all States.
- Ratification by States → **simple majority**, not Art. 368 special majority.
- Art. **162** requires State ratification if amended; Art. **158** does not.
- Simple-majority constitutional changes ≠ amendments “for purposes of Article 368”.
- Art. 368 itself describes **two** methods; three-category classification includes simple-majority changes outside Art. 368.
- Art. 368(4),(5) may appear in historical amendment text ≠ operative unlimited amending power; **Minerva Mills invalidated them**.
- **39th**, not 44th, contained the impugned PM-election judicial-review exclusion; **44th omitted Art. 329A**.
- 91st: **deleted 1/3 split exception** ≠ changed a 1/3 “merger” rule into 2/3.
- Ninth Schedule ≠ absolute immunity from Basic Structure review.
- `judicial review` as constitutional power/basic feature ≠ an expressly enumerated constitutional definition of “Basic Structure”.
- `UPSC-CSE-2020-GS1-Q013`: canonical key = **D**; final note must preserve the textual-vs-judicial-doctrine distinction rather than state that the Constitution expressly defines/names these doctrines.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT

| Base/source fact | Corrected fact | Authority/reason |
|---|---|---|
| “Parliament can't amend provisions which form Basic Structure.” | Parliament may amend **any provision**, but cannot **damage/destroy Basic Structure**. | Kesavananda; Art. 368 doctrine. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2017/18297/18297_2017_Judgement_17-Aug-2017.pdf) |
| Special majority = “>50% of total members…” | Use exact exam wording: **majority of total membership** + **not less than ⅔ present & voting**. | Art. 368(2). [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf) |
| “Ratification by half of States.” | **Not less than one-half of States**, only for provisions specified in Art. 368(2) proviso. | Current constitutional text. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf) |
| “42nd: DPSP >> FR; Minerva Mills: FR >> DPSP.” | 42nd extended Art. 31C protection to **all DPSPs**; Minerva Mills invalidated that extension. **Harmony/balance of FR–DPSP** is Basic Structure; avoid blanket `FR > DPSP` shorthand. | Minerva Mills. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/4488.pdf) |
| “91st changed 1/3 merger to 2/3.” | 1/3 was a **split exception** under original anti-defection regime; 91st **deleted split protection**. 2/3 merger exception remains. | 91st Amendment / Tenth Schedule. Base formulation at PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… |
| “State Legislative Agency” | **State Legislative Assembly**. | Art. 164(1A). [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/c9fe9c9b6840524844316f74bb1c556c.pdf) |
| “44th Amendment removed Article 275A.” | **Article 257A** was omitted. | 44th Amendment, s.33. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/376b37bf0ae18f1d9276ce226d0e67a1.pdf) |
| `UPSC-CSE-2024-GS1-Q066` raw statements blank | Restore **Addition; Variation; Repeal**; correct option D. Keep canonical ID/status unchanged. | Ghatnachakra + Art. 368(1). PRE__POL__PYQ__GHATNACHAKRA__20… [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf) |

### VERIFY

- **None unresolved.**
- `UPSC-CSE-2024-GS1-Q066` remains a **canonical data-quality correction**, not a substantive factual uncertainty.

### OMIT

- Exhaustive list of every Constitutional Amendment.
- Full details of amendments primarily owned by Elections, Local Government, Rights, Languages, Federalism or Schedules nodes.
- Long judicial quotations / bench-level doctrinal nuance unnecessary for Prelims.
- Current office-holders, schemes and other dynamic material.
- Repetitive 42nd/44th facts already captured once in the landmark-amendment table.

## 8. Supplement Decision

`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

Current taxonomy + canonical PYQs + base notes + Laxmikanth + Ghatnachakra + official Constitution/Amendment Acts/Supreme Court material answer every identified demand.

Ghatna Chakra independently reproduces the 2018 count as **3–5–2–1** and the 2025 question with **Socialist + Secular** as the correct pair. PRE__POL__PYQ__GHATNACHAKRA__20…

### Demand synthesis
- **Recurring concepts:** exact Preamble vocabulary; objectives; part/enforceability distinction; original vs amended formulation.
- **Recurring traps:** current wording substituted for 1950 wording; `part of Constitution` ≠ `independently enforceable`; non-Preamble words inserted as distractors.
- **Qualifier patterns:** **NOT**, **exact**, **legal nature**, **in that order**, **inserted by 42nd Amendment**.
- **UPSC:** conceptual/legal distinctions + historical constitutional status.
- **UPPCS:** higher literal-recall load—word counts, amendment words, presence/absence; taxonomy additionally requires source-country mapping.
- **Recent reinforcement:** UPPCS 2025 again tested 42nd-Amendment insertion.

## 3. Base-Note Coverage
- **already strong — KEEP:** Objectives Resolution; “We the People”; 42nd Amendment; non-justiciability; Berubari–Kesavananda–LIC distinction; key Preamble terms. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **partial — COMPRESS:** long explanations of Sovereign/Socialist/Secular/Democratic/Republic → retain only PYQ-relevant qualifiers.
- **missing:** explicit **3–5–2–1** recall device; clean **26 Jan 1950 vs present** comparison; explicit `Federal`-absent trap.
- **redundant — OMIT:** long quotations/epithets; four devices of direct democracy; extended socialism/secularism case material; peripheral philosophical commentary.
- **outdated/suspect — CORRECT:** base source table attributes **SC advisory jurisdiction → Canada**; current reference edition instead maps it to **Government of India Act, 1935**. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20… Section 213 of the 1935 Act empowered consultation of the Federal Court, supporting the correction. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/repealedfileopen?rfilename=A1935.pdf\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Preamble — skeleton
- Immediate basis → **Objectives Resolution**; moved by **Jawaharlal Nehru**; adopted by Constituent Assembly.
- Constitutional authority → **“We, the People of India”** → people are ultimate source of authority.
- Four components:
  1. **Source of authority** → People of India
  2. **Nature of State** → Sovereign, Socialist, Secular, Democratic, Republic
  3. **Objectives** → Justice, Liberty, Equality, Fraternity
  4. **Adoption date** → **26 November 1949**  
  BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

### B. Exact content → **3–5–2–1**

| Element | Exact content |
|---|---|
| **Justice — 3** | Social; Economic; Political |
| **Liberty — 5** | Thought; Expression; Belief; Faith; Worship |
| **Equality — 2** | Status; Opportunity |
| **Fraternity — 1** | Assures dignity of individual + unity and integrity of Nation |

Official constitutional text confirms this wording. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19632/1/the_constitution_of_india.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### C. Original vs present constitutional status

| Point | Original / 26 Jan 1950 | Present |
|---|---|---|
| State-description | **Sovereign Democratic Republic** | **Sovereign Socialist Secular Democratic Republic** |
| Fraternity clause | unity of the Nation | unity **and integrity** of the Nation |

- **42nd Constitutional Amendment Act, 1976**:
  - inserted **Socialist**
  - inserted **Secular**
  - inserted **Integrity** into the fraternity clause
  - effective substitution from **3 January 1977**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19632/1/the_constitution_of_india.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Preamble amended **only once** so far. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…
- Chronology:
  - **15 Aug 1947 → 25 Jan 1950:** Dominion status.
  - **26 Jan 1950:** Constitution commenced; India became a **Sovereign Democratic Republic**. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

### D. Legal status

| Proposition | Position |
|---|---|
| Part of Constitution? | **YES** |
| Independently enforceable? | **NO** |
| Grants substantive rights by itself? | **NO** |
| Interpretive aid? | **YES** |
| Source of legislative power? | **NO** |
| Independent prohibition on legislative power? | **NO** |
| Amendable under Art. 368? | **YES**, subject to Basic Structure limitation |

The Supreme Court continues to treat the Preamble as an integral part and a source of foundational constitutional values, while holding that it is not by itself directly enforceable and does not independently grant substantive rights. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2012/35071/35071_2012_Judgement_24-Aug-2017.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### E. Case-law sequence

| Case | Preamble position |
|---|---|
| **Berubari Union (1960)** | Preamble = interpretive aid / key to framers’ mind; **not part** of Constitution — historical view |
| **Kesavananda Bharati (1973)** | Overruled that aspect of Berubari → **Preamble is part**; amendable, but basic features cannot be destroyed |
| **LIC of India (1995)** | Reaffirmed Preamble as **integral part** |

BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20… [Supreme Court of India](https://www.sci.gov.in/document/his-holiness-kesavananda-bharati-v-state-of-kerala-1973-supp-scr-1/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### F. High-value word traps
- **Federal** → **NOT** in Preamble.
- **Economic liberty** → **NOT** in Preamble.
- Economic **justice** → YES.
- `Socialist`, `Secular`, `Integrity` → **not in original 1949 Preamble**.
- `Sovereign`, `Democratic`, `Republic` → **original words**.
- Absence of `Socialist`/`Secular` from original wording ≠ absence of socialist/secular constitutional features before 1976.

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

### Sources of the Constitution

Base notes already contain a strong source table. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… Reference cross-check: Laxmikanth 8e source table. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20… BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

| Source | High-yield borrowings |
|---|---|
| **Government of India Act, 1935** | Federal scheme; Governor; judiciary; PSCs; emergency provisions; administrative details; **SC advisory-jurisdiction antecedent** |
| **British Constitution** | Parliamentary government; Rule of Law; legislative procedure; single citizenship; cabinet; prerogative writs; parliamentary privileges; bicameralism |
| **US Constitution** | Fundamental Rights; independent judiciary; judicial review; President impeachment; removal of SC/HC judges; Vice-President |
| **Irish Constitution** | DPSP; nomination to Rajya Sabha; method of election of President |
| **Canadian Constitution** | Federation with strong Centre; residuary powers with Centre; appointment of Governors by Centre |
| **Australian Constitution** | Concurrent List; freedom of trade/commerce/intercourse; joint sitting |
| **Weimar Germany** | Emergency provision relating to suspension of Fundamental Rights |
| **USSR/Soviet Constitution** | Fundamental Duties; **social, economic, political justice** ideal in Preamble |
| **French Constitution** | **Republic; Liberty, Equality, Fraternity** |
| **South Africa** | Constitutional-amendment procedure; election of Rajya Sabha members |
| **Japan** | Procedure established by law |

**Source trap:** Indian Preamble’s **immediate basis = Objectives Resolution**; foreign-source mapping of individual ideals/features ≠ replacement for this fact.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- Preamble **part of Constitution** ≠ independently enforceable.
- Non-justiciable ≠ legally irrelevant; it remains an **interpretive aid**.
- Interpretive aid ≠ source of legislative power.
- Preamble cannot override an express constitutional provision.
- Amendable ≠ Basic Structure destroyable.
- **26 Nov 1949 adoption** ≠ **26 Jan 1950 commencement**.
- **26 Jan 1950:** Sovereign Democratic Republic ≠ present five-word formulation.
- **Socialist + Secular** added to State-description; **Integrity** added in fraternity clause.
- Sovereign / Democratic / Republic = original, not 42nd-Amendment additions.
- Justice–Liberty–Equality–Fraternity = **3–5–2–1**.
- Liberty → thought, expression, belief, faith, worship; **not economic liberty**.
- `Federal` ≠ Preamble word.
- Objectives Resolution ≠ foreign constitutional-source table.
- **SC advisory-jurisdiction antecedent → GOI Act 1935**, not Canada in this frozen pack.

- **Recurring concepts:** Part II/Arts. 5–11; single citizenship; acquisition/loss; Parliament’s power; Arts. 2–4; chronology of State formation.

- **Qualifier patterns:** “only”; “not correctly matched”; “under Article 3”; “earliest to latest”; “came into existence on basis of…”.
- **UPSC vs UPPCS:** UPSC → conceptual/legal-basis pair/statement traps; UPPCS → direct provision recall + chronology.
- **Recent reinforcement:** State formation/legal basis in UPSC 2025; State-power matching in UPPCS 2024; chronology in UPPCS 2025.

## 3. Base-Note Coverage

- **already strong — KEEP:**
  - single citizenship;
  - Arts. 1–4 structure and Article 3 procedure;
  - Arts. 5–11 mapping;
  - five modes of acquisition + three modes of loss;
  - Dhar → JVP → Fazl Ali → 1956 reorganisation framework;
  - post-1956 State chronology. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **partial — COMPRESS:**
  - detailed citizenship-at-commencement conditions;
  - OCI/NRI/PIO material → retain only durable status distinction if needed;
  - long State-reorganisation narrative → convert to chronology + legal-basis traps.
- **missing:**
  - explicit Article 2 vs Article 3 elimination rule;
  - corrected post-2004 citizenship-by-birth test;
  - complete descent cut-offs;
  - UPSC-2025 Nagaland legal-basis correction.
- **redundant:** citizen-only FR table; extensive OCI privileges; detailed NPR/NRIC discussion.
- **outdated/suspect — CORRECT:** “Arts. 5–11 in Part III”; Sikkim full State in 1974; post-2004 birth rule; incomplete descent rule. Base citizenship section is otherwise substantial. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Constitutional map

| Anchor | Payload |
|---|---|
| **Part I — Arts. 1–4** | Union & its Territory |
| **Art. 1** | India, that is Bharat = **Union of States**; territory = States + UTs + acquired territories |
| **Art. 2** | Parliament → **admit into Union / establish new States** |
| **Art. 3** | Parliament → form State; increase/diminish area; alter boundary/name |
| **Art. 4** | Arts. 2/3 laws may amend **First + Fourth Schedules** + incidental/consequential provisions; **not deemed Art. 368 constitutional amendments** |
| **Part II — Arts. 5–11** | Citizenship |

Constitutional text directly separates admission/establishment under Art. 2 from formation/territorial/name changes under Art. 3. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

### B. Article 3 procedure

- Bill → **either House of Parliament**.
- **Prior recommendation of President** mandatory.
- If State’s **area/boundary/name** affected → President refers Bill to concerned State Legislature for **views** within specified/extended period.
- State Legislature’s **consent not required**; its view is not a veto.
- In Art. 3 clauses, “State” includes UT; in the **proviso requiring reference**, “State” excludes UT.
- Parliament can form a State/UT by combining parts of a State/UT with another State/UT. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Art. 2/3 law → ordinary legislative process/simple majority; outside Art. 368.
- **Cession of Indian territory to a foreign State ≠ ordinary Art. 3 reorganisation** → constitutional-amendment route.

### C. Citizenship — constitutional provisions

| Article | Durable recall |
|---|---|
| **5** | Citizenship at commencement: domicile in India + birth / parent born / 5-year ordinary residence condition |
| **6** | Migrants **from Pakistan to India** |
| **7** | Persons who migrated **to Pakistan** after 1 Mar 1947; return-permit exception |
| **8** | Persons of Indian origin residing **outside India** |
| **9** | Voluntary acquisition of foreign citizenship → cannot claim citizenship under Arts. 5, 6 or 8 |
| **10** | Continuance of citizenship, subject to parliamentary law |
| **11** | **Parliament** may legislate on acquisition, termination and all other citizenship matters |

Arts. 8–11 and Parliament’s statutory power are explicit in Part II. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### D. Single citizenship

- India → **single citizenship of India**; no separate State citizenship.
- Federal/dual polity **does not imply dual citizenship**.
- UPSC-2021 formulation to retain: **one citizenship + one domicile**.
- Naturalised citizen ≠ constitutionally barred from Presidency merely because naturalised: Art. 58 requires **“a citizen of India”**, not “citizen by birth”. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/06/a714fb6b673825b7170f2f0c979a913c.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### E. Citizenship Act, 1955

**Acquisition — 5 routes**

1. **Birth** — s.3  
2. **Descent** — s.4  
3. **Registration** — s.5  
4. **Naturalisation** — s.6  
5. **Incorporation of territory** — s.7 [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/handle/123456789/19760?sam_handle=123456789%2F2505\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Citizenship by birth — high-value cut-offs**

| Born in India | Rule |
|---|---|
| 26 Jan 1950 – 30 Jun 1987 | citizen by birth, subject to statutory exceptions |
| 1 Jul 1987 – 2 Dec 2004 | **either parent** citizen at birth |
| From 3 Dec 2004 | **both parents citizens OR one citizen + other not an illegal migrant** |

Foreign diplomat/immunity and enemy-alien exceptions apply under s.3(2). [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/6793/1/the_citizenship_act_1955.pdf)

**Citizenship by descent — elimination cut-offs**

- 26 Jan 1950–9 Dec 1992 → **father** citizen at birth.
- From 10 Dec 1992 → **either parent** citizen at birth.
- From 3 Dec 2004 → consular-registration requirement applies, subject to statutory conditions. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/6793/1/the_citizenship_act_1955.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Loss — 3 routes**

| Mode | Core distinction |
|---|---|
| **Renunciation — s.8** | voluntary declaration |
| **Termination — s.9** | voluntary acquisition of citizenship of another country |
| **Deprivation — s.10** | compulsory Central Government action against statutorily specified categories/grounds |

Therefore: **“once citizenship is granted it can never be deprived” = false.** [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/6793/1/the_citizenship_act_1955.pdf)

### F. State reorganisation — foundation chronology

| Year | Event |
|---|---|
| **1948** | **Dhar Commission** → linguistic reorganisation examined; preferred administrative convenience |
| **1948–49** | **JVP Committee** = Jawaharlal Nehru + Vallabhbhai Patel + Pattabhi Sitaramayya → rejected language as sole basis |
| **1953** | **Andhra State** created from Madras → first linguistic State **after Independence** |
| **1953** | **Fazl Ali / States Reorganisation Commission** appointed; K.M. Panikkar + H.N. Kunzru |
| **1955** | SRC report → broadly accepted language; rejected “one language–one State” |
| **1956** | **States Reorganisation Act + 7th CA** → major restructuring; **14 States + 6 UTs** |

Reference treatment confirms Dhar/JVP and Andhra State sequence. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

### G. PYQ-critical State chronology

| State/event | Durable date/legal hook |
|---|---|
| **Nagaland** | **1963**; formed under **State of Nagaland Act, 1962** |
| **Haryana / reorganised Punjab** | **1966** |
| **Himachal Pradesh** | **1971** |
| **Manipur, Tripura, Meghalaya** | **1972** |
| **Sikkim — associate status** | **1974/35th CA framework** |
| **Sikkim — full State** | **1975/36th CA** |
| **Mizoram, Arunachal Pradesh, Goa** | **1987** |
| **Chhattisgarh, Uttarakhand, Jharkhand** | **2000** |
| **Telangana** | **2014** |

Nagaland was formed by a separate parliamentary statute; the 13th Amendment inserted the special constitutional framework for Nagaland, and its own Statement of Objects explicitly referred to a **separate Article 3 formation Bill**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1384/1/196227.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Two PYQ sequences to freeze**

- `Haryana 1966 → Goa 1987 → Jharkhand 2000 → Telangana 2014`
- `Nagaland 1963 → Punjab 1966 → Sikkim 1975 → Arunachal Pradesh 1987`

UPPCS-oriented sources repeatedly test formation-year sequencing. PRE__POL__PYQ__GHATNACHAKRA__20…

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

- **Part II = Arts. 5–11** → direct recall.
- **Art. 2** = admission/establishment; **Art. 3** = internal formation/reorganisation.
- Authority to create or rename a State = **Parliament**, not State Legislature.
- Chronology priority:
  - Nagaland 1963
  - Haryana/Punjab reorganisation 1966
  - Himachal 1971
  - Manipur/Tripura/Meghalaya 1972
  - Sikkim 1975
  - Mizoram/Arunachal/Goa 1987
  - Chhattisgarh/Uttarakhand/Jharkhand 2000
  - Telangana 2014.
- Keep **Andhra State 1953 ≠ Andhra Pradesh 1956** explicit.
- Ghatnachakra confirms UPPCS emphasis on single citizenship/acquisition-loss distinctions. PRE__POL__PYQ__GHATNACHAKRA__20…

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist

- **Part I = Union & territory ≠ Part II = Citizenship**
- **Citizenship = Arts. 5–11, not Part III**
- **Single citizenship ≠ dual citizenship because India is federal**
- **Citizen by birth ≠ only citizen eligible for President**
- **Citizenship granted ≠ incapable of deprivation**
- **Art. 2 admission/establishment ≠ Art. 3 internal reorganisation**
- **State Legislature gives views ≠ State consent required**
- **Alter State name → Parliament ≠ State Legislature**
- **Art. 2/3 law ≠ Art. 368 constitutional amendment**
- **Nagaland formation statute ≠ 13th CA itself**
- **Andhra State 1953 ≠ Andhra Pradesh 1956**
- **Sikkim associate-status phase ≠ full Statehood**
- **Sikkim full State = 1975/36th CA ≠ 1974**
- **Post-2004 citizenship by birth = both citizen parents OR one citizen + other not illegal migrant**
- **Renunciation ≠ termination ≠ deprivation**
- **PIO = legacy category; merged OCI scheme ≠ separate current citizenship category**

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

|---|---|---|
| Base: “Arts. 5–11 in **Part III**” | **Part II** | Constitution Part II; taxonomy/PYQs |
| Base: post-2004 birth requires **both parents citizens only** | Both citizens **OR** one citizen + other not illegal migrant | Citizenship Act s.3 [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/6793/1/the_citizenship_act_1955.pdf) |
| Base: descent after 2004 only vaguely stated | Preserve 1992 parent-rule + post-2004 consular-registration condition | Citizenship Act s.4 [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/6793/1/the_citizenship_act_1955.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| Base: Sikkim “1974 — 36th CA — full State” | Associate-status phase via **35th CA**; full State **1975 via 36th CA** | Constitution records 36th CA omission of Art. 2A from 1975 [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) |
| Reference chronology sometimes labels “Andhra Pradesh — 1953” | **Andhra State 1953; Andhra Pradesh 1956** | States-reorganisation history |
| `UPSC-CSE-2025-GS1-Q052` stored answer = all three pairs | **Nagaland pair is legally false as worded**: State formation was under State of Nagaland Act, 1962; 13th CA dealt with special provisions/Art. 371A. Treat canonical answer record as requiring correction. | India Code + 13th Amendment [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1384/1/196227.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| `UPPCS-2025-GS1-Q033` stored answer/option mapping | Factual order = **Nagaland → Punjab → Sikkim → Arunachal Pradesh = 3-1-4-2** | Formation statutes/reference chronology |
| `UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q096` | Stem is truncated/malformed in gateway; recoverable demand = **admission of new States belongs Art. 2, not Art. 3** | Constitution Arts. 2–3 [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) |
