## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Fundamental Rights & writs

**Framework**
- **Part III = Articles 12–35**; justiciable; qualified, not absolute.
- **Art. 12:** “State” for Part III.
- **Art. 13:** law inconsistent with FR void to extent of inconsistency; “law” includes **Ordinance, order, bye-law, rule, regulation, notification, custom/usage having force of law**; constitutional amendment under Art. 368 excluded by Art. 13(4). [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- **Art. 14:** equality before law + equal protection → **any person**; arbitrary/unguided State discretion can offend Art. 14.
- **Arts. 15, 16, 19:** citizen-specific rights.
- **Art. 17:** untouchability abolished → **Right to Equality**, not Right against Exploitation.
- **Art. 18:** titles prohibited except military/academic distinctions; Bharat Ratna/Padma awards ≠ prohibited titles when not used as prefix/suffix. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **Art. 19:** six freedoms → speech/expression; peaceful assembly without arms; association/union/co-operative society; movement; residence; profession/occupation/trade/business. **Property is no longer Art. 19.**
- **Art. 20:** no ex-post-facto criminal punishment; no double jeopardy; no compelled self-incrimination.
- **Art. 21:** life/personal liberty → durable judicial inclusions: dignity, privacy, choice of spouse, healthy environment, livelihood, speedy/fair trial, legal aid.
- **Art. 21A:** free + compulsory education, **6–14 years**; 86th Amendment.
- **Art. 22:** ordinary arrest → grounds of arrest + lawyer + magistrate within 24 h; these ordinary safeguards do **not** apply identically to enemy aliens/preventive detention.
- **Arts. 23–24:** trafficking/begar/forced labour; child <14 in factory/mine/hazardous employment.
- **Arts. 25–28:** freedom of religion.
- **Arts. 29–30:** cultural/educational rights; Art. 30 protects religious/linguistic minorities; Constitution does not define “minority”.
- **Art. 32:** direct SC remedy for FR; five writs; Parliament may empower another court without derogating from SC powers.
- **Art. 300A:** property = constitutional right outside Part III; available to **any person**; deprivation only by authority of law; no Art. 32 claim merely because Art. 300A is infringed.

**Writ matrix**

| Writ | Core use | Trap |
|---|---|---|
| **Habeas Corpus** | test legality of detention | can reach public authority **or private custodian**; strict locus standi relaxed |
| **Mandamus** | compel performance of public/legal duty | not for purely private duty; **private organisation entrusted with public duty can fall within writ reach** |
| **Prohibition** | superior court stops inferior court/tribunal from continuing beyond jurisdiction | **preventive**; 2024 PYQ: lower court, not government officer/legislature |
| **Certiorari** | quash/bring up decision/proceeding for jurisdictional/legal error | generally corrective; ≠ Prohibition |
| **Quo Warranto** | challenge authority to hold substantive public office | petitioner need not show personal injury |

- **Art. 32 vs 226:** SC → enforcement of FR; HC → FR **and “any other purpose”**. Art. 226(2): cause of action wholly/partly arising within HC territory can confer jurisdiction even if authority/person is located outside it. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- **Emergency trap:** Arts. 20 & 21 cannot be included in an Art. 359 suspension of court-enforcement; Art. 19 has separate Art. 358 treatment.
- **Ordinance:** has force of law but cannot abridge FR; may operate retrospectively subject to constitutional limits.

### B. Directive Principles of State Policy

- **Part IV = Arts. 36–51.**
- **Art. 37:** non-justiciable **but fundamental in governance**; State has duty to apply them while making laws. [Education Government of India](https://www.education.gov.in/directive_principles_of_state_policy_article-37)
- Welfare-State demand → **DPSP**, not FR/FD.

| Article | High-yield payload |
|---|---|
| **38** | social order/welfare; minimise inequalities |
| **39(a)** | adequate means of livelihood |
| **39(b)** | material resources → common good |
| **39(c)** | prevent concentration of wealth/means of production |
| **39(d)** | equal pay for equal work |
| **39(e),(f)** | protection of workers/children |
| **39A** | equal justice + free legal aid |
| **40** | village panchayats |
| **41** | work, education, public assistance — within economic capacity/development |
| **42** | just/humane work conditions + maternity relief |
| **43** | living wage; cottage industries |
| **43A** | workers’ participation in management |
| **43B** | co-operative societies |
| **44** | Uniform Civil Code |
| **45** | early childhood care/education **below 6** |
| **46** | educational/economic interests of SC/ST/weaker sections; protection from exploitation |
| **47** | nutrition, standard of living, public health |
| **48** | agriculture/animal husbandry |
| **48A** | environment, forests, wildlife |
| **49** | monuments/objects of national importance |
| **50** | separation of judiciary from executive in State public services |
| **51** | international peace/security; international law/treaties; arbitration |

Article 43B is the co-operative-society DPSP; Article 48A is environment. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/c9fe9c9b6840524844316f74bb1c556c.pdf)

**Amendment hooks**
- **42nd:** Art. 39(f) revised; **39A + 43A + 48A** inserted.
- **44th:** Art. 38(2) inequality provision.
- **86th:** Art. 21A + substituted Art. 45 + FD 51A(k).
- **97th:** **Art. 43B**, not 48B.

**Classification**
- Socialist / Gandhian / Liberal-intellectual = **study classification**, not classification written into Constitution.

### C. Fundamental Duties

- **Part IVA; Art. 51A.**
- **42nd Amendment:** originally 10 duties; Swaran Singh Committee background.
- **86th Amendment:** 11th duty → parent/guardian provide education opportunity to child/ward aged **6–14**.
- Duties attach to **citizens**; non-justiciable directly; legislation may give legal effect to related conduct. Current constitutional text contains 11 duties. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

**51A quick map**
- **(a)** Constitution/ideals/institutions + Flag + Anthem
- **(b)** freedom-struggle ideals
- **(c)** sovereignty, unity, integrity
- **(d)** defend country/national service
- **(e)** harmony/common brotherhood; dignity of women
- **(f)** composite cultural heritage
- **(g)** environment + compassion for living creatures
- **(h)** scientific temper/humanism/inquiry/reform
- **(i)** public property + abjure violence
- **(j)** excellence
- **(k)** education opportunity to child/ward 6–14

**Not enumerated FDs**
- vote in public elections
- payment of taxes
- protection of monuments/places of national importance → **Art. 49 DPSP**
- generic protection of weaker sections from social injustice → not an FD

**Overlap trap**
- Environment → **Art. 48A DPSP + Art. 51A(g) FD**.

### D. Fifth / Sixth Schedule

| | Fifth Schedule | Sixth Schedule |
|---|---|---|
| Anchor | Art. **244(1)** | Art. **244(2)** |
| Area | Scheduled Areas/STs outside Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram | tribal areas in **Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram (ATM2)** |
| State executive | **continues to extend** to Scheduled Areas | autonomous district/regional council architecture |
| Key institution | Tribes Advisory Council | Autonomous District/Regional Councils |
| Key executive actor | Governor + President | Governor + councils |
| PESA | **applies to Fifth-Schedule Scheduled Areas** | not the PESA field |

**Fifth Schedule**
- Scheduled Area → declared/altered by **President**, not Governor.
- Governor → report on administration to **President** annually/when required.
- Union may give directions to State regarding administration.
- **TAC:** **not more than 20 members**; as nearly as possible ¾ are representatives of STs in State Legislative Assembly.
- Governor may regulate peace/good government; regulate/restrict tribal-land transfers, land allotment, money-lending.
- Governor may direct Parliamentary/State law not to apply or apply with exceptions/modifications; regulations modifying law require Presidential assent.
- Fifth Schedule **does not** → create UT / remove State executive power / automatically create a local self-government unit. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Core PYQ consequence → special protection against alienation/transfer of tribal land.

**Sixth Schedule**
- autonomous districts/regions; Governor has organisation/reorganisation role.
- District/Regional Councils exercise specified legislative, judicial, administrative and fiscal functions.
- Do not equate Sixth-Schedule councils with Fifth-Schedule TAC/PESA. Base-note Sixth-Schedule coverage is already substantial. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…

### E. PESA, ST recognition, PVTGs, FRA

**PESA Act, 1996**
- extends **Part IX Panchayat provisions to Scheduled Areas** with exceptions/modifications.
- Gram Sabha/Panchayat consultation before land acquisition + resettlement/rehabilitation.
- prior recommendation mandatory for minor-mineral prospecting/mining leases and concessions by auction.
- specific empowerment includes:
  - minor forest produce ownership
  - prevention/restoration of alienated tribal land
  - village markets
  - money-lending control
  - intoxicants
  - minor water bodies
  - social-sector institutions
  - local plans/resources including tribal sub-plans. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)

**Scheduled Tribe recognition — Art. 342**
- **President** specifies STs by public notification.
- for a State → consultation with Governor.
- **Parliament by law** alone can include/exclude communities thereafter.
- recognition is **State/UT-specific**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**PVTGs**
- administrative high-vulnerability category within tribal communities; not a separate constitutional list.
- **75 PVTG communities in 18 States + 1 UT**.
- durable identification features: pre-agricultural technological level, stagnant/declining population, very low literacy, subsistence/economic backwardness; official material also stresses forest dependence. [Tribal Affairs India](https://tribal.nic.in/PM-JANMAN.aspx)
- `95 PVTGs` = PYQ trap.

**Forest Rights Act, 2006**
- full title: Scheduled Tribes and Other Traditional Forest Dwellers (Recognition of Forest Rights) Act, 2006.
- **Gram Sabha initiates** determination of individual/community forest-right claims. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/13241/1/st_%26_otfd_rfact_2006.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- bamboo = **minor forest produce**; FRA recognises ownership/access/collection/use/disposal rights over MFP. [India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_CEN_42_71_00001_200702_1517807323238\&filename=a2007-02.pdf\&type=actfile)
- Ministry handling **Tribal Affairs** = national nodal agency. [Tribal Affairs India](https://tribal.nic.in/downloads/acts-and-rules/FRARulesBook.pdf)
- “Critical Wildlife Habitat” is defined within FRA.
- habitat rights of PVTGs/pre-agricultural communities ≠ declaration by MoEFCC alone.

### F. Statutory Law & Rights Legislation

**RTE**
- Art. 21A + **Right of Children to Free and Compulsory Education Act, 2009** → 6–14 years.
- Sec. 23: minimum teacher qualifications set by academic authority authorised by Central Government.
- NCTE framework includes TET as minimum eligibility condition for relevant Classes I–VIII appointments.
- **Passing TET ≠ right to appointment.** [India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_PB_82_937_00001_00001_1551763725450\&filename=the_right_of_children_to_free_and_compulsory_education_act%2C_2009.pdf\&type=actfile)

**Disability rights**
- current central baseline = **Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016**, not “Act passed in 2018”.
- benchmark disability → **≥40%** specified disability subject to statutory certification framework.
- benchmark-disability provisions: free education 6–18; ≥5% seats in government/government-aided higher education; ≥4% vacancies in government establishments. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/handle/123456789/2155?locale=en)
- 2011 UPSC disability PYQ belongs to predecessor-law context → retain as historical demand only.

**Consumer protection**
- current statutory anchor = **Consumer Protection Act, 2019**; CCPA established under it; NCDRC = statutory/quasi-judicial, not constitutional. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/16939/1/a2019-35.pdf)
- avoid freezing changing pecuniary-jurisdiction thresholds.
- **MRP = Maximum Retail Price**, inclusive of taxes; retailer cannot charge above MRP → MRP is a ceiling, not a compulsory exact sale price. [Consumer Affairs](https://consumeraffairs.nic.in/sites/default/files/file-uploads/consumer_information/Consumer%20Handbook.pdf)
- UPSC 2012 consumer question = historical predecessor-law procedure; do not universalise its fee rules to current law.

**Cyber / IT**
- CERT-In → statutory role under **Sec. 70B, IT Act 2000**.
- directions/reporting framework reaches specified service providers, intermediaries, data centres, bodies corporate, etc.
- retain entity-scope concept; omit volatile reporting timelines from frozen note. [CERT-IN](https://cert-in.org.in/Directions70B.jsp)

**Custody / parole / prisons**
- **Police custody ≠ judicial custody.**
- judicial custody → accused under judicial/Magistrate authority, ordinarily lodged in jail; police interrogation during it requires judicial permission.
- parole = conditional temporary release; **not an absolute right**; largely governed by State prison/parole rules.
- prisons/persons detained therein = **State List**; administration/management primarily States/UTs.
- Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act, 2023 = **model for State/UT adoption**, not automatic nationwide substitution. [Ministry of Home Affairs](https://www.mha.gov.in/en/commoncontent/prison-reforms-pr-section)

**Preventive detention**
- distinguish from punitive detention.
- ordinary Art. 22 lawyer/24-hour safeguards ≠ wholesale application to preventive detention.
- keep constitutional safeguards; omit transient criminal-code section-number conversion.

**Flag Code**
- **Flag Code of India, 2002**.
- rectangular; length : height = **3 : 2**.
- `600 mm × 400 mm` ≠ one of the Code's listed standard sizes in the tested 2023 formulation. [Ministry of Home Affairs](https://www.mha.gov.in/sites/default/files/flagcodeofindia_070214.pdf)

**Historical POTA trap**
- POTA 2002 = repealed historical legislation.
- keep only for `UPSC-CSE-2023-GS1-Q077`: its special evidentiary regime made the blanket proposition “police confession could not be evidence” incorrect.
- do not carry POTA as present law.

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

- **Part map:** III → FR (12–35); IV → DPSP (36–51); IVA → FD (51A); X → Scheduled/Tribal Areas (244–244A); XII → property right Art. 300A.
- **Article recall:** 39A legal aid; 40 Panchayats; 43A workers’ management; **43B co-operatives**; 44 UCC; 46 weaker sections/SC/ST; 48A environment; 50 judiciary–executive separation; 51 international peace.
- **Writ literals:** Habeas Corpus = have the body; Mandamus = we command; Prohibition = to forbid; Certiorari = to be certified/informed; Quo Warranto = by what authority.
- **Amendment recall:** 42nd → FDs/39A/43A/48A; 44th → property removed from FR + Art. 38(2); 86th → 21A/45/51A(k); 97th → 43B.
- **Direct recall:** PESA = 1996; Sixth Schedule = ATM2; MRP = maximum, not compulsory exact selling price.
- **Canonical one-off:** “Fundamental Rights are a pledge to our people and a pact with the civilized world” → **Dr. S. Radhakrishnan** (`UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q016`).
- DPSP source → **Irish Constitution**; Socialist/Gandhian/Liberal-intellectual classification is analytical, not constitutional text.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist

- Art. 14 **person** ≠ Arts. 15/16/19 **citizen**
- Art. 17 untouchability → Equality ≠ Right against Exploitation
- Right against Exploitation = Arts. 23–24
- property → Art. 300A ≠ FR / Art. 19
- privacy + spouse choice → Art. 21
- Art. 20 = ex-post-facto + double jeopardy + self-incrimination ≠ generic torture protection
- preventive detention ≠ ordinary arrest safeguards
- Art. 32 FR-only remedy ≠ Art. 226 FR + other legal purposes
- HC writ jurisdiction ≠ authority must physically sit inside State
- Mandamus against **pure private duty** no ≠ private body performing **public duty** never
- Prohibition = stop proceeding ≠ Certiorari = quash/correct
- DPSP non-justiciable ≠ constitutionally irrelevant
- Welfare State → DPSP
- 43A workers ≠ **43B co-operatives**
- 48A environment ↔ 51A(g) environment
- Art. 49 monuments ≠ Fundamental Duty
- voting ≠ enumerated FD
- payment of taxes ≠ enumerated FD
- Fifth Schedule ≠ Sixth Schedule
- ATM2 → Sixth Schedule
- President declares Scheduled Areas ≠ Governor
- Governor reports Scheduled Areas administration → President ≠ CM → Home Ministry
- State executive continues in Fifth-Schedule area ≠ replaced by local government
- TAC **≤20** ≠ exactly 20
- ST specification → President after Governor consultation; alteration → Parliament
- PESA → Fifth-Schedule Scheduled Areas ≠ Sixth-Schedule councils
- PESA recommendation on minor minerals can be **mandatory** ≠ mere advice
- PVTG = **75** ≠ 95
- FRA Gram Sabha initiates rights process ≠ Forest Department/Collector
- FRA nodal ministry → Tribal Affairs ≠ Environment Ministry
- bamboo under FRA = MFP; 2017 Forest Act amendment concerned bamboo in **non-forest** areas
- RPwD **2016** ≠ 2018
- RTE teacher eligibility authority/NCTE framework ≠ State Council claim
- TET eligibility ≠ guaranteed appointment
- parole ≠ absolute entitlement
- prisons = State List
- MRP = maximum ceiling ≠ seller must charge exact MRP
- Flag ratio = 3:2; `600×400 mm` ≠ tested standard-size entry
- NCDRC statutory ≠ constitutional
- ordinance has force of law ≠ can abridge FR

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT

- **Base:** `97th Amendment → Article 48B (Co-operative Societies)`  
  → **Correct:** `Article 43B` → 97th Amendment. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/c9fe9c9b6840524844316f74bb1c556c.pdf)

- **Base:** HC writ reach stated as confined to bodies within State territory  
  → **Correct:** Art. 226(2) permits jurisdiction where cause of action wholly/partly arises within HC territory even when authority/person is outside it. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

- **Base:** Mandamus “cannot be issued against a private individual/body” as an absolute rule  
  → **Correct:** cannot enforce a purely private duty; a private organisation entrusted with a **public duty** can fall within writ jurisdiction → directly tested by `UPSC-CSE-2022-GS1-Q018`.

- **Base:** Fifth-Schedule TAC = “20-member body”  
  → **Correct:** **not more than 20**; approximately ¾ representation rule as specified in Fifth Schedule. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

- **Base notation:** “Article 39B / 39C”  
  → **Correct:** **Article 39(b) / Article 39(c)**.

- **Canonical gateway:** `UPSC-CSE-2019-GS1-Q055` is **FROZEN with answer D**, but statutory evidence supports **B = statements 2 & 3 only**. The 2017 Forest amendment removed bamboo from “tree” to facilitate felling/transit of bamboo grown in **non-forest areas**; it did not create the claimed forest-area right. FRA separately defines bamboo as MFP and recognises MFP rights. **Use corrected factual answer; canonical answer record requires upstream repair.** [India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_CEN_16_0_00013_192716_1523350029217\&filename=gazette_notification_indian_forest_%28amendment%29_act%2C_2017.pdf\&type=notification\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)

- **Historical statutory layer:** old disability-law detail  
  → current baseline = **RPwD Act, 2016**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/handle/123456789/2155?locale=en)

- **Historical statutory layer:** Consumer Protection Act, 1986 material  
  → current baseline = **Consumer Protection Act, 2019**; preserve 2012 question only as historical PYQ context. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/16939/1/a2019-35.pdf)

### VERIFY

- **No unresolved material factual claim remains.**
- **Canonical-record QA — non-blocking:**
  - `UPPCS-2019-GS1-Q026` → `EDITABLE_REVIEW / OPTION_BOUNDARY_UNCLEAR`
  - `UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q046` → `EDITABLE_REVIEW / TABLE_STRUCTURE_UNCLEAR`
  - `UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q138` → `EDITABLE_REVIEW / TABLE_STRUCTURE_UNCLEAR`
  - `UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q110` → runtime FROZEN, but parsed stem/options visibly boundary-corrupted
  - `UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q122` → runtime FROZEN, but parsed matching table visibly boundary-corrupted
- Use these records for **demand evidence only** unless the gateway is repaired; underlying constitutional facts have been independently resolved.

### OMIT

- dynamic count of States that have notified PESA Rules.
- current office-holders/body chairpersons.
- exhaustive Article 21 judgment chronology; retain durable rights only.
- current political disputes around privacy/religion/reservation.
- new criminal-law section-number substitutions and implementation trivia.

# Polity & Governance → Rights, Duties & Protective Frameworks → Fundamental Rights & writs — Source Pack v01

**Status:** PASS_WITH_CORRECTIONS  
**taxonomy_id:** `prelims-polity-and-governance-rights-duties-and-protective-frameworks-fundamental-rights-and-writs`

## 1. Scope
- **Includes:** Part III Articles 12–35; FR categories/article mapping; equality/reservation; Articles 19–22; judicial expansion of Article 21; preventive detention; exploitation/religious/cultural-minority rights; Article 32 + writs; Articles 31A–31C/33–35; Right to Property under Article 300A; UDHR linkage.
- **Excludes:** current-event treatment of judgments; scheme/office-holder trivia; detailed criminal/telecom statutory law owned by sibling node **Statutory Law & Rights Legislation**; general basic-structure history except where needed to qualify FR amendment/judicial review.
- **UPSC depth:** Article 21 expansion—especially privacy; Article 14/non-arbitrariness; qualifier-heavy writ questions; Article 13 meaning of “law”; constitutional-vs-legal-right distinctions.
- **UPPCS depth:** direct Article/amendment recall; writ meanings/powers; citizen-vs-person availability; occasional quotation/person attribution.
- **Recurring evidence clusters:** Article 21 expansion; FR classification; UDHR; writs; with closely related demand on Article 14, minority rights, preventive detention, reservation and property. PRE-POL__TAXONOMY_GATEWAY

### Demand synthesis
- **Recurring concepts:** Article 21 expansion; article↔right mapping; writ applicability; Article 14 equality/non-arbitrariness; citizen vs person; property after 44th Amendment.
- **Recurring traps:** Art 17 ≠ Right against Exploitation; property ≠ Art 19; Art 29(1) ≠ minority-only; prohibition ≠ certiorari; Art 32 ≠ Art 226; preventive detention ≠ ordinary arrest safeguards.
- **Qualifier patterns:** `only`, `not`, `any person`, `citizen`, `public duty`, `lower court`, `how many`, assertion–reason, exact article/amendment.
- **UPSC vs UPPCS:** UPSC increasingly tests doctrine/application and statement elimination; UPPCS more often tests direct article/amendment/writ recall.

## 3. Base-Note Coverage
- **already strong — KEEP:** Part III nature/features; Articles 12–18; Article 19 freedoms; Articles 20–30; Article 32; writ framework; property shift to Article 300A. Base notes correctly foreground justiciability, qualified nature, citizen/person distinction and emergency exceptions. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **partial — COMPRESS/CORRECT:** long Article 21 case list; privacy doctrine; preventive detention; writ edge cases; Article 31A–31C; reservation provisions.
- **missing / under-emphasised:** recent Article 13 PYQ pattern; mandamus against private bodies performing public duty; unnotified 44th-Amendment preventive-detention trap; clean Art 32 vs 226 comparison; UDHR elimination cues.
- **redundant — OMIT:** long statutory/case minutiae, detailed criminal-procedure provisions, expansive award-history material, peripheral case names with no identified PYQ demand.
- **outdated/suspect:** “Mandamus cannot issue against private bodies” as an absolute rule; Puttaswamy conflated with Aadhaar merits case; Unni Krishnan education wording; overly broad claim that Article 300A protects only against executive action.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Part III architecture

| Anchor | Frozen fact |
|---|---|
| **Articles 12–35** | Fundamental Rights; presently **6 categories**. Originally 7; Right to Property removed from Part III by 44th Amendment. |
| **Art 12** | “State” → Union Government + Parliament; State Governments + Legislatures; local authorities; other authorities within India / under GoI control. |
| **Art 13** | Pre-Constitution inconsistent law → void **to extent of inconsistency**; State cannot make law taking away/abridging FR. “Law” includes **ordinance, order, bye-law, rule, regulation, notification, custom/usage having force of law**. Art 13(4): constitutional amendment under Art 368 excluded from Art 13; amendment nevertheless subject to **basic structure** review. |
| **Nature** | Justiciable; generally qualified, not absolute; constitutional amendment possible **subject to basic structure**. Some FR operate against private persons also. |

Official text confirms Articles 12–15 and Article 13’s deliberately broad definition of “law”. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

**Availability traps**
- Art **14** → **any person**.
- Arts **15, 16, 19** → **citizens**.
- Arts **20, 21** → **persons**, not citizen-only.
- Art **25** → all persons.
- Art **29(1)** → “any section of **citizens**” with distinct language/script/culture.
- Art **30** → religious/linguistic minorities.
- Strong horizontal-operation set: **15(2), 17, 23, 24**.

### B. Right to Equality — Articles 14–18

| Article | Payload / elimination value |
|---|---|
| **14** | Equality before law + equal protection of laws. Equality before law = negative/British; equal protection = positive/US. **Class legislation prohibited; reasonable classification allowed** → intelligible differentia + rational nexus. Arbitrariness/unguided discretion can violate Art 14. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2022/6747/6747_2022_1_1501_48671_Judgement_30-Nov-2023.pdf) |
| **15** | No State discrimination against citizens **only** on religion, race, caste, sex, place of birth. **15(2)** also horizontally protects access to specified public-use places. Exceptions/enabling provisions: **15(3)** women/children; **15(4)** SEBC/SC/ST; **15(5)** admissions incl. private aided/unaided institutions **except minority institutions**; **15(6)** EWS. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| **16** | Equality of opportunity in public employment → citizens. **16(3)** residence requirement only by **Parliament**; **16(4)** inadequately represented backward class; **16(4A)** promotion for SC/ST; **16(4B)** carry-forward vacancies; **16(6)** EWS. Art 335 efficiency is relevant to reservation, but **does not define “efficiency of administration.”** [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| **17** | Untouchability abolished; practice in any form forbidden; offence punishable by law. **Right to Equality**, not Right against Exploitation. |
| **18** | State cannot confer titles except **military/academic distinctions**. National civilian awards are not prohibited titles when not used as prefixes/suffixes. |

**UPSC-2021 award trap:** Padma Awards instituted **1954**; interruptions occurred in **1978–79 and 1993–97**; Bharat Ratna annual number is capped at **3**, not 5. [Padma Awards](https://padmaawards.gov.in/About?q=9hETifMJ51Cujmb2krY%2FNQ%3D%3D)

### C. Right to Freedom — Articles 19–22

**Article 19 — six freedoms**
1. speech & expression;
2. peaceful assembly **without arms**;
3. association/union/co-operative society;
4. movement throughout India;
5. residence/settlement;
6. profession/occupation/trade/business.

**Restriction grounds**
- **Speech [19(2)]** → sovereignty & integrity; security of State; friendly relations with foreign States; public order; decency/morality; contempt; defamation; incitement to offence.
- **Assembly [19(3)]** → sovereignty & integrity; public order.
- **Association [19(4)]** → sovereignty & integrity; public order; morality.
- **Movement/residence [19(5)]** → general public; interests of Scheduled Tribes.
- **Profession [19(6)]** → general public; professional/technical qualifications; State monopoly. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

**Property is NOT an Article 19 freedom**: former Art 19(1)(f) omitted by 44th Amendment. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

| Article | Frozen payload |
|---|---|
| **20** | Three protections only: **(1) no ex-post-facto criminal conviction/greater penalty; (2) no double jeopardy—prosecuted and punished for same offence; (3) no compelled self-incrimination by accused.** [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| **21** | No person deprived of life/personal liberty except according to **procedure established by law**. *Maneka Gandhi* → procedure must be fair, just, reasonable; substantive due-process/natural-justice dimension. |
| **21A** | State to provide **free + compulsory education, age 6–14**; inserted by **86th Amendment Act, 2002**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| **22** | Ordinary arrest: grounds of arrest + chosen legal practitioner + magistrate within **24 h excluding journey time**. These Art 22(1)–(2) protections do **not** apply to enemy aliens or preventive detention. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |

### D. Article 21 — judicial expansion: retain only high-yield durable layer

- **Human dignity**
- **privacy** — *K.S. Puttaswamy (9J), 2017*; intrinsic facet of liberty/dignity/Art 21 + Part III; **not absolute**.
- **Privacy restriction test:** legality/existence of law → legitimate State aim/need → proportionality. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2017/16058/16058_2017_14_1501_65616_Judgement_10-Nov-2025.pdf)
- **marry/person of one’s choice**
- **healthy/decent environment**
- livelihood
- shelter/health
- free legal aid
- speedy/fair trial
- travel abroad

Laxmikanth’s updated treatment likewise places privacy, livelihood, legal aid, speedy trial and allied rights within the Article 21 expansion. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

**Due process trap:** `procedure established by law` ≠ mere existence of a formally enacted procedure after *Maneka*; fairness/natural justice is constitutionally relevant.

### E. Article 22 — preventive detention

- Art 22(1)–(2) ordinary-arrest safeguards excluded by **22(3)** for preventive detention.
- Preventive detainee:
  - grounds to be communicated as soon as may be;
  - earliest opportunity to make representation;
  - facts against public interest need not be disclosed.
- **Current effective constitutional threshold:** detention beyond **3 months** normally requires Advisory Board route.
- **Critical trap:** 44th Amendment text substituting **2 months** has **not been notified**; official Constitution still marks it “date yet to be notified.” [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### F. Exploitation, religion, minorities

| Articles | Payload |
|---|---|
| **23–24** | **Right against Exploitation**. Art 23: trafficking, begar, similar forced labour prohibited; compulsory public service permissible without specified discrimination. Art 24: child **below 14** cannot work in factory/mine/other hazardous employment. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| **25** | Freedom of conscience + profess/practise/propagate religion; subject to public order, morality, health and other FR; secular activities/social reform regulable. |
| **26** | Religious denomination/section → establish institutions; manage religious affairs; own/acquire/administer property; subject to public order, morality, health. |
| **27** | No compulsion to pay **taxes specifically appropriated** to promotion/maintenance of a particular religion/denomination. |
| **28** | Wholly State-maintained institution → no religious instruction; trust/endowment exception; recognised/aided institution → no compulsory religious instruction/worship without consent. |
| **29(1)** | **Any section of citizens**, not merely a minority, having distinct language/script/culture → conserve it. |
| **29(2)** | No denial of admission to State-maintained/aided institution only on religion/race/caste/language. |
| **30** | **Religious or linguistic minorities** → establish/administer educational institutions; State cannot discriminate in aid because institution is minority-managed. Constitution does **not define “minority.”** |

Official text supports the Art 23–30 distinctions, including the crucial “any section of citizens” wording in Art 29 and religion/language formulation in Art 30. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### G. Articles 31A–31C + Right to Property

- **Art 31 omitted** by 44th Amendment.
- **31A:** specified estate/property-management etc. laws protected primarily from Arts **14 & 19** challenge; State law requires reservation for President + assent for protection.
- **31B / Ninth Schedule:** no blanket immunity from basic-structure judicial review; post-*Kesavananda* insertions remain reviewable.
- **31C — operative scope:** laws implementing **Art 39(b) or 39(c)** protected from Arts 14/19 challenge; 42nd-Amendment extension to all DPSPs was invalidated; judicial review cannot be wholly excluded. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- **Art 300A:** “No person shall be deprived of his property save by authority of law.” → **constitutional right, not Fundamental Right; available to any person**. UPSC 2021’s option described it as a “legal right available to any person.” [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- No direct Art 32 petition **solely** to enforce Art 300A as an FR; constitutional/judicial remedies remain otherwise available.

### H. Article 32 + writs

- **Art 32 itself = guaranteed FR** for enforcement of Part III.
- SC: directions/orders + **Habeas Corpus, Mandamus, Prohibition, Certiorari, Quo Warranto**.
- Parliament may empower another court with Art 32(2)-type powers within its jurisdiction. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

| Writ | Meaning / core use | High-value trap |
|---|---|---|
| **Habeas Corpus** | “have the body”; release from unlawful detention | Can operate against **public or private detention**; locus standi relaxed. |
| **Mandamus** | “we command”; compel performance of public/legal duty | **Nature of duty matters, not merely identity of body**. Can reach a private body performing a public duty; not for purely private-law obligations. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2024/56494/56494_2024_8_1501_62737_Judgement_30-Jul-2025.pdf) |
| **Prohibition** | higher court → lower court/tribunal; stop proceedings for jurisdictional/legal defect | **Preventive** → issued before proceeding is completed. UPSC 2024: prevents lower court continuing proceeding. |
| **Certiorari** | higher court calls for/quashes proceeding/order of inferior judicial/quasi-judicial authority; modern scope also reaches qualifying administrative decisions | Mainly **corrective/curative**; Prohibition stops, Certiorari quashes. |
| **Quo Warranto** | “by what authority”; challenges unlawful occupation of public office | Petitioner need not show personal injury; office must be public/substantive and legally created. |

**Article 32 vs 226**
- **SC Art 32:** FR enforcement only; itself guaranteed FR.
- **HC Art 226:** FR **and “any other purpose”** → substantively wider writ jurisdiction.
- Art 226 can operate where cause of action wholly/partly arises within HC jurisdiction even if authority/person is outside. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### I. Articles 33–35 + emergency effect

- **Art 33:** **Parliament** may restrict/abrogate Part III rights for Armed Forces, forces maintaining public order, intelligence/counter-intelligence and specified connected telecom personnel—to ensure duties/discipline.
- **Art 34:** Parliament may indemnify acts etc. connected with martial law.
- **Art 35:** specified Part III legislative fields reserved to **Parliament**, including matters under Arts 16(3), 32(3), 33, 34 and punishment for acts declared offences under Part III. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- **Art 358:** Art 19 effect changes automatically only during National Emergency based on **war or external aggression**, not armed rebellion.
- **Art 359:** Presidential order may suspend **right to move courts for enforcement** of specified Part III rights; **Arts 20 & 21 cannot be included**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### J. UDHR elimination layer

- **UDHR 1948:** right to education; equal access to public service; adequate standard of living including food → all recognised in UDHR. [United Nations](https://www.un.org/en/about-us/universal-declaration-of-human-rights)
- Indian constitutional reflection of UDHR principles is **not confined to Fundamental Rights** → also Preamble + DPSP + Fundamental Duties.
- Do not equate every UDHR right with a directly enforceable Indian Fundamental Right.

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer
- **Quotation recall:** “Fundamental Rights — a pledge to our people and a pact with the civilized world” → **Dr S. Radhakrishnan** (`UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q016`).
- **Latin/direct writ recall:** Habeas Corpus → have the body; Mandamus → we command; Certiorari → to be certified/informed; Quo Warranto → by what authority. `UPPCS-2019-GS1-Q032`.
- **Amendment recall:** Art 21A / Right to Education → **86th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2002** (`UPPCS-2022-GS1-Q126`).
- No separate Uttar Pradesh-specific rights layer is supported by the identified canonical demand.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- Art **14 = any person** ≠ Arts **15/16/19 = citizens**.
- Art 29(1) = **any section of citizens** ≠ minority-only.
- Art 30 = **religious/linguistic minorities**; “minority” itself not constitutionally defined.
- Untouchability **Art 17 / Right to Equality** ≠ Right against Exploitation.
- Right against Exploitation = **Arts 23–24**.
- Property **≠ Art 19 FR**; now **Art 300A**, non-FR constitutional right.
- Art 20 = ex-post-facto criminal law + double jeopardy + self-incrimination; **torture is not one of its three textual clauses**.
- Art 21 “procedure established by law” after *Maneka* ≠ arbitrary procedure.
- Privacy = Art 21/Part III; **not absolute**.
- Art 21A = **6–14 years** ≠ all education.
- Preventive detention ≠ ordinary arrest; Art 22(1)–(2) protections excluded.
- Preventive-detention threshold currently **3 months** ≠ unnotified 2-month 44th-Amendment text.
- Mandamus against private body **can lie for public duty** ≠ blanket prohibition.
- Prohibition **stops** proceeding ≠ Certiorari **quashes/corrects**.
- Art 32 = FR enforcement only ≠ Art 226 = FR + other legal rights/purposes.
- Quo Warranto ≠ requirement of personal injury.
- National civilian awards ≠ prohibited titles when not used as prefix/suffix.
- Art 13 “law” **includes custom/usage with force of law**.
- Constitutional amendment excluded from Art 13 by 13(4) ≠ immune from basic-structure review.
- Art 31C operative protection = **Art 39(b),(c)** ≠ every DPSP.
- Art 358 = Article 19 + war/external aggression ≠ every National Emergency.
- Art 359 can suspend enforcement remedy for specified FR ≠ Arts 20/21.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT

| Base/source fact | Corrected fact | Authority/reason |
|---|---|---|
| Mandamus framed as unavailable against private bodies | Private status alone is not decisive; mandamus/Art 226 can reach a private body where a **public duty/public-law element** is involved | UPSC-2022 demand + SC doctrine [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2024/56494/56494_2024_8_1501_62737_Judgement_30-Jul-2025.pdf) |
| `K.S. Puttaswamy` treated simply as “Aadhaar case” | **Puttaswamy (9J), 2017** established constitutional privacy; later Aadhaar merits judgment applied privacy/proportionality doctrine | SC [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2020/23817/23817_2020_34_1501_31829_Judgement_06-Dec-2021.pdf) |
| Unni Krishnan phrasing suggests education “up to secondary level” | Judicially recognised free education **until age 14**; Art 21A later constitutionally fixes **6–14 years** | Constitution + updated reference [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| FR may be curtailed/repealed by constitutional amendment without qualification | Amendment power exists **subject to basic structure** | Kesavananda/basic-structure qualification; Article 13(4) itself only removes amendments from Art 13 [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| Art 300A characterised as merely protecting against executive, not legislative, deprivation | Property is a **constitutional right**; deprivation requires valid authority of law and remains subject to constitutional scrutiny | SC 2024 treatment [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/1992/78629/78629_1992_1_1501_57003_Judgement_05-Nov-2024.pdf) |
| Any broad reading that Art 31C protects implementation of all DPSPs | Operative protection limited to **Art 39(b),(c)**; 42nd-Amendment extension to all Part IV was invalidated | Official Constitution footnote / Minerva Mills [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |

### VERIFY
- **None unresolved.**
- `UPPCS-2019-GS1-Q026` remains a **canonical data-quality issue** (`OPTION_BOUNDARY_UNCLEAR`); its concepts are usable, but its stored option-answer record should not be used for scoring until corpus correction.

### OMIT
- Dynamic telecom/criminal-procedure section numbers and current statutory minutiae nested under Articles 19/21/22 → sibling **Statutory Law & Rights Legislation** node.
- Long Article 21 judgment catalogue beyond the high-yield durable rights retained above.
- Detailed current award administration except the exact durable facts required by the UPSC-2021 PYQ.
- PM’s 15-Point Programme as a permanent constitutional minority-right rule: retain `UPSC-CSE-2011-GS1-Q033` only as a **legacy PYQ context**; programme eligibility is non-constitutional/dynamic.
- Current-event narration around privacy/marriage-choice judgments; retain only durable constitutional propositions as required by taxonomy.

## 8. Supplement Decision

`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

Current taxonomy + canonical PYQs + base notes + reference book + official constitutional/Supreme Court verification answer all identified demands.

# Polity & Governance → Rights, Duties & Protective Frameworks → DPSP & Fundamental Duties — Source Pack v01

**Status:** PASS_WITH_CORRECTIONS  
**taxonomy_id:** `prelims-polity-and-governance-rights-duties-and-protective-frameworks-dpsp-and-fundamental-duties`

## 1. Scope
- **Includes:**
  - DPSP: Part IV, Articles 36–51; nature, purpose, article-content mapping, conventional classification, amendment-added directives.
  - Fundamental Duties: Part IVA, Article 51A; origin, nature, complete list, amendment history.
  - High-yield DPSP–FD overlaps/distinctions.
  - Limited FR–DPSP relationship only where necessary for Article 31C / PYQ traps.
  - Part/article/amendment/source mapping.
- **Excludes:**
  - Fundamental Rights/writ doctrine except narrow DPSP interaction.
  - Detailed statutory implementation, welfare schemes, labour-law provisions, UCC current-policy debate.
  - Scheduled/tribal frameworks and other rights legislation.
  - Dynamic scheme examples, current office-holders, sectoral economic-policy detail.
- **UPSC depth:** qualifier-heavy nature questions; “which Part enshrines X”; welfare-state identification; DPSP vs enforceable rights; Gandhian classification; article-content elimination; FD membership/non-membership.
- **UPPCS depth:** greater Part/article/amendment/source recall; direct matching; DPSP–FD overlap; “not correctly matched” formats.
- **Recurring evidence clusters:** DPSP content; DPSP nature; Fundamental Duties nature/list; welfare-state location. This is the exact taxonomy-owned scope. PRE-POL__TAXONOMY_GATEWAY

### Demand synthesis

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Constitutional frame

| Item | Payload |
|---|---|
| **DPSP location** | **Part IV; Articles 36–51** |
| **Source** | Idea borrowed from **Irish Constitution**; Irish model itself influenced by Spanish Constitution |
| **Art. 36** | “State” = same meaning as Part III |
| **Art. 37** | DPSPs **not enforceable by any court**; nevertheless **fundamental in governance**; State has duty to apply them while making laws |
| **Core purpose** | Social + economic democracy; welfare-state orientation |
| **Nature** | Constitutional directions to State; primarily positive obligations; no individual judicial remedy merely for non-implementation |
| **Classification** | Socialist / Gandhian / Liberal–Intellectual = **scholarly/conventional classification, not constitutional classification** |

Article 37 expressly combines non-enforceability with their status as fundamental to governance. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### B. DPSP article map

| Article | Core content | High-yield tag |
|---|---|---|
| **38** | Welfare-promoting social order; justice — social, economic, political; minimise inequalities | Socialist |
| **39(a)** | Adequate means of livelihood | Socialist |
| **39(b)** | Material resources distributed to subserve **common good** | Socialist / Art.31C |
| **39(c)** | Prevent concentration of wealth / means of production to common detriment | Socialist / Art.31C |
| **39(d)** | Equal pay for equal work — men & women | Socialist |
| **39(e)** | Protect health/strength of workers; tender age of children | Socialist |
| **39(f)** | Healthy development of children; dignity; protection from exploitation/abandonment | 42nd CA modification |
| **39A** | Equal justice + free legal aid | 42nd CA |
| **40** | Village panchayats as units of self-government | Gandhian |
| **41** | Work, education, public assistance in specified cases — **within economic capacity/development** | Socialist |
| **42** | Just & humane conditions of work + maternity relief | Socialist |
| **43** | Living wage, decent standard of life, leisure/social-cultural opportunities; rural cottage industries | Socialist + Gandhian element |
| **43A** | Workers’ participation in management of industries | 42nd CA |
| **43B** | Voluntary formation, autonomous functioning, democratic control, professional management of cooperatives | 97th CA |
| **44** | Uniform Civil Code for citizens | Liberal–Intellectual |
| **45** | Early childhood care + education **below 6 years** | 86th CA revised text |
| **46** | Educational/economic interests of weaker sections, particularly SCs/STs; protection from social injustice/exploitation | Gandhian |
| **47** | Nutrition, standard of living, public health; prohibition of injurious intoxicants/drugs except medicinal use | Socialist + Gandhian |
| **48** | Agriculture/animal husbandry on modern scientific lines; preserve/improve breeds; cattle-slaughter directive | Gandhian + Liberal element |
| **48A** | Protect/improve environment; safeguard forests & wildlife | 42nd CA |
| **49** | State obligation to protect monuments/places/objects of national importance | Liberal–Intellectual |
| **50** | Separate judiciary from executive in public services of State | Liberal–Intellectual |
| **51** | International peace/security; honourable relations; international law/treaties; arbitration | Liberal–Intellectual |

The constitutional text directly distinguishes **43A workers**, **43B cooperatives**, **44 UCC** and **45 under-six care/education**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf) Articles 46–51 establish the weaker-section, public-health, agriculture, environment, monument, judicial-separation and international-peace mappings. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

### C. Amendment map

| Amendment | DPSP / Duty effect |
|---|---|
| **42nd CA, 1976** | Modified Art.39(f); inserted **39A, 43A, 48A**; inserted **Part IVA + Art.51A**, initially 10 duties |
| **44th CA, 1978** | Added **Art.38(2)** → minimise inequalities in income/status/facilities/opportunities |
| **86th CA, 2002** | Recast **Art.45** to early childhood care/education below 6; added **Art.51A(k)** |
| **97th CA, 2011** | Inserted **Art.43B** — cooperative societies |

### D. Fundamental Rights ↔ DPSP: only PYQ-relevant constitutional position
- No blanket formula **“FR always supreme”** or **“DPSP always supreme.”**
- **Article 31C:** protection attaches to laws giving effect to **Article 39(b) or 39(c)** against challenge under **Articles 14 and 19**, subject to judicial review.
- 42nd Amendment attempted to extend this protection from 39(b)/(c) to **all DPSPs** → extension invalidated in **Minerva Mills**.
- Constitutional balance/harmony between Parts III and IV is a basic-structure consideration; recent Supreme Court treatment continues to describe Article 31C as balancing Part IV goals with Part III rights. [Supreme Court of India](https://www.sci.gov.in/sci-get-pdf/?diary_no=786291992\&from=latest_judgements_order\&order_date=2024-11-05\&type=j\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### E. Fundamental Duties

**Part IVA — Article 51A — duty of every citizen of India.** Original 10 inserted by 42nd CA; clause (k) added by 86th CA. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

| Clause | Duty — compressed payload |
|---|---|
| **51A(a)** | Abide Constitution; respect ideals/institutions; National Flag + Anthem |
| **(b)** | Cherish/follow noble ideals of freedom struggle |
| **(c)** | Uphold/protect sovereignty, unity, integrity |
| **(d)** | Defend country; render national service when called |
| **(e)** | Promote harmony/common brotherhood; renounce practices derogatory to dignity of women |
| **(f)** | Value/preserve rich heritage of composite culture |
| **(g)** | Protect/improve natural environment incl. forests, lakes, rivers, wildlife; compassion for living creatures |
| **(h)** | Scientific temper, humanism, spirit of inquiry & reform |
| **(i)** | Safeguard public property; abjure violence |
| **(j)** | Strive for excellence — individual + collective activity |
| **(k)** | Parent/guardian: provide educational opportunities to child/ward **6–14 years** |

**Nature**
- Only **citizens**, not foreigners.
- Non-justiciable: Article 51A itself provides no direct judicial remedy for breach.
- Parliament may enact laws dealing with conduct corresponding to particular duties; statutory liability ≠ direct judicial enforceability of Article 51A.
- Enjoyment of Fundamental Rights is **not generally conditional** on prior performance of Fundamental Duties.

### F. Three-way education trap

| Provision | Who owes/holds what? | Age |
|---|---|---|
| **Art.45 — DPSP** | State → early childhood care + education | **below 6** |
| **Art.21A — FR** | State → free & compulsory education | **6–14** |
| **Art.51A(k) — FD** | Parent/guardian → educational opportunity to child/ward | **6–14** |

### G. High-yield overlap / non-overlap

| Item | DPSP | Fundamental Duty |
|---|---|---|
| **Environment** | **48A** | **51A(g)** |
| Monuments of national importance | **49** | **No** |
| Weaker sections / SC-ST interests | **46** | **No** |
| Workers in management | **43A** | **No** |
| Cooperatives | **43B** | **No** |
| UCC | **44** | **No** |
| Sovereignty, unity, integrity | — | **51A(c)** |
| Scientific temper | — | **51A(h)** |
| Vote in elections | — | **Not a Fundamental Duty** |
| Pay taxes | — | **Not a Fundamental Duty** |

Environment is deliberately present on both sides: State directive under Article 48A and citizen duty under Article 51A(g). [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer
- **Origin recall:** DPSP → Ireland; Fundamental Duties → erstwhile USSR; FD insertion → **Swaran Singh Committee + 42nd CA**.
- **Part recall:** DPSP → **Part IV**; FD → **Part IVA**.
- **Rapid article map:** `39A legal aid` → `40 panchayat` → `43A workers` → `43B cooperatives` → `44 UCC` → `46 weaker sections` → `48A environment` → `49 monuments` → `50 judiciary` → `51 international peace` → `51A duties`.
- **UPPCS-specific elimination:** environment = both; monument protection = DPSP only; vote ≠ FD; `43A ≠ 43B`; `48 ≠ 50`.
- **Gateway integrity flags:** `UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q046`, `UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q138` remain editable-review/table-structure records; `UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q110` has malformed parsed structure. Preserve canonical IDs; use for demand evidence, not blind answer ingestion.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- Part **IV** ≠ Part **IVA**.
- Article **37: non-justiciable** ≠ non-fundamental to governance.
- DPSP **guides State action** ≠ constitutionally limits legislative/executive functions.
- Welfare-state ideal → **DPSP** in recurring UPSC “which Part?” questions.
- **39(c)** ≠ “Article 39C”.
- **39A** free legal aid ≠ Fundamental Right by textual location.
- **43A workers** ≠ **43B cooperatives**.
- **48 agriculture/cattle** ≠ **48A environment**.
- **48A + 51A(g)** = environment in both.
- **49 monuments** ≠ Fundamental Duty.
- **50 judiciary–executive separation** ≠ Article 48.
- **51 international peace** ≠ Fundamental Duty.
- **Art.45 <6** ≠ **Art.21A 6–14** ≠ **51A(k) parent/guardian 6–14**.
- Protect weaker sections = **Art.46 DPSP**, not FD.
- Voting ≠ FD; paying tax ≠ FD.
- Fundamental Duties apply to **citizens only**.
- Socialist/Gandhian/Liberal–Intellectual grouping = **study classification**, not constitutional text.
- Article 31C protection = **39(b)/(c)**; no blanket DPSP supremacy.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT
| Base/source fact | Corrected fact | Authority/reason |
|---|---|---|
| “97th Amendment: **Article 48B** — Cooperative Societies” | **Article 43B** | Constitution expressly places cooperative-society DPSP at 43B. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf) |
| “Article **39C** — concentration of wealth” | **Article 39(c)** | Clause (c) within Article 39. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| “42nd: DPSP >> FR; Minerva Mills: FR >> DPSP; current FR supreme” | Remove blanket hierarchy. Harmony/balance governs; Art.31C survives in limited 39(b)/(c) field | Supreme Court treatment of Article 31C / Minerva Mills. [Supreme Court of India](https://www.sci.gov.in/sci-get-pdf/?diary_no=786291992\&from=latest_judgements_order\&order_date=2024-11-05\&type=j\&utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| “Art.31C → FRs can be amended and such amendments cannot be challenged” | Art.31C concerns protection of qualifying **laws** implementing 39(b)/(c); judicial-review exclusion did not survive | Supreme Court. [Supreme Court of India](https://www.sci.gov.in/sci-get-pdf/?diary_no=786291992\&from=latest_judgements_order\&order_date=2024-11-05\&type=j\&utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| “Art.48A shifted forests/wildlife to Concurrent List” | 42nd CA inserted Art.48A **and separately** amended Seventh Schedule; Art.48A itself is the environmental directive | Avoid causal conflation |
| “FDs are enforceable by law” | **Art.51A is non-justiciable**; legislation may independently prescribe enforceable duties/offences | Distinguish constitutional duty from statutory sanction |

### VERIFY
- **`UPPCS-2025-GS1-Q146`** — canonical gateway records option **B**, while Ghatna Chakra and multiple secondary answer reproductions differ. Official UPPSC key was not located during verification. **Use question only as evidence of demand; do not encode its answer into the final note until canonical record is source-fixed.**
- **`UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q046`**, **`UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q138`** — canonical disposition already marks table structure/answer usability for review; retain IDs, not their malformed rendering.
- **`UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q110`** — FROZEN record but parsed question/options structurally malformed; semantic demand is Part IVA/Fundamental Duties mapping.

### OMIT
- Long UCC litigation/history and current-policy debate.
- Current schemes as examples for Arts.41/47.
- Detailed maternity-benefit statute numbers.
- Detailed legal-aid institutional eligibility.
- Dynamic cooperative-sector counts/initiatives.
- Long lists of DPSP criticisms and quotations.
- Absolute claim that Japan is the “only” other democratic constitution with duties.
- Sectoral/current implementations not required by the taxonomy/PYQs.

## 8. Supplement Decision

`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

All identified substantive PYQ demands are answerable from the current base notes + constitutional/reference sources. Remaining issue is a **canonical PYQ answer-key/data-quality repair**, not a content-source gap.

# Polity & Governance → Rights, Duties & Protective Frameworks → Scheduled & tribal areas (Fifth/Sixth Schedule, PESA) — Source Pack v01

**Status:** PASS_WITH_CORRECTIONS  
**taxonomy_id:** `prelims-polity-and-governance-rights-duties-and-protective-frameworks-scheduled-and-tribal-areas-fifth-sixth-schedule-pesa`

## 1. Scope
- Includes: Fifth Schedule; Sixth Schedule; Scheduled Area notification/administration; Tribes Advisory Council; Governor/President roles; PESA powers; ST recognition under Article 342; PVTGs. PRE-POL__TAXONOMY_GATEWAY

- UPSC depth: Fifth Schedule **purpose + legal/administrative consequences**; President vs Governor; continuation of State executive power; land safeguards; PESA qualifier traps; ST recognition; PVTG identification.
- UPPCS depth: Part/Schedule/Article mapping; Sixth-Schedule State mapping; PESA enactment year; direct institutional recall.

### UPPCS

  - `UPPCS-2019-GS1-Q031`
- **PESA — enactment year**
  - `UPPCS-2022-GS1-Q076`
- **Part mapping — Scheduled & Tribal Areas = Part X**
  - `UPPCS-2023-GS1-Q001`

### Demand synthesis
- recurring concepts: Fifth vs Sixth; tribal-land protection; Scheduled Area declaration; State/Governor/Union roles; PESA Gram Sabha; ST notification; PVTG criteria.
- recurring traps:
  - President declares Scheduled Area ≠ Governor.
  - Governor reports to **President** ≠ CM reports to Home Ministry.
  - Fifth Schedule ≠ automatic autonomous local government.
  - Scheduled Area declaration ≠ State executive power ceases.
  - PESA mining recommendation = **minor minerals**, not any mineral.
  - PESA ≠ creation of autonomous tribal regions.
  - ST status = State/UT-specific.
- qualifier patterns: **minor**, **not more than**, **as nearly as may be**, **annually or whenever required**, **consulted** vs **recommendation mandatory**.
- UPSC vs UPPCS: UPSC → statement/consequence/qualifier elimination; UPPCS → direct Schedule–State–Part–year recall.
- recent reinforcement: continuous UPSC testing from 2022–2026 on Scheduled Area consequences, notification, ST recognition and Fifth/Sixth distinction.

## 3. Base-Note Coverage
- already strong: Fifth/Sixth architecture; ATM2 distinction; President/Governor roles; State executive power; TAC; land-transfer regulation; ADC/ARC; PESA exclusion from Sixth Schedule. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- partial: PESA statutory wording; Sixth-Schedule council composition; precise TAC composition; ST-notification procedure.
- missing: PVTG payload; explicit `consultation ≠ mandatory recommendation`; PESA fund-utilisation **certification**; constitutional/statutory qualifier language.
- redundant: detailed current number of individual ADCs/councils; committee trivia not demanded by canonical PYQs.
- outdated/suspect: fixed “30 = 26 elected + 4 nominated” wording; current count of States that have notified PESA Rules; attribution of “Constitution within Constitution” to PESA.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### Constitutional map

| Anchor | Payload |
|---|---|
| **Part X** | Scheduled and Tribal Areas |
| **Art. 244(1) + Fifth Schedule** | Administration/control of Scheduled Areas + STs; excludes Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| **Art. 244(2) + Sixth Schedule** | Tribal Areas in **Assam, Meghalaya, Tripura, Mizoram (ATM2)**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| **Art. 244A** | Parliament may create an autonomous State within Assam comprising specified tribal areas; legislature/CoM/both possible. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| **Art. 275(1)** | Grants-in-aid relevant to ST welfare / raising administration level in Scheduled Areas. |
| **Art. 339** | Union control over Scheduled-Area administration + ST welfare; commission mechanism. |
| **Art. 342** | President specifies STs by public notification; for a State → consultation with Governor; Parliament alone can later include/exclude by law; status is State/UT-specific. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) |

### Fifth Schedule

- **Scheduled Area declaration:** President by Order; alteration/redefinition powers also with President; Governor consultation required in specified cases. Declaration criteria are established administrative criteria, **not written into Constitution**: tribal-population preponderance; compact/reasonable area; viable administrative unit; relative economic backwardness. [Tribal Affairs India](https://tribal.nic.in/Clm.aspx?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **State executive power continues** in Scheduled Areas. Governor → report to President **annually or whenever President requires**; Union → may give directions to State regarding administration. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Tribes Advisory Council (TAC):**
  - mandatory in each State having Scheduled Areas;
  - may also be created in a State having STs but no Scheduled Area if President directs;
  - **not more than 20 members**;
  - as nearly as possible **3/4 = ST representatives in State Legislative Assembly**;
  - advises on ST welfare/advancement matters referred by Governor. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Governor — law/regulation power:**
  - public notification → Parliamentary/State Act may not apply or may apply with exceptions/modifications; direction may be retrospective;
  - regulations for peace and good government;
  - may prohibit/restrict ST land transfer, regulate land allotment and money-lending;
  - regulations can amend/repeal applicable Parliamentary/State laws; require **President's assent**; TAC consultation where applicable. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **PYQ consequence:** Fifth Schedule provides the legal protective framework under which tribal-land alienation can be restricted; it does **not** by itself turn area into UT, create an autonomous local body, give Special Category State status, or extinguish State administration.
- Scheduled-Area hierarchy tested by UPSC: **district = largest unit; cluster of villages in a block = lowest unit**.
- Fifth Schedule purpose → protective administration: tribal autonomy/culture + protection from exploitation/land alienation; distinguish from Sixth Schedule's stronger autonomous-council model.

### Sixth Schedule

- Applies only to tribal areas in **Assam + Meghalaya + Tripura + Mizoram**. `Arunachal Pradesh / Nagaland / Manipur / Sikkim` ≠ Sixth Schedule. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Tribal areas → **autonomous districts**; different tribes within district → Governor may create **autonomous regions**.
- Governor can organise/reorganise autonomous districts: area/boundary/name etc.
- **District Council — general constitutional rule:** ≤30 members; ≤4 nominated by Governor; remainder elected by adult suffrage. Assam-specific constitutional exceptions exist → do not universalise `26 elected + 4 nominated`. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- District/Regional Councils possess substantial **legislative + executive + judicial + fiscal** powers; areas include land, non-reserved forests, shifting cultivation, village administration, inheritance, marriage/social customs; relevant council laws require Governor's assent.
- Councils may establish/manage institutions such as schools, dispensaries, markets, roads; may constitute village councils/courts and levy specified taxes.
- Autonomous districts **remain within the executive authority of the State**.
- Application/exclusion/modification of Parliamentary and State laws is specially regulated under the Sixth Schedule; authority varies by State/provision → avoid blanket rules.
- **PESA does not govern Sixth-Schedule tribal areas**: PESA defines “Scheduled Areas” by reference to **Art. 244(1)**, i.e. Fifth-Schedule Scheduled Areas. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)

### PESA, 1996

- Exact statute: **Provisions of the Panchayats (Extension to the Scheduled Areas) Act, 1996 — Act 40 of 1996; 24 Dec 1996.**
- Extends **Part IX** Panchayat provisions to Art. 244(1) Scheduled Areas, subject to statutory exceptions/modifications. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)
- State Panchayat laws must conform to **customary law + social/religious practices + traditional management of community resources**.
- Every village → Gram Sabha of persons on village-Panchayat electoral roll.
- Gram Sabha:
  - safeguard traditions/customs, cultural identity, community resources, customary dispute resolution;
  - approve development plans/programmes/projects before implementation;
  - identify/select beneficiaries.
- Village Panchayat must obtain **Gram Sabha certification of utilisation of funds** — not merely “permission to spend”. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)
- **Reservation:** ST seats ≥ **1/2 of total seats**; all Panchayat chairperson seats at all levels reserved for STs. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)
- **Consultation required:** Gram Sabha/Panchayat at appropriate level before **land acquisition** for development projects and before **resettlement/rehabilitation**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)
- **Mandatory prior recommendation:** prospecting licence/mining lease **for minor minerals**; concession for exploitation of **minor minerals** by auction. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)
- Gram Sabha / appropriate Panchayat specifically endowed with:
  - prohibition/regulation of intoxicants;
  - **ownership of minor forest produce**;
  - prevent land alienation + restore unlawfully alienated ST land;
  - manage village markets;
  - control money-lending to STs;
  - control social-sector institutions/functionaries;
  - control local plans/resources incl. tribal sub-plans. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)
- Appropriate Panchayat → planning/management of **minor water bodies**.
- Higher Panchayat cannot appropriate powers of lower Panchayat/Gram Sabha.

### ST recognition

- **Governor does not declare/recognise STs.**
- President → initial specification by public notification; State case requires consultation with Governor.
- Parliament → inclusion/exclusion thereafter **by law**.
- ST status is **territorially specific**: ST in one State/UT ≠ automatically ST elsewhere. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

### PVTGs

- PVTG = specially identified vulnerable category among tribal communities; not a separate constitutional Schedule/category under Art. 342.
- **75 PVTG communities; 18 States + 1 UT (Andaman & Nicobar Islands).**
- Identification indicators:
  - pre-agricultural level of technology;
  - stagnant/declining population;
  - very low literacy;
  - subsistence-level economy. [Tribal Affairs India](https://tribal.nic.in/DivisionsFiles/GuidelinesofPVTGs17092019.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Canonical-PYQ examples: **Irular + Konda Reddi = PVTGs**.
- Trap: **75 ≠ 95**.

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer
- **Part X** → Scheduled and Tribal Areas.
- **Sixth Schedule → ATM2**; among UPPCS options, **Tripura = yes; Nagaland/Sikkim = no**.
- **PESA = 1996**.
- High-value mapping:
  - Art. 244(1) → Fifth Schedule.
  - Art. 244(2) → Sixth Schedule.
  - Art. 244A → autonomous State within Assam.
  - Art. 342 → Scheduled Tribe specification.
- UPPCS demand is predominantly direct constitutional mapping; do not overload with changing council/state-rule counts.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- Fifth Schedule ≠ Sixth Schedule.
- Fifth → Scheduled Areas/ST administration outside ATM2; Sixth → tribal areas of ATM2.
- **President declares Scheduled Area ≠ Governor.**
- **President specifies ST ≠ Governor.**
- Governor consultation ≠ Governor notification of ST.
- Governor reports to **President** ≠ CM reports to Home Ministry.
- Scheduled Area → **State executive power continues**.
- Fifth Schedule ≠ automatic autonomous local body / UT / Special Category State.
- TAC = **≤20**, not exactly 20.
- Sixth-Schedule District Council = **≤30**, not universally 30/26+4.
- PESA → Fifth-Schedule Scheduled Areas ≠ Sixth-Schedule tribal areas.
- PESA **consultation** for land acquisition/R&R ≠ mandatory recommendation.
- PESA **mandatory recommendation** for mining/prospecting = **minor minerals only**.
- Gram Sabha → ownership of **minor forest produce**.
- PESA ≠ creation of autonomous regions.
- ST status = State/UT-specific.
- PVTG = 75, not 95.
- “Scheduled Area criteria” = established administrative criteria ≠ text expressly listed in Constitution.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT
- Base: **TAC = 20-member body** → **not more than 20**; as nearly as possible 3/4 ST representatives in State Assembly → Fifth Schedule para 4. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Base: **Sixth-Schedule councils generally 30 = 26 elected + 4 nominated** → general constitutional ceiling = **≤30; ≤4 nominated; rest elected**; Assam-specific exceptions → Sixth Schedule para 2. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Base: **PESA “was called Constitution within the Constitution”** → do **not** attribute this label to PESA; it is not PESA statutory terminology. If retained at all, treat only as secondary commentary about the Fifth-Schedule protective framework.
- Base: **Panchayat uses funds with permission of Gram Sabha** → statute requires **Gram Sabha certification of utilisation of funds**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)
- Base title: “Panchayat Extension to Scheduled Areas Act” → exact title: **Provisions of the Panchayats (Extension to the Scheduled Areas) Act, 1996**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1973/1/a199640.pdf)
- Base: fixed/current PESA-rules count → remove from durable payload; notification status is dynamic.
- Base Fifth/Sixth core is otherwise strong and PYQ-aligned. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…

### VERIFY
- None materially unresolved.

### OMIT
- Current count/status of States notifying PESA Rules.
- Exhaustive current list/count of individual Autonomous District Councils.
- Dynamic office-holders / tribal schemes.
- Detailed committee history unless separately demanded.
- FRA substantive provisions: separate topic; retain only where needed for an explicit PESA/Fifth-Schedule distinction.

## 8. Supplement Decision
`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

Current taxonomy + canonical PYQ gateway + base notes + Laxmikanth + official Constitution/India Code/Ministry of Tribal Affairs material answer all identified PYQ demands.
