- **already strong:** Article 21A/RTE constitutional anchor; 6–14 years; basic RTE Act provisions; Article 22 arrest safeguards; basic bail taxonomy; Naveen Jindal/flag-hoisting cross-link. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **partial:** custody/bail material exists but is mixed with Fundamental Rights and the dynamic new-criminal-law layer; RTE lacks the decisive **NCTE/TET** PYQ distinction; Flag coverage lacks Flag Code dimensions.
- **missing:** consumer-law core; parole/prisons framework; current RPwD Act payload; Sugamya Bharat/NDFDC institutional traps; CERT-In reporting; Official Secrets/Arms Act matching; land-acquisition core.
- **redundant:** extended Article 21 case-law catalogue; ASER/PRATHAM detail; DPDP/personality-rights layer; preventive-detention case law for this node.
- **outdated/suspect:** “regular bail = person in police custody” is too narrow; BNSS §479 is placed under a misleading **“Bailable Offences”** label; base-note Unni Krishnan formulation (“up to secondary level”) requires correction. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Custody, parole & prisons

| Item | Frozen fact | Elimination trap |
|---|---|---|
| **Police custody** | Accused physically in police custody; ordinarily police lock-up; investigation/interrogation by police | ≠ judicial custody |
| **Judicial custody** | Accused under custody/order of Magistrate/court; ordinarily lodged in jail | **not** police-station lock-up |
| **Interrogation in judicial custody** | Police cannot interrogate merely on their own authority; court permission required | judicial custody ≠ total bar on interrogation |
| **Parole** | Conditional/temporary release mechanism; **not an absolute enforceable right**; States have their own parole rules | sufficient case ≠ automatic right to parole |
| **Prisons** | **State List, Entry 4**; management/administration primarily State responsibility; Prisons Act, 1894 + State prison laws/manuals/rules form the legacy framework | prisons ≠ Union List |
| **Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act, 2023** | Model law supplied for adoption/adaptation by States | model law ≠ automatically operative uniform central prison law |

MHA confirms prisons are a State subject and State Governments hold primary responsibility for prison administration; it separately lists the Prisons Act, 1894 and the Model Prisons and Correctional Services Act. [Ministry of Home Affairs](https://www.mha.gov.in/MHA1/PrisonReforms/home.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### B. Act ↔ offence/action pairs

| Action | Correct legal anchor | Trap |
|---|---|---|
| Unauthorised use/wearing of police or military uniform in the statutory context | **Official Secrets Act, 1923 — §6** | OSA is not confined to spying |
| Knowingly misleading/interfering with police/Armed Forces personnel in specified duties | **Official Secrets Act, 1923 — §7** | **not Indian Evidence Act, 1872** |
| Rash/negligent **celebratory gunfire** endangering human life/personal safety | **Arms Act, 1959 as amended by Arms (Amendment) Act, 2019** | explicit statutory offence |

Official Secrets Act §§6–7 contain the uniform/interference offences; the 2019 Arms amendment inserted the celebratory-gunfire offence. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/2379/1/A1923-19.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### C. Consumer protection

- **Current spine:** **Consumer Protection Act, 2019**; replaced the 1986 Act; provides CCPA + District/State/National Consumer Disputes Redressal Commissions. Avoid dynamic pecuniary thresholds in frozen notes. [Consumer Affairs](https://consumeraffairs.nic.in/sites/default/files/file-uploads/annualreports/1729492569_AR_2023_2024.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Who may complain:** consumer; recognised consumer association; governments/CCPA; consumers with same interest; **legal heir/legal representative after death of consumer**; parent/legal guardian of minor. [Consumer Affairs](https://consumeraffairs.nic.in/sites/default/files/file-uploads/latestnews/FAQ.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **2012 PYQ — legacy demand:** food sample for testing = permissible; “no complaint fee whatsoever” = false; legal heir after death = permissible. Preserve as **legacy PYQ rule**, not as a current fee schedule.
- **MRP:** Maximum Retail Price = **ceiling**, not mandatory transaction price; commodity may legally be sold **below MRP**. [Consumer Affairs](https://consumeraffairs.nic.in/sites/default/files/file-uploads/latestnews/LM_FAQs.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Consumer-law note must distinguish **Consumer Protection Act** from **Legal Metrology (Packaged Commodities) Rules**; MRP trap belongs primarily to the latter.

### D. Right to Education legislation

- **Constitutional anchor:** **Article 21A** → free + compulsory education, **6–14 years**; inserted by **86th Constitutional Amendment Act, 2002**.
- **Implementing statute:** **Right of Children to Free and Compulsory Education Act, 2009**; Article 21A + RTE Act operative from **1 April 2010**. [Education Government of India](https://www.education.gov.in/sites/upload_files/mhrd/files/document-reports/57thCABEAgenda-Background.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Teacher eligibility — §23:** minimum qualifications laid down by the academic authority authorised by Central Government; **NCTE** laid down the qualifications for Classes I–VIII. **Not the State Council of Teacher Education.**
- **TET:** essential qualification in the relevant category; conducted by the appropriate Government/designated body **in accordance with NCTE guidelines**. [DSEL Education](https://dsel.education.gov.in/sites/default/files/rte/RTE_TET.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Stable RTE prohibitions: **capitation fee + screening procedure prohibited**; physical punishment/mental harassment prohibited; lack of age proof cannot itself justify denial of admission. [Education Government of India](https://www.education.gov.in/sites/upload_files/mhrd/files/upload_document/rte.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Constitution vs statute:** 86th Amendment creates constitutional FR architecture; RTE Act operationalises it.

### E. Disability rights

| Anchor | High-value fact |
|---|---|
| **Rights of Persons with Disabilities Act, 2016** | Current principal disability-rights statute; **2016, not 2018** |
| §31 | Child with **benchmark disability, 6–18 years** → right to free education in neighbourhood or special school of choice |
| §32 | Govt + Govt-aided higher educational institutions → **≥5% seats** for persons with benchmark disabilities |
| §34 | Government establishments → **≥4% vacancies** for persons with benchmark disabilities |
| §§40–42 | Accessibility; transport; ICT/access to information | :chatgpt-content-reference{index="13"}


- **Sugamya Bharat / Accessible India Campaign:** universal accessibility for PwDs; three verticals → **built environment + transportation + information/communication ecosystem**. [Empowerment for Persons with Disabilities](https://depwd.gov.in/en/accessible-india-campaign/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **NDFDC:** Central Public Sector Undertaking under **DEPwD, Ministry of Social Justice & Empowerment**; **not Ministry of Corporate Affairs**. [NDFDC](https://www.ndfdc.nic.in/contact-us?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **2011 disability PYQ:** all three then-asked benefits were accepted as legally available — free schooling to 18 in specified framework; preferential land allotment; ramps. Treat as **legacy-law evidence**; current-law recall must use RPwD Act, 2016.

### F. IT Act / cyber-reporting

- **IT Act, 2000 — §70B:** statutory basis for **CERT-In**.
- Mandatory cyber-incident reporting category includes **service providers + intermediaries + data centres + body corporates**; current directions additionally expressly cover Government organisations. [CERT-IN](https://cert-in.org.in/PDF/CERT-In_Directions_70B_28.04.2022.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- UPSC-2017 formulation — **service providers + data centres + body corporate = all covered**.
- Current reporting deadline/incident list = **replaceable current layer**; do not freeze changing operational timelines into permanent short notes.

### G. Flag Code / national-symbol law

- **Flag Code of India, 2002:** consolidates applicable laws, conventions, practices and instructions; display is also governed by statutes including the **Emblems and Names (Prevention of Improper Use) Act, 1950** and **Prevention of Insults to National Honour Act, 1971**. **Flag Code ≠ parliamentary statute.** [Ministry of Home Affairs](https://www.mha.gov.in/sites/default/files/flagcodeofindia_070214.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- National Flag shape: rectangular; **length : height(width) = 3 : 2**.
- Prescribed standard sizes in mm: **6300×4200; 3600×2400; 2700×1800; 1800×1200; 1350×900; 900×600; 450×300; 225×150; 150×100**. [Ministry of Home Affairs](https://www.mha.gov.in/sites/default/files/FlagAdvisoryEng_06082021_0.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Therefore **600×400 has the correct 3:2 ratio but is NOT a listed standard size**.
- **Dynamic marker:** MHA now separately lists later amendments/advisories, including a 2026 National Honour amendment; do not freeze amendment trivia unless a later current-affairs layer explicitly requires it. [Ministry of Home Affairs](https://www.mha.gov.in/en/documents/national-flag-emblem-anthem?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### H. Land acquisition — closely related stable layer

- **RFCTLARR Act, 2013**; effective **1 January 2014**.
- Core architecture: participative/transparent acquisition + fair compensation + rehabilitation & resettlement; statutory process includes **Social Impact Assessment (SIA)** and public hearing. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/handle/123456789/17043?col=123456789%2F2510\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Prior consent where applicable:
  - private-company acquisition → **≥80% affected families**;
  - PPP acquisition where land ownership continues with Government → **≥70% affected families**;
  - consent process accompanies SIA. [India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_CEN_18_43_00003_201330_1517807327433\&filename=a2013-30.pdf\&type=actfile\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **No direct canonical PYQ located in the supplied gateway for this sub-layer** → retain only this compact statutory spine.

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

- `UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q118` → **Sugamya Bharat = persons with disabilities**; learn three accessibility verticals, not scheme trivia.
- `UPPCS-2022-GS1-Q126` → **RTE as FR = 86th Amendment, 2002**; distinguish Amendment from RTE Act, 2009.
- `UPPCS-2025-GS1-Q137` → **MRP is maximum/ceiling; sale below MRP allowed**. Supplied canonical answer record is inconsistent with the official Legal Metrology rule. [Consumer Affairs](https://consumeraffairs.nic.in/sites/default/files/file-uploads/latestnews/LM_FAQs.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- No durable Uttar Pradesh-specific statute is demanded by the canonical records retrieved for this node.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist

- Police custody ≠ judicial custody.
- Judicial custody = jail/court custody ≠ police-station lock-up.
- Police interrogation in judicial custody → **court permission required**.
- Parole eligibility ≠ absolute legal right.
- Prisons = **State List** ≠ Union List.
- Model Prisons Act 2023 ≠ automatically binding State law.
- Official Secrets Act covers uniform/interference offences ≠ only espionage.
- Police/military interference pair ≠ Indian Evidence Act.
- Celebratory gunfire endangering safety → Arms law.
- Consumer legal heir **can complain** after death.
- MRP = maximum price ≠ price seller must necessarily charge.
- Sale **below MRP permitted** ≠ violation.
- 86th Amendment, 2002 ≠ RTE Act, 2009.
- Article 21A **6–14** ≠ RPwD free-education provision **6–18** for benchmark disability.
- RTE teacher-qualification authority: **NCTE/centrally authorised academic authority ≠ State Council**.
- **5% higher-education seats ≠ 4% government-employment vacancies** under RPwD.
- RPwD Act **2016 ≠ 2018**.
- Sugamya Bharat = built environment + transport + ICT.
- NDFDC → **DEPwD/MoSJ&E ≠ Ministry of Corporate Affairs**.
- CERT-In reporting: service providers + intermediaries + data centres + body corporates.
- Flag ratio **3:2** ≠ every 3:2 dimension being an authorised standard size.
- **600×400 ≠ standard Flag Code size**.
- Flag Code ≠ statute; National Honour Act = statute.
- Legacy 2011 disability-law PYQ ≠ present statutory wording.
- RFCTLARR: **80% private ≠ 70% PPP**.

- Laxmikanth coverage matches the required President→VP→PM→CoM→Parliament→Parliamentary Committees sequence and was used only for gap/correction support. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20…

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Parliament

**Constitutional map**
- **Arts 79–122** → Parliament.
- **Art 79:** President + Council of States + House of the People.
- **Art 80:** RS max **250** = 12 nominated + ≤238 States/UTs; nominated expertise = **literature, science, art, social service**; State representatives elected by elected MLAs through PR-STV. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- **Art 81:** LS constitutional ceiling = ≤530 State representatives + ≤20 UT representatives.
- RS = permanent House; LS ordinarily 5 years unless sooner dissolved.

**Presiding officers**
- VP = ex-officio Chairman, RS; **not an RS member**.
- Deputy Chairman = elected from RS members.
- Deputy Chairman removal → majority of **all then members** of RS (= effective majority), 14-day notice. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- Speaker + Deputy Speaker → elected by LS.
- Their removal → majority of **all then members** of LS; 14-day notice.
- Speaker survives LS dissolution until immediately before first meeting of new LS. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- During own removal resolution: Speaker cannot preside; may participate; first-instance vote, no casting vote.
- Deputy Speaker election date → **Speaker fixes under Lok Sabha Rule 8**; opposition-member convention ≠ constitutional/rule mandate. [eParliament](https://eparlib.sansad.in/bitstream/123456789/2495198/1/Rules_of_Procedures_E_2019_16th_edition.pdf)

**Sessions**
- Art 85: President summons; **≤6 months** between last sitting of one session and first sitting of next.
- Constitution does **not** prescribe “three sessions per year”.
- **Adjournment** → presiding officer; **prorogation** → President; **dissolution** → LS only. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/getFile/BillsTexts/LSBillTexts/Asintroduced/573LS%20As%20Int....pdf?source=legislation)

**Questions / accountability**
- Starred → oral answer → supplementaries permitted.
- Unstarred → written answer → **no supplementary**.
- Question Hour normally first hour.
- Zero Hour = parliamentary convention/device; not constitutional provision.
- No-confidence motion → **LS only**; term absent from Constitution; operationalises Art 75(3) collective responsibility. [eParliament](https://eparlib.sansad.in/faq.jsp)

**Bills / deadlock**
- Ordinary Bill → either House.
- Art 108 deadlock: rejection / disagreement over amendments / >6 months pendency in other House → President may notify joint sitting.
- Joint sitting: Speaker presides; simple majority of members present & voting.
- **No joint sitting:** Money Bill; Constitutional Amendment Bill.
- Once President has notified intention for joint sitting, later LS dissolution does **not** cause bill to lapse.

**Dissolution of LS — lapse**
- Pending in LS → lapses.
- Passed LS + pending RS → lapses.
- Pending RS, not yet passed LS → **does not lapse**.
- Passed both + awaiting President → does not lapse.
- Returned by President for reconsideration → does not lapse.
- Joint-sitting notification already issued → does not lapse.

**Money vs Financial Bills**
- **Money Bill — Art 110:** only Art 110 matters; only LS; President's recommendation; Speaker certification; RS has **14 days + recommendations only**; LS may accept/reject; no joint sitting. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- **Financial Bill-I — Art 117(1):** Art 110 matter + other matters; LS only + President recommendation; otherwise ordinary-bill procedure → RS can amend/reject; joint sitting possible.
- **Financial Bill-II — Art 117(3):** no Art 110 matter, but entails expenditure from CFI; either House; President recommendation needed **before consideration**, not necessarily introduction; RS equal power.
- **All Money Bills are financial bills; all financial bills are not Money Bills.**

**Budget**
- **Art 112:** President causes Annual Financial Statement to be laid before both Houses.
- **Art 113:** Demands for Grants → voted **only by LS**; demand requires President's recommendation.
- Charged expenditure → discussed, not voted.
- Appropriation Bill → withdrawal from CFI.
- Vote on Account = advance grant for part of financial year.

**Committees**
- PAC = **22: 15 LS + 7 RS**; appropriation/finance accounts + CAG reports.
- Estimates Committee = **30 LS only**.
- COPU = **22: 15 LS + 7 RS**.
- Financial-committee tenure = one year. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/ls/committee/introduction)
- PAC report → presented to LS; copy laid in RS. [eParliament](https://eparlib.sansad.in/bitstream/123456789/4658/1/pac_3_40_1965.pdf)
- Committee on Subordinate Legislation → delegated rules/regulations/orders within delegated authority.
- Ethics Committee: originally ad hoc; **complaint may originate from any person, but an outsider's complaint must be forwarded by an LS member**; sub-judice matters excluded under procedure. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/ls/committee/other-parliamentary-standing-committees/51-ethics-)
- Committee on Welfare of SC/ST = **30: 20 LS + 10 RS**; Chairperson appointed by Speaker; minister ineligible; term ≤1 year. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/ls/committee/other-parliamentary-standing-committees/2-welfare%20of%20scheduled%20castes%20and%20scheduled%20tribes-nameH%3D%E0%A4%85%E0%A4%A8%E0%A5%81%E0%A4%B8%E0%A5%82%E0%A4%9A%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%A4%20%E0%A4%9C%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%AF%E0%A5%8B%E0%A4%82%20%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%A5%E0%A4%BE%20%E0%A4%85%E0%A4%A8%E0%A5%81%E0%A4%B8%E0%A5%82%E0%A4%9A%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%A4%20%E0%A4%9C%E0%A4%A8%E0%A4%9C%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%AF%E0%A5%8B%E0%A4%82%20%E0%A4%95%E0%A5%87%20%E0%A4%95%E0%A4%B2%E0%A5%8D%E0%A4%AF%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%A3%20%E0%A4%B8%E0%A4%82%E0%A4%AC%E0%A4%82%E0%A4%A7%E0%A5%80%20%E0%A4%B8%E0%A4%AE%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%BF)

---

### B. Judiciary & Legal Services

**Supreme Court — anchor**
- **Arts 124–147**.
- Number of SC judges → Parliament may alter by law; do not freeze current strength.
- Judge qualification: citizen + 5 years HC judge **or** 10 years HC advocate **or** distinguished jurist in President's opinion; retirement **65**.
- Appointment formally by President; operative appointment system = judicially evolved **collegium**. SC collegium for SC appointments = CJI + four senior-most puisne judges; 99th Amendment/NJAC system was invalidated in 2015. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/43070.pdf)

**Removal**
- Ground: **proved misbehaviour or incapacity**.
- President removes after address by **each House in same session**.
- Each House: majority of total membership **+ ≥2/3 present & voting**.
- Judges (Inquiry) Act, 1968:
  - notice → ≥100 LS members / ≥50 RS members;
  - Speaker/Chairman may admit or refuse;
  - if admitted → 3-member inquiry committee: SC CJI/judge + HC Chief Justice + distinguished jurist. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/1539/2/A1968-51.pdf)

**Jurisdiction / powers**
- **Art 129:** court of record + contempt power.
- **Art 131:** exclusive original federal jurisdiction for specified Union–State / inter-State legal-right disputes.
- **Arts 132–134:** appellate jurisdiction.
- **Art 136:** Special Leave to Appeal.
- **Art 137:** review own judgments/orders.
- **Art 141:** law declared by SC binding on all courts.
- **Art 142:** orders/decrees necessary for **complete justice**.
- **Art 143:** advisory jurisdiction. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- SC does **not** have exclusive jurisdiction over constitutional validity of Central laws: former Art 131A was omitted; High Courts also exercise judicial review. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

**Judicial review**
- Constitutional power; linked inter alia to Arts 13, 32, 136, 226/227.
- Judicial review = constitutionality/legality review; **not** judicial substitution of policy wisdom.
- Judicial review forms part of basic structure.

**Retired SC judges**
- Art 128: CJI may request attendance of qualifying former judge **with previous consent of President + consent of judge**; while sitting gets powers/privileges but is not otherwise deemed a sitting SC judge.

**Contempt**
- SC: Art 129; HC: Art 215.
- Constitution confers contempt power but does **not** define civil/criminal contempt.
- Definitions/procedure → **Contempt of Courts Act, 1971**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/15384/1/the_contempt_of_courts_act%2C_1971.pdf)

**Legal aid**
- **Art 39A** → equal justice + free legal aid.
- Legal Services Authorities Act, **1987** → NALSA/SLSAs/DLSAs/Taluk committees; came into force in 1995.
- Section 12 eligibility includes SC/ST; trafficking/begar victims; women/children; persons with disability; specified disaster/violence victims; industrial workmen; persons in custody; income-threshold category.
- Income ceilings = rule/notification dependent → do not universalise one figure.
- CJI = **Patron-in-Chief, NALSA**; senior-most SC judge = Executive Chairman; SCLSC has its own SC-judge Chairman. [National Legal Services Authority](https://nalsa.gov.in/introduction/)

**Lok Adalat**
- Statutory basis → Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987.
- Covers pending cases + pre-litigation disputes.
- No jurisdiction over **non-compoundable offences**.
- Ordinary Lok Adalat = settlement/compromise; cannot impose adjudicated settlement.
- Award = deemed civil-court decree; final/binding; no appeal.
- **Permanent Lok Adalat:** public-utility services; pre-litigation; if conciliation fails it may decide dispute on merits within statutory limits. [National Legal Services Authority](https://nalsa.gov.in/permanent-lok-adalat/?locale=bg)

**Gram Nyayalayas**
- Gram Nyayalayas Act, **2008**; operative from 2 Oct 2009.
- Civil + criminal jurisdiction; local/doorstep justice; conciliatory approach.
- Establishment by State Governments in consultation with High Courts.
- **Act does not make establishment mandatory everywhere.** [Department of Justice](https://www.doj.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/11/50108dbd8fa50433201b0356e8286353.pdf)

**Tribunals**
- **Part XIV-A; 42nd Amendment.**
- **Art 323A:** administrative tribunals; Parliament alone; public-service/service-condition disputes.
- **Art 323B:** tribunals for enumerated subjects; Parliament or competent State Legislature.
- NGT → **National Green Tribunal Act, 2010**, not 2002; not bound by CPC procedure, guided by natural justice. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

---

### C. Union Executive

**President**
- Arts **52–62**; Art 53 executive power formally vested in President.
- Parliamentary executive: President acts on aid/advice of CoM under Art 74; advice may be returned once for reconsideration, but reconsidered advice is binding.

**Election**
- Art 54 electoral college:
  - elected MPs of **both Houses**;
  - elected MLAs of States;
  - for Arts 54–55, “State” includes **Delhi + Puducherry**.
- Nominated MPs/MLAs excluded.
- PR-STV + secret ballot.
- SC finally decides President/VP election disputes.
- Vacancy in electoral college ≠ ground to invalidate election.
- If election later declared void → official acts already done remain valid. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

**Impeachment**
- Art 61; charge = violation of Constitution.
- Either House may initiate.
- ≥14-day notice + signatures of ≥1/4 total membership of initiating House.
- Resolution in each House → **≥2/3 of total membership**.

**Ordinance — Art 123**
- Available except when **both Houses are in session** → one or both Houses may be out of session.
- Same force/effect as Parliamentary Act, within Parliament's legislative competence.
- Laid before both Houses; ceases 6 weeks after reassembly unless approved/replaced; earlier withdrawal/disapproval possible.
- Can amend/repeal ordinary Central legislation; can have retrospective operation.
- Cannot amend Constitution; cannot validly abridge FR beyond ordinary legislative competence.
- Ordinance power ≠ parallel permanent legislature. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

**Pardon — Art 72**
- President: court-martial cases; Union-law offences; death sentences.
- Forms: pardon / reprieve / respite / remission / suspension / commutation.
- Exercise subject to **limited judicial review**; constitutional President acts on Union CoM advice. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

**PM / Council of Ministers / Cabinet**
- PM appointed by President; other ministers appointed by President on PM advice.
- Non-MP may be minister for maximum **6 consecutive months**.
- Art 75(1A): PM + all ministers ≤ **15% of LS strength**.
- Art 75(3): CoM collectively responsible to **Lok Sabha**, not both Houses.
- Constitution does **not** classify ministers into four ranks.
- **Cabinet ≠ entire CoM:** constitutional expression in Art 352 = PM + Ministers of Cabinet rank. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- Art 78 → PM's information/communication duties towards President.

**Cabinet Secretariat / business rules**
- Functions directly under PM.
- Secretarial assistance to Cabinet + Cabinet Committees.
- Administers **Allocation of Business Rules, 1961** + **Transaction of Business Rules, 1961**, framed under Art 77(3).
- Inter-ministerial coordination; Cabinet Secretary = administrative head + ex-officio Chair, Civil Services Board.
- Cabinet Committee names/composition = dynamic → do not freeze. [Cabinet Secretariat](https://cabsec.gov.in/aboutus/functions/)

**Attorney General**
- **Art 76:** appointed by President; qualification = qualified for appointment as SC judge.
- Holds office during President's pleasure; remuneration determined by President.
- Art 88: may speak/participate in either House, joint sitting and committees of which named member; **no vote merely by virtue of Art 88**.
- **Attorney General = constitutional; Solicitor General ≠ constitutional office.** [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer
- **Vice-President:** Arts 63–71.
  - Electoral college = **members of both Houses** → elected + nominated MPs; State legislatures excluded.
  - PR-STV + secret ballot.
  - Ex-officio Chairman RS; **not member** of RS.
  - Removal initiated only in RS → majority of all **then members**; agreed to by LS; 14-day notice; Constitution specifies no impeachment ground.
  - Election disputes → SC; acts before election declared void remain valid. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- **UP parliamentary representation (current 2026):** 80 LS + 31 RS; retain as replaceable factual layer because future delimitation can alter representation. [Election Commission of India](https://www.eci.gov.in/term-of-the-houses/)
- **PMO trap:** PMO ≠ Cabinet Secretariat; Cabinet Secretary does not head PMO. For `UPPCS-2019-GS1-Q030`, retain exam-specific chronology that the Prime Minister's Secretariat was redesignated PMO in 1977; do not infer that the institution itself originated only then.
- **Direct-Article recall:** 61 President impeachment; 67 VP; 90 Deputy Chairman RS; 94 Speaker/Deputy Speaker LS; 108 joint sitting; 110 Money Bill; 323A administrative tribunals.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- Parliament = **President + RS + LS**; President ≠ member of either House.
- RS nominated fields: **social service ≠ social sciences**.
- Speaker/Deputy Speaker removal = **effective majority**, not absolute majority.
- Deputy Chairman RS removal = **effective majority**, not absolute majority.
- VP = RS Chairman **but not RS member**.
- President election: nominated MPs **excluded**; VP election: nominated MPs **included**.
- Money Bill: RS **recommends**, cannot amend/reject.
- Money Bill ≠ Financial Bill-I ≠ Financial Bill-II.
- Joint sitting: ordinary/Financial Bill deadlock possible; **not Money/Constitution Amendment Bills**.
- LS dissolution ≠ prorogation; prorogation does not make pending bills lapse.
- Joint-sitting notification already issued → bill does **not** lapse on LS dissolution.
- Demand for Grant → **LS only**.
- Question Hour ≠ Zero Hour; starred ≠ unstarred.
- Ethics complaint: outsider may complain **through an LS member**.
- PAC: **15 LS + 7 RS**, not 15 RS.
- SC original federal jurisdiction ≠ exclusive power to test constitutional validity of all Central laws.
- Constitution grants contempt power ≠ Constitution defines civil/criminal contempt.
- Ordinary Lok Adalat **settles** ≠ Permanent Lok Adalat may **decide on merits** after failed conciliation.
- Gram Nyayalaya Act ≠ mandatory establishment in every eligible location.
- Art 323A: Parliament only ≠ Art 323B: Parliament/State legislature according to competence.
- CoM collectively responsible to **LS**, not Parliament/both Houses.
- Cabinet ≠ Council of Ministers.
- Attorney General ≠ Solicitor General.
- Art 123 ordinance can amend ordinary Act ≠ can amend Constitution.
- Pardon power subject to limited judicial review; President ≠ personal/discretionary decision-maker.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT
- Base: RS nominees — “literature, science, art, **social sciences**”  
  → **literature, science, art, social service**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- Base: Deputy Chairman RS removed by “absolute majority”  
  → majority of **all then members** = effective majority. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- Base comparison: Cabinet contains Cabinet Ministers + Ministers of State etc.  
  → **Cabinet = PM + Ministers of Cabinet rank; Council of Ministers is wider.** [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- Base: CJI described as “patron and chief” of NALSA/SCLSC  
  → CJI = **Patron-in-Chief, NALSA**; senior-most SC judge = Executive Chairman; SCLSC separately chaired by an SC judge. [National Legal Services Authority](https://nalsa.gov.in/introduction/)
- Base: Permanent Lok Adalat pecuniary jurisdiction = ₹10 lakh  
  → obsolete. Current official NALSA material states **₹1 crore**; because ceiling is notification-dependent, keep it as a dynamic fact rather than frozen core. [Rajasthan State Legal Services Authority](https://rajasthan.nalsa.gov.in/acts/)
- Base: “NGT Act 2002”  
  → **National Green Tribunal Act, 2010**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/indiacode/handle/123456789/2025?view_type=browse)

### VERIFY
- **None blocking.**
- Canonical review-state/malformed PYQ records listed in §2 must not be used to infer answer wording beyond the recoverable concept.

### OMIT
- Current Speaker/Deputy Speaker/VP/CJI/judge names.
- Current Cabinet Committee count/composition/chairpersons.
- Current SC sanctioned strength as durable content.
- Dynamic legal-aid income ceilings and PLA pecuniary ceiling from the frozen core.
- `UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q049` first e-Lok Adalat pandemic trivia.
- `UPPCS-2024-GS1-Q144` obituary/personality-current-affairs layer.
- Long PIL/judicial-activism narratives not demanded by this taxonomy node.

## 8. Supplement Decision
`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

- Taxonomy + canonical PYQs + base notes + targeted Laxmikanth gap-fill + official constitutional/statutory verification answer all identified demands.
- Ghatna Chakra/test-series supplementation is not necessary for the final note.

### Demand synthesis

- **Recurring concepts:** presiding officers > budget/financial control > committees > Money/Financial Bills > lapse/joint sitting > House asymmetry > devices/sessions.
- **Recurring traps:** `may ≠ shall`; `recommend ≠ amend`; effective majority ≠ absolute majority; `2/3 present & voting ≠ 2/3 total membership`; President is part of Parliament but not either House; Chairman of RS ≠ member, Deputy Chairman = member.
- **Qualifier patterns:** *only*, *not*, *final*, *present and voting*, *all the then members*, *within 14 days*, *more than six months*, *first session*.
- **UPSC:** increasingly procedural/qualifier-heavy; 2022–26 reinforces Deputy Speaker, Ethics Committee, Speaker removal, lapse, Question Hour and committee composition.
- **UPPCS:** more direct Article-number, seat-number, committee-size, state-representation and office-holder chronology recall.
- **Recent reinforcement:** UPSC 2024–26 + UPPCS 2025 strongly reinforce presiding officers, joint sitting, committees and precise majority formulae.

## 3. Base-Note Coverage

- **already strong:** Parliament architecture; LS/RS composition; Speaker/Deputy Speaker; sessions; Question/Zero Hour; motions; bills; Money/Financial Bills; lapse/joint sitting; Budget; financial committees. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **partial:** LoP recognition; exact majority for RS Deputy Chairman; Article 101(4); Finance Bill taxonomy; judicial review of Money-Bill certification; committee-rule fine print.
- **missing:** 2026 SC/ST Welfare Committee PYQ facts; recent starred/unstarred-question reinforcement; UPPCS-specific UP representation/RS seat-match facts.
- **redundant:** long catalogue of parliamentary functions/forums/groups; detailed privilege prose; procedural minutiae without PYQ support.
- **outdated/suspect:** dynamic House/current-office facts; “10% statutory LoP rule”; “Money-Bill certification immune from judicial review”; “Deputy Chairman removal = absolute majority”; blanket equation of Finance Bill with Article 110.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### Constitutional map

| Anchor | Payload |
|---|---|
| **Art. 79** | Parliament = **President + Rajya Sabha + Lok Sabha**. President is part of Parliament, **not member of either House**. |
| **Art. 80** | RS constitutional maximum **250** = 12 nominated + ≤238 States/UT representatives. Nominated fields: literature, science, art, social service. State representatives: elected MLAs; PR–STV. Fourth Schedule → seat allocation. |
| **Art. 81** | LS constitutional maximum under present Art. 81 formula = **550**: ≤530 States + ≤20 UTs. |
| **Art. 83** | RS = continuing House; not dissolved; ≈⅓ retire every second year. Member term = **6 years** under RPA 1951. LS normal term = 5 years unless sooner dissolved. |
| **Art. 85** | President summons Houses; **<6 months** between last sitting of one session and first sitting of next. Constitution does **not** prescribe 3 sessions/year or minimum sitting-days. |
| **Arts. 89–96** | RS/LS presiding officers. |
| **Art. 98** | Separate secretarial staff for each House; common posts permitted; Parliament may regulate recruitment/service conditions by law. |
| **Art. 100** | Quorum = **1/10 of total membership** unless Parliament provides otherwise. |
| **Art. 101(4)** | 60 days' absence from all meetings without House permission → House **may declare seat vacant**; periods of prorogation or adjournment >4 consecutive days excluded. |

Constitutional composition/session/secretariat/vacancy rules: [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) RPA 1951 fixes the ordinary RS-member term at six years. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/2096/5/a1951-43.pdf)

### Presiding officers

| Office | High-yield rule |
|---|---|
| **RS Chairman** | Vice-President = ex-officio Chairman; **not an RS member**. |
| **RS Deputy Chairman** | Elected **from among RS members**; removal by majority of **all the then members** (= effective majority); 14-day notice. |
| **LS Speaker / Deputy Speaker** | Chosen by LS from among its members. Removal = majority of **all the then members**; 14-day notice. |
| **Speaker after dissolution** | Continues until immediately before first meeting of new LS. |
| **Speaker during own removal motion** | Cannot preside; may speak/take part; may vote in first instance—not casting vote on equality. |
| **Deputy Speaker election** | Lok Sabha Rule 8: date fixed by **Speaker**; no constitutional/rule mandate that office belong to opposition/ruling party; motion moved by the member in whose name it stands, not by Speaker. |

The Constitution uses “majority of all the then members” for removal of Deputy Chairman/Speaker/Deputy Speaker. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) Lok Sabha Rule 8 governs Deputy-Speaker election. [eParliament](https://eparlib.sansad.in/bitstream/123456789/2495198/1/Rules_of_Procedures_E_2019_16th_edition.pdf)

### Lok Sabha vs Rajya Sabha

| Matter | Position |
|---|---|
| Ordinary Bill | Broadly equal powers |
| Constitutional Amendment Bill | Equal; separately passed; **no joint sitting** |
| Money Bill | LS dominant; RS recommendations only |
| Demands for Grants | **LS votes**; RS may discuss |
| No-confidence | **LS only** |
| Art. 249 | RS: ≥**2/3 present & voting** → Parliament may legislate State List in national interest; resolution ≤1 year, renewable |
| Art. 312 | RS: ≥**2/3 present & voting** → Parliament may create new All-India Service(s) by law |

Art. 249 and Art. 312 both use **two-thirds of members present and voting**, not total membership. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

### Legislative procedure: bill types

| Type | Introduction | RS power | Joint sitting |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Ordinary Bill** | Either House | amend/reject | possible |
| **Money Bill — Art. 110** | **LS only**; President's recommendation | recommend only; return within **14 days** | **No** |
| **Financial Bill-I — Art. 117(1)** | **LS only**; President's recommendation | amend/reject like ordinary Bill | possible |
| **Financial Bill-II — Art. 117(3)** | Either House | ordinary-bill powers | possible; President's recommendation required before passage/consideration |
| **Constitution Amendment Bill** | Either House | equal | **No** |

**Money Bill = contains only Art. 110(1)(a)–(g) matters:** taxation; Union borrowing/guarantee; CFI/Contingency Fund custody/withdrawal; appropriation from CFI; charged expenditure; CFI/Public Account receipts/custody/issue and audit; incidental matters. RS may recommend; LS may accept/reject recommendations; if RS does not return within 14 days → deemed passed in LS form. Speaker certifies it as Money Bill. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

**Certification trap:** Art. 110(3) calls Speaker's decision “final”, but Supreme Court precedent does **not** support blanket immunity from judicial review where constitutional infirmity is alleged. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2017/9680/9680_2017_1_1501_18247_Judgement_13-Nov-2019.pdf)

### Lapse + joint sitting

| Situation on LS dissolution | Result |
|---|---|
| Bill pending in LS | **Lapses** |
| Bill passed LS, pending RS | **Lapses** |
| Bill pending RS, not yet passed LS | **Does not lapse** |
| Bill passed both Houses, pending President | **Does not lapse** |
| President returned Bill for reconsideration | **Does not lapse** |
| President already notified intention for joint sitting | **Does not lapse** |
| Prorogation | **No Bill lapses** |

**Art. 108 joint-sitting trigger:** other House rejects Bill / final disagreement on amendments / **more than 6 months** elapse without passage. President **may** summon; Speaker ordinarily presides; decision by simple majority of members present and voting. No joint sitting for Money Bill or Constitutional Amendment Bill. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/c9fe9c9b6840524844316f74bb1c556c.pdf)

### Sessions / devices

- **Adjournment:** ends a sitting; by presiding officer.
- **Adjournment sine die:** ends sitting(s) without naming next date.
- **Prorogation:** President terminates a session; House may be prorogued even while technically in session; does **not** dissolve House.
- **Dissolution:** ends life of LS; RS cannot be dissolved.
- **Question Hour:** starred → oral answer + supplementary possible; unstarred → written answer + **no supplementary**. [eParliament](https://eparlib.sansad.in/faq.jsp)
- **Zero Hour:** follows Question Hour/laying of papers and precedes listed business; not formally recognised in Rules. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/ls/faq)
- **Adjournment Motion:** definite matter of urgent public importance; LS device; carries element of censure.
- **No-confidence Motion:** LS only; Constitution does not expressly mention the device; tests collective responsibility.
- **Private Member:** MP who is **not a Minister**; elected/nominated status irrelevant.
- **President's special address — Art. 87:** first session after each LS general election + first session each year; informs Parliament of causes of summons.

### Budget / financial procedure

- **Art. 112 AFS:** President causes Annual Financial Statement to be laid before **both Houses**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Expenditure **charged on CFI** → discussed but **not voted**.
- Other expenditure → Demands for Grants → **Lok Sabha only**; demand only on President's recommendation. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Appropriation Bill/Act — Art. 114:** legal authority for withdrawal from CFI.
- **Art. 115:** supplementary/additional/excess grants.
- **Art. 116:** Vote on Account = advance grant for part of FY pending completion of normal grant/appropriation procedure; Vote of Credit = unexpected/indefinite demand; Exceptional Grant = outside current service. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/08/7af1daa22d65f9d04c00ae9b9aa5a799.pdf)
- **Public Account of India ≠ CFI:** withdrawals from Public Account do not require parliamentary appropriation.
- **Vote on Account ≠ Interim Budget:** former is a constitutionally recognised grant mechanism; “interim budget” is not a distinct constitutional category.
- Core sequence: **AFS → general discussion → DRSC scrutiny → Demands for Grants/ cut motions (LS) → Appropriation Bill → taxation/Finance Bill**.

### Parliamentary committees

| Committee | Composition / core function |
|---|---|
| **PAC** | ≤22 = **15 LS + 7 RS**; elected annually PR–STV; Minister ineligible; Chair appointed by Speaker from LS members; convention since 1967: Opposition member. Examines appropriation/finance accounts + CAG reports. Report presented to LS / laid in RS. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/getFile/LSSCOMMITTEE/Public%20Accounts/Introduction/Introduction_of_PAC_Home_Page_2022-2023.pdf?source=loksabhadocs) |
| **Estimates Committee** | **30, all LS**; largest parliamentary committee; economy/efficiency/alternative policy within policy underlying estimates. |
| **Committee on Public Undertakings** | **22 = 15 LS + 7 RS**; Minister ineligible; accounts/reports/working of public undertakings. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/ls/committee/financial-committees/27-Public%20Undertakings%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20%20-nameH%3D%E0%A4%B8%E0%A4%B0%E0%A4%95%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%B0%E0%A5%80%20%E0%A4%89%E0%A4%AA%E0%A4%95%E0%A5%8D%E0%A4%B0%E0%A4%AE%E0%A5%8B%E0%A4%82%20%E0%A4%B8%E0%A4%82%E0%A4%AC%E0%A4%82%E0%A4%98%E0%A5%80%20%E0%A4%B8%E0%A4%AE%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%BF) |
| **DRSCs** | **24**; normally 31 each = 21 LS + 10 RS; scrutinise Demands for Grants, Bills referred, annual reports, long-term policy documents; Ministers excluded. |
| **Committee on Subordinate Legislation** | Scrutinises whether delegated rule/regulation/by-law powers are properly exercised within delegation. |
| **Lok Sabha Ethics Committee** | Complaint may originate from any person or member; complaint by a non-member must be forwarded by an MP; examines unethical conduct. “Only an MP can make a complaint” = false. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/getFile/LSSCOMMITTEE/Ethics/Introduction/Introduction%20Ethics%20Committee%20to%20be%20uploaded%20on%20website.pdf?source=loksabhadocs) |
| **SC/ST Welfare Committee** | **30 = 20 LS + 10 RS**; Chair appointed by **Speaker** from members; **no Minister eligible**; term ≤1 year. [Digital Sansad](https://sansad.in/ls/committee/other-parliamentary-standing-committees/2-welfare%20of%20scheduled%20castes%20and%20scheduled%20tribes-nameH%3D%E0%A4%85%E0%A4%A8%E0%A5%81%E0%A4%B8%E0%A5%82%E0%A4%9A%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%A4%20%E0%A4%9C%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%AF%E0%A5%8B%E0%A4%82%20%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%A5%E0%A4%BE%20%E0%A4%85%E0%A4%A8%E0%A5%81%E0%A4%B8%E0%A5%82%E0%A4%9A%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%A4%20%E0%A4%9C%E0%A4%A8%E0%A4%9C%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%AF%E0%A5%8B%E0%A4%82%20%E0%A4%95%E0%A5%87%20%E0%A4%95%E0%A4%B2%E0%A5%8D%E0%A4%AF%E0%A4%BE%E0%A4%A3%20%E0%A4%B8%E0%A4%82%E0%A4%AC%E0%A4%82%E0%A4%A7%E0%A5%80%20%E0%A4%B8%E0%A4%AE%E0%A4%BF%E0%A4%A4%E0%A4%BF) |

### Other direct PYQ anchors

- **Leader of Opposition:** statutory recognition under Salary and Allowances of Leaders of Opposition in Parliament Act, 1977; definition = leader of opposition party having greatest numerical strength and recognised by Speaker/Chairman. **Act itself contains no 10% threshold.** First formally recognised LoP in Lok Sabha: **1969**. [Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs](https://mpa.gov.in/sites/default/files/actopp.pdf)
- **Office of profit:** Art. 102(1)(a) disqualification anchor; exemptions via **Parliament (Prevention of Disqualification) Act, 1959**.
- **Parliament Secretariat:** Art. 98 → separate staff, common posts permitted, service conditions regulable by Parliament. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

- **UP representation:** Lok Sabha **80**; Rajya Sabha **31** — retain as replaceable allocation fact; re-check after future delimitation/reallocation.
- **RS seat-match facts directly reinforced:** Rajasthan 10; Gujarat 11; Karnataka 12; Punjab 7; Maharashtra 19; Bihar 16; Tamil Nadu 18.
- **LS constitutional UT maximum:** **20**.
- **Art.-provision recall:** 79 Parliament; 80 RS; 81 LS; 85 sessions; 89 Chairman/Deputy Chairman; 90 Deputy-Chairman removal; 93 Speaker/Deputy Speaker; 94 removal; 98 secretariat; 101 vacancy; 108 joint sitting; 109 Money-Bill procedure; 110 Money-Bill definition; 112 AFS; 113 grants; 114 appropriation; 116 special grants; 117 financial bills; 249 State List; 312 AIS.
- **Historical/name recall demanded:** first woman Lok Sabha Speaker = **Meira Kumar**; Speaker matching: 11th—P.A. Sangma; 12th—G.M.C. Balayogi; 14th—Somnath Chatterjee; 17th—Om Birla.
- **Absence trap:** “60 days” is correct duration, but constitutional consequence is **House may declare seat vacant**, not automatic “disqualification”. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist

- President **part of Parliament** ≠ member of either House.
- RS Chairman **not member** ≠ Deputy Chairman **is RS member**.
- Speaker/Deputy Speaker removal = **effective majority** ≠ absolute majority.
- Art. 249/312 = **2/3 present & voting** ≠ 2/3 total membership.
- Money Bill **recommendations** by RS ≠ amendment/rejection power.
- Money Bill **14 days** ≠ ordinary-Bill time limit.
- Money Bill ≠ every Financial/Finance Bill.
- Financial Bill-I only LS ≠ Money-Bill treatment after introduction.
- Joint sitting **may** be summoned ≠ automatically/necessarily held.
- Art. **108** joint sitting ≠ Art. 109.
- Joint sitting: ordinary/Financial Bills possible ≠ Money/Constitution Amendment Bill.
- Prorogation ≠ dissolution; prorogation does **not** lapse Bills.
- Bill pending RS alone ≠ Bill passed LS and pending RS on dissolution.
- Charged expenditure: discuss **yes**, vote **no**.
- AFS before both Houses ≠ Demands for Grants voted by both.
- Vote on Account ≠ Interim Budget.
- Starred = oral + supplementary ≠ unstarred = written, no supplementary.
- Zero Hour ≠ formal rule-book device.
- Private member = non-Minister ≠ nominated MP.
- PAC = **15 LS + 7 RS** ≠ reverse.
- Estimates Committee = **30 LS only**.
- SC/ST Welfare Committee = **20 LS + 10 RS**, Speaker-appointed Chair, no Minister.
- LoP 1977 Act ≠ statutory 10% requirement.
- 60-day absence → House **may declare seat vacant** ≠ automatic disqualification.
- Speaker's Money-Bill certificate “final” ≠ absolutely immune from judicial review.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT

| Base/source fact | Corrected fact | Authority / reason |
|---|---|---|
| RS “changes its entire membership every six years” | Continuing House; ≈⅓ retire every 2 years; whole House never expires together | Art. 83 + RPA 1951 |
| RS Deputy Chairman removed by “absolute majority” | Majority of **all the then members** = effective majority | Art. 90 [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) |
| Speaker/Deputy Speaker removal loosely treated as absolute majority | Effective majority | Art. 94 [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) |
| Money-Bill certification “not subject to judicial review” | “Final” under Art. 110(3), but narrow constitutional judicial review recognised | SC [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2017/9680/9680_2017_1_1501_18247_Judgement_13-Nov-2019.pdf) |
| “Money Bills are Appropriation Bill and Finance Bill” | Art. 110 test controls; Appropriation Bill is Money Bill; Finance/Financial Bill category must be tested separately | Arts. 110, 117 |
| “Finance Bill = Art. 110” | Art. 110 defines **Money Bill**; Art. 117 governs other Financial Bills | Constitution |
| LoP requires 10% as statutory rule | 1977 Act contains **no 10% threshold** in its definition | Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs Act text [Ministry of Parliamentary Affairs](https://mpa.gov.in/sites/default/files/actopp.pdf) |
| `Office of Profit (Prevention of Disqualification) Act` | **Parliament (Prevention of Disqualification) Act, 1959** | statutory title |
| Joint-sitting deadlock phrased as “pending for 6 months” | Art. 108: **more than six months elapse** | Constitution |
| `UPPCS-2018-GS1-Q023`: “MP can be disqualified after 60 days” | 60-day threshold correct; House **may declare seat vacant** | Art. 101(4) [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) |
| `UPPCS-2025-GS1-Q011` canonical key = **A (Only 2)** | Question as stored: statement 1 true; statement 2 false → **D (Only 1)**. Canonical frozen key conflicts with Art. 312 and needs corpus remediation. | Art. 312: 2/3 **present & voting**, not total membership [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/ca7ce5c746fa7480804bbdeb6cb704f0.pdf) |

### VERIFY

- **Canonical-record integrity only; factual proposition recoverable independently:**
  - `UPSC-CSE-2024-GS1-Q068` — `EDITABLE_REVIEW / EMPTY_STATEMENT`; do not use its reconstructed statements.
  - `UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q132` — `TABLE_STRUCTURE_UNCLEAR`.
  - `UPPCS-2021-GS1-Q128` — `TABLE_STRUCTURE_UNCLEAR`.
  - `UPPCS-2023-GS1-Q009` — `OPTION_BOUNDARY_UNCLEAR`.
- No unresolved substantive Parliament fact remains after constitutional/official verification.

### OMIT

- Current Speaker/Deputy-Speaker vacancy debate; current Ethics Committee cases.
- Current House-strength/current office-holder trivia except direct, replaceable UPPCS allocation facts.
- Dynamic Budget allocations/scheme figures.
- Anti-defection detail → Schedules/Provision Mapping.
- Delimitation procedure → Elections & Representation.
- Parliamentary-system theory → Constitutional Foundations.
- Detailed privileges catalogue, parliamentary forums/groups, salaries, staff-service minutiae.
- Blanket claim that defeat of **any** government Bill automatically equals loss of confidence.
- Unverified/low-value rule that a second no-confidence motion cannot be introduced in same session.
- “75-day Finance Bill” detail from base note: omit from permanent core unless separately required.

## 8. Supplement Decision

`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

Current taxonomy + canonical PYQs + base notes + Laxmikanth/Ghatna Chakra + Constitution/Sansad/SC verification cover all identified Parliament PYQ demands. Test-series/topper PDFs would add volume, not a material missing demand.

# Polity & Governance → Union Institutions & Judiciary → Judiciary & Legal Services — Source Pack v01

**Status:** PASS_WITH_CORRECTIONS  
**taxonomy_id:** `prelims-polity-and-governance-union-institutions-and-judiciary-judiciary-and-legal-services`

## 1. Scope
- **Includes:** Supreme Court jurisdiction/powers; judges—appointment, qualifications, tenure, removal; judicial independence; judicial review; contempt; legal services/NALSA; Lok Adalats; Gram Nyayalayas; tribunals; compact PIL/legal-profession linkage. Taxonomy specifically identifies Legal Services/ADR, SC jurisdiction, judges’ appointment/removal and judicial review as recurring clusters. PRE-POL__TAXONOMY_GATEWAY
- **Excludes:** full writ taxonomy → Fundamental Rights & Writs; basic-structure doctrine generally → Amendments & Basic Structure; environmental merits of NGT cases; detailed subordinate-court hierarchy; Family/Commercial Courts; judiciary schemes/portals; current judges/office-holders; transient court statistics.
- **UPSC depth:** statute vs Constitution; Judges (Inquiry) Act vs Art. 124; Contempt of Courts Act vs Arts. 129/215; legal-aid eligibility; qualifier-heavy jurisdiction/removal statements.
- **UPPCS depth:** Article/Part mapping; direct institutional/personnel facts; PIL/access-to-justice; legal-services factual recall; occasional one-off judiciary/current-personality questions.

### Demand synthesis
- **Recurring concepts:** Art. 131 original jurisdiction; Art. 142; guardian/custodian role; appointment/removal safeguards; judicial review; NALSA/legal-aid eligibility; Gram Nyayalayas; contempt; constitutional location of tribunals.
- **Recurring traps:** original ≠ appellate; exclusive original Art.131 ≠ exclusive power to test Central laws; judicial review ≠ review petition; constitutional removal majority ≠ statutory inquiry procedure; ordinary Lok Adalat ≠ Permanent Lok Adalat.
- **Qualifier patterns:** *exclusive*, *only*, *cannot*, *all*, *constitutional vs statutory*, *may vs shall*, *pending vs pre-litigation*, *civil only vs civil + criminal*.
- **UPSC vs UPPCS:** UPSC repeatedly tests legal effect/qualifiers and Constitution–statute boundaries; UPPCS more often tests Article/Part mapping, personalities, one-step institutional facts and current/history hooks.
- **Recent reinforcement:** UPSC—Contempt (2022); UPPCS—tribunal + judge resignation (2023), PIL/access-to-justice (2025).

## 3. Base-Note Coverage
- **already strong:** SC–HC comparison; Arts. 124–147; qualifications/tenure; collegium/NJAC; removal; Art. 131/136/142/143; contempt; judicial review/PIL; ADR; Lok Adalat; Gram Nyayalaya; tribunals.
- **partial:** current legal-aid eligibility/ceilings; precise NALSA office structure; ordinary Lok Adalat vs Permanent Lok Adalat adjudicatory distinction; statutory vs constitutional tribunal distinction.
- **missing/high-value addition:** omitted Art. 131A trap; Gram Nyayalaya establishment is **not mandatory**; Advocates Act anchor; current Permanent Lok Adalat ceiling.
- **redundant:** long judicial-activism critiques; extended case catalogues; judiciary-scheme/portal inventory; infrastructure/pending-case discussion.
- **outdated/suspect:** Second Judges Case year; Permanent Lok Adalat ₹10-lakh ceiling; NALSA leadership formulation; NGT Act year; overly broad “SC superintendence over all courts/tribunals”; incomplete Art.128 retired-judge conditions.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Constitutional map

| Anchor | Payload |
|---|---|
| **Part V, Ch. IV — Arts. 124–147** | Union Judiciary / Supreme Court |
| **Art. 124** | SC establishment, judges, appointment/removal framework |
| **Art. 129** | SC = Court of Record + contempt power |
| **Art. 130** | Seat = Delhi; CJI may appoint another place **with President’s approval** |
| **Art. 131** | Exclusive original federal jurisdiction |
| **Arts. 132–134** | Constitutional/civil/criminal appellate jurisdiction |
| **Art. 136** | Special Leave to Appeal; discretionary; courts/tribunals, except Armed Forces tribunal/court exclusion |
| **Art. 137** | SC review of its own judgments/orders |
| **Art. 141** | Law declared by SC binding on all courts in India |
| **Art. 142** | Orders/decrees necessary for **complete justice** |
| **Art. 143** | Presidential reference/advisory jurisdiction |
| **Art. 215** | HC = Court of Record |
| **Arts. 226/227** | HC writ jurisdiction / superintendence |
| **Art. 39A** | Equal justice + free legal aid |
| **Art. 50** | Separation of judiciary from executive — DPSP |
| **Part XIV-A** | Tribunals — Arts. **323A, 323B** |

Official Constitution places the Union Judiciary at Arts. 124 onward and expressly identifies Arts. 129–143 by these functions. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/c9fe9c9b6840524844316f74bb1c556c.pdf)

### B. Supreme Court: composition + judges
- **Sanctioned strength:** **34 = CJI + 33 judges** since 2019; number altered by **Parliament by law**, not by executive order. `[replaceable statutory number]` [Department of Justice](https://www.doj.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/11/111f4227503f28285033dfceb01775c6.pdf)
- **Appointment:** President.
- **Qualification:** citizen of India + any one:
  - HC judge ≥ **5 years**; OR
  - HC advocate ≥ **10 years**; OR
  - distinguished jurist in President’s opinion.
- No constitutional minimum age.
- Retirement: **SC 65 ≠ HC 62**.
- SC judge resignation → **President**.
- CJI appointment by seniority = convention/practice, **not text of Art.124**.

### C. Collegium / NJAC chronology
- **First Judges Case (1981):** consultation ≠ concurrence.
- **Second Judges Case = 1993, not 1983:** judicial primacy; collegium emerges.
- **Third Judges Case (1998):** SC appointments → CJI + **4 senior-most SC judges**; plurality consultation.
- **99th Constitutional Amendment + NJAC Act, 2014** → NJAC.
- **Fourth Judges Case (2015):** 99th Amendment + NJAC Act struck down; collegium revived; judicial independence treated as basic-structure concern. The Supreme Court itself records the Second Judges Case as 1993 and Third Judges Case as 1998. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/43070.pdf)

### D. Removal of SC/HC judges

| Constitution | Judges (Inquiry) Act, 1968 |
|---|---|
| **Grounds:** proved misbehaviour or incapacity | Detailed investigation/proof procedure |
| Each House separately | Notice: **100 LS / 50 RS** members |
| Majority = **majority of total membership + ≥2/3 present & voting** | Speaker/Chairman **may admit or refuse** |
| Address must be in same session | If admitted → 3-member inquiry committee |
| President issues removal order | SC judge/CJI + HC Chief Justice + distinguished jurist |

- Constitutional expression = **removal**; “impeachment of a judge” = common shorthand, not the Constitution’s formal terminology.
- Constitution supplies grounds + parliamentary majority; **Judges (Inquiry) Act, 1968 supplies process**.
- “Proved misbehaviour/incapacity” not exhaustively defined by Constitution.
- Same constitutional removal mechanism applies to HC judges through Art. 218/read with Art.124(4). [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/indiacode/handle/123456789/1539?view_type=browse\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### E. Special judge/personnel traps
- **Acting CJI:** President appoints when CJI office vacant / CJI absent / unable to act.
- **Ad hoc SC judge:** CJI may request qualified HC judge; consultation with concerned HC Chief Justice + previous President consent.
- **Retired judge — Art.128:** CJI request + **previous consent of President** + retired judge must consent.
- Retired SC judge cannot plead/act before any court/authority in India.

### F. SC jurisdiction / power distinctions
- **Art.131 original jurisdiction:** Centre ↔ State(s); Centre + State(s) ↔ other State(s); State ↔ State; dispute must involve existence/extent of a **legal right**.
- **Not Art.131:** private citizen v Centre/State; inter-State river-water disputes where Art.262 law excludes jurisdiction; certain pre-Constitution treaty disputes; Finance Commission-linked adjustment matters.
- **Art.136 SLP:** exceptional/discretionary appellate gateway; **not ordinary appeal as of right**.
- **Art.137:** review of SC’s own judgment ≠ judicial review of legislation/executive action.
- **Art.142:** “complete justice” constitutional power; do not reduce it to powers supplied by ordinary statute. [IndiaCode by eCourtsIndia](https://indiacode.ecourtsindia.com/constitution-of-india/article/142/)
- **Art.143:** President may refer question of law/fact of public importance; advisory opinion ≠ binding judgment on President.
- **Art.141:** binding precedent ≠ Art.142 complete-justice power.
- **SC as custodian/guardian of Constitution:** flows from constitutional interpretation, FR enforcement and judicial review—not from an unchecked general supervisory jurisdiction.

### G. Judicial independence
- Security of tenure.
- Difficult removal procedure.
- Salaries/allowances protected against disadvantage after appointment except Financial Emergency; expenses charged on CFI.
- Parliamentary/State-legislative discussion of judges’ conduct restricted except removal process.
- SC administrative staff under judicial institutional control.
- Constitutional jurisdiction cannot be removed by ordinary legislation merely at legislative discretion.
- **Independence of judiciary = basic-structure component**; key NJAC elimination point.

### H. Judicial review
- **Meaning:** judicial examination of constitutionality of legislative enactments + executive action.
- Core anchors: **Arts. 13, 32, 226** + constitutional supremacy/basic-structure doctrine.
- Constitutional amendments also judicially reviewable for **basic-structure violation**.
- HC can test constitutional validity of **Central laws**.
- **Art.131A:** inserted by 42nd Amendment to give SC exclusive jurisdiction over constitutional validity of Central laws → **omitted by 43rd Amendment**; therefore no present SC-exclusive rule. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/officereport/2004/7058/7058_2004_2022-08-30.pdf)
- **Judicial review ≠ appellate review ≠ Art.137 review petition.**
- Constitution does **not** itself enumerate/define the “basic structure”.

### I. Contempt
- **Art.129:** SC Court of Record + contempt power.
- **Art.215:** same for HCs.
- Constitution grants power; **Contempt of Courts Act, 1971** defines/regulates contempt.
- **Civil contempt:** wilful disobedience of court judgment/order/writ/process or wilful breach of undertaking.
- **Criminal contempt:** publication/act that scandalises court, prejudices judicial proceedings, or obstructs administration of justice.
- Constitution **does not define** civil/criminal contempt.
- Innocent publication; fair/accurate reporting; fair criticism of judicial acts → statutory protections.
- H.N. Sanyal Committee preceded the 1971 legislation—direct UPSC trap. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/15384/1/the_contempt_of_courts_act%2C_1971.pdf)

### J. Legal services / NALSA
- **Art.39A:** equal justice + free legal aid.
- **Legal Services Authorities Act, 1987**; in force from **9 Nov 1995**.
- Purpose → free/competent legal services + Lok Adalats.
- **NALSA:** statutory apex body.
  - CJI = **Patron-in-Chief**.
  - senior-most SC judge = **Executive Chairman**.
  - Supreme Court Legal Services Committee has a **separate SC-judge Chairman**.
  - State: HC Chief Justice = Patron-in-Chief; DLSA = District Judge as ex-officio Chairman. [National Legal Services Authority](https://nalsa.gov.in/organogram/)

**Section 12 eligibility — alternatives, not cumulative conditions**
- SC/ST.
- victim of trafficking/begar.
- **woman or child**.
- person with disability.
- victim of mass disaster/ethnic violence/caste atrocity/flood/drought/earthquake/industrial disaster etc.
- industrial workman.
- person in specified custody.
- income below prescribed ceiling. [National Legal Services Authority](https://nalsa.gov.in/about-nalsa/)

**Income trap**
- Court other than SC → ceiling prescribed by relevant State.
- Supreme Court → current NALSA FAQ: **annual income < ₹5 lakh**.
- “All senior citizens”, “all OBCs”, “all transgender persons” are **not blanket all-India Section 12 categories merely by that status**; State rules/schemes may expand eligibility. [National Legal Services Authority](https://nalsa.gov.in/faqs/)
- Therefore `UPSC-CSE-2020-GS1-Q009` reflects the rule/ceiling relevant to its examination context; **do not fossilise ₹1 lakh as the current all-India ceiling**.

### K. Lok Adalat vs Permanent Lok Adalat

| Ordinary Lok Adalat | Permanent Lok Adalat |
|---|---|
| LSA Act, 1987 | Added to LSA Act framework in 2002 |
| Pending court case **or pre-litigation** matter | **Pre-litigation** public-utility dispute |
| Conciliatory/settlement forum | First conciliation; if it fails, may **decide dispute on merits** within jurisdiction |
| Cannot impose settlement where parties do not agree | Adjudicatory power after failed conciliation = key exception |
| No jurisdiction over non-compoundable offences | Same non-compoundable-offence bar |
| Award = civil-court decree; final/binding; no appeal | Award final/binding |
| No court fee; paid fee refunded on settlement | Public-utility-services jurisdiction |

- Current PLA pecuniary bar: property in dispute **exceeding ₹1 crore** is outside jurisdiction; ₹1 crore threshold substituted in 2015. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19731/1/book_for_upload.pdf)
- Ordinary Lok Adalat award = deemed civil-court decree; final and binding; no appeal. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19023/1/legal_service_authorities_act%2C_1987.pdf)

### L. Gram Nyayalayas
- **Gram Nyayalayas Act, 2008**; operational commencement **2 Oct 2009**.
- Access-to-justice/Art.39A logic; based substantially on Law Commission 114th Report.
- State Government **may establish**, after consultation with HC → **not mandatory**.
- Nyayadhikari = judicial officer; court of Judicial Magistrate First Class level.
- Seat: intermediate Panchayat HQ; **mobile**—may visit villages.
- Jurisdiction: specified **criminal + civil** matters → “civil only” = false.
- Summary criminal procedure; settlement by conciliation as far as possible.
- Not bound by ordinary Evidence Act rules; guided by natural justice/HC rules.
- Excluded territorial application: Nagaland, Arunachal Pradesh, Sikkim + specified Sixth Schedule tribal areas.
- Criminal appeal → Sessions Court; civil appeal → District Court. [DoJ Dashboard](https://dashboard.doj.gov.in/gn/introduction)

### M. Tribunals
- Original Constitution had no Part XIV-A.
- **42nd CA, 1976 → Part XIV-A**.
- **Art.323A:** administrative tribunals; recruitment/service conditions; **Parliament only**.
- **Administrative Tribunals Act, 1985 → CAT/SAT/JAT framework**.
- CAT = original jurisdiction over covered public-service recruitment/service matters; procedure guided by natural justice rather than strict CPC.
- **Art.323B:** tribunals for specified subjects—taxation; foreign exchange/import-export; industrial/labour; land reforms; urban-property ceiling; elections; foodstuffs; rent/tenancy etc.; **Parliament or competent State Legislature**.
- **323A ≠ 323B:** service matters only / broader specified matters; Parliament only / appropriate legislature; no tribunal hierarchy contemplated under 323A / hierarchy may be created under 323B.
- **L. Chandra Kumar (1997):** exclusion of HC Arts.226/227 and SC Art.32 judicial review unconstitutional; judicial review = basic structure; tribunal decisions subject to HC Division Bench scrutiny. [Indian Kanoon](https://indiankanoon.org/doc/115651420/)
- Not every tribunal is “created by Art.323A/323B”: many are created directly by statutes.
- Example: **NGT = statutory tribunal under National Green Tribunal Act, 2010**, not “NGT Act 2002”. [Green Tribunal](https://www.greentribunal.gov.in/about-us)

### N. PIL / legal profession — minimum required layer
- **PIL:** judge-made procedural innovation; not expressly created by Constitution/statute; relaxation of *locus standi* for genuine public-interest/access-to-justice litigation.
- Indian PIL pioneers → **V.R. Krishna Iyer + P.N. Bhagwati**.
- For `UPPCS-2018-GS1-Q093`, canonical key points to **P.N. Bhagwati**; do not generalise this into a simplistic “single inventor of PIL” proposition.
- PIL ≠ judicial review; PIL is a route/procedural device, judicial review is a constitutional power/doctrine.
- **Advocates Act, 1961:** statutory basis for State Bar Councils + Bar Council of India; BCI functions include professional-conduct standards, legal-education standards/supervision and legal-aid-related functions. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/indiacode/handle/123456789/1631?view_type=browse)

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer
- Memorise direct mappings: **39A—legal aid; 50—separation judiciary/executive; 323A—administrative tribunals; Part XIV-A—tribunals; 124—SC judges**.
- Judge resignation → **President**, not CJI/Parliament.
- PIL: retain **P.N. Bhagwati** for canonical `UPPCS-2018-GS1-Q093`, alongside broader Krishna Iyer–Bhagwati pioneer formulation.
- UPPCS has tested “first e-Lok Adalat” and judicial-personality trivia; treat these as **isolated PYQ evidence, not permanent core expansion**.
- Legal-aid income limits are State-specific. Current UPSLSA portal states **₹3 lakh**, while NALSA’s national FAQ table still displays an older **₹1 lakh** figure for Uttar Pradesh; therefore **do not freeze an UP numerical ceiling in the permanent note without re-verification near the exam**. [UP Legal Services Authority](https://uttarpradesh.nalsa.gov.in/legal-aid/)

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- **Art.131 original jurisdiction ≠ constitutional-validity jurisdiction over every Central law.**
- **Art.131A no longer exists** → HC can invalidate Central law.
- **Judicial review ≠ Art.137 review jurisdiction.**
- **Art.141 precedent ≠ Art.142 complete justice.**
- **President appoints SC judges ≠ President has unfettered appointment discretion.**
- **Second Judges Case = 1993, not 1983.**
- **Number of SC judges → Parliament by law**, not CJI/President alone.
- **SC retirement 65 ≠ HC retirement 62.**
- Judge resignation → **President**.
- Judge **removal** ≠ President’s constitutional “impeachment”.
- **Constitution:** grounds + majority ≠ **Judges (Inquiry) Act:** procedural machinery.
- Speaker/Chairman **may reject** removal notice.
- CJI calling retired judge ≠ unilateral power; **President’s prior consent + judge’s consent**.
- **Court of Record** → contempt power; Constitution does **not** define civil/criminal contempt.
- **Art.39A legal aid ≠ Art.50 separation judiciary/executive.**
- Section 12 legal-aid categories ≠ income test for every beneficiary category.
- Historical ₹1-lakh PYQ ceiling ≠ present universal ceiling.
- **Ordinary Lok Adalat cannot adjudicate merits after failed settlement ≠ Permanent Lok Adalat can in its statutory field.**
- Lok Adalat award final/no appeal ≠ ordinary court decree reached after trial.
- Gram Nyayalaya: **civil + criminal**, not civil only.
- Gram Nyayalaya establishment = **permissive/not mandatory**.
- **323A Parliament only ≠ 323B Parliament/State Legislature.**
- **323A service matters ≠ 323B other enumerated fields.**
- Tribunal jurisdiction ≠ exclusion of HC/SC judicial review after **L. Chandra Kumar**.
- **NGT Act 2010 ≠ 2002**.
- PIL ≠ writ ≠ judicial review.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT
- Base: **Second Judges Case (1983)** → **Second Judges Case (1993)**. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/43070.pdf)
- Base: “Judges Enquiry Act” → formal title **Judges (Inquiry) Act, 1968**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/indiacode/handle/123456789/1539?view_type=browse)
- Base: PLA pecuniary jurisdiction **₹10 lakh** → current statutory threshold **₹1 crore**; revised in 2015. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19731/1/book_for_upload.pdf)
- Base: “CJI = patron and chief of NALSA/SCLSC” → **CJI = Patron-in-Chief, NALSA; senior-most SC judge = Executive Chairman; SCLSC has separate SC-judge Chairman**. [National Legal Services Authority](https://nalsa.gov.in/organogram/)
- Base: fixed/general legal-aid **₹1 lakh** formulation → ceilings are now court/state-specific; NALSA currently gives **₹5 lakh for SC matters**. [National Legal Services Authority](https://nalsa.gov.in/faqs/)
- Base: SC alternative seat at CJI choice → requires **President’s approval**.
- Base: Art.128 retired judge condition incomplete → **CJI request + previous President consent + judge’s consent**.
- Base: general SC “superintendence over all courts/tribunals” → **omit**; Art.227 superintendence is HC-specific. Use SC’s Art.136 appellate/SLP and other express constitutional powers instead.
- Base: **NGT Act 2002** → **National Green Tribunal Act, 2010**. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/indiacode/handle/123456789/2025?view_type=browse)

### VERIFY
- **None blocking.**
- UP legal-aid income ceiling is a **dynamic official-source inconsistency** (current UPSLSA page ₹3 lakh vs NALSA FAQ table ₹1 lakh for UP); exclude the figure from frozen core and verify only if adding a current UP-specific layer. [UP Legal Services Authority](https://uttarpradesh.nalsa.gov.in/legal-aid/)

### OMIT
- Current CJI/judge names and vacancies.
- CAT/SC/HC current bench counts.
- Live Gram Nyayalaya notified/functional counts.
- DISHA, Nyaya Bandhu, Nyaya Mitra, Nyaya Vikas, Justice Clock, current portals/schemes.
- `UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q049` first e-Lok-Adalat trivia from durable core.
- `UPPCS-2024-GS1-Q144` judicial-personality/current-affairs detail from durable core.
- Extended judicial-activism/overreach debate and long judgment catalogues.
- Family Courts / Commercial Courts unless a later taxonomy node specifically owns them.
- Judge salaries and other low-yield mutable monetary figures.

## 8. Supplement Decision
`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

- Canonical PYQ demand is covered by base notes + Laxmikanth/reference material.
- Material errors/current statutory changes have been resolved with Constitution/India Code/Supreme Court/NALSA/Department of Justice sources.
- No test-series/topper/coaching supplement is required for this taxonomy node.

# Polity & Governance → Union Institutions & Judiciary → Union Executive — Source Pack v01

**Status:** PASS_WITH_CORRECTIONS  
**taxonomy_id:** `prelims-polity-and-governance-union-institutions-and-judiciary-union-executive`

## 1. Scope

- **Includes:** President—election, tenure/removal, powers, assent/veto, ordinance, pardon, reports laid; PM; Union Council of Ministers; Cabinet; Cabinet Secretariat; Union business rules; Attorney-General; direct PYQ-linked PMO distinctions; Vice-President as UPPCS extension. PRE-POL__TAXONOMY_GATEWAY
- **Excludes:** Parliament procedure except where directly testing President/CoM; Governor/State executive; constitutional/statutory bodies except President-linked report-laying; current office-holders; dynamic Cabinet Committee membership/count; sectoral governance.
- **UPSC depth:** Presidential-election fine print; Art. 71 consequences; ordinance limits; pardon + judicial review; Cabinet Secretariat; Art. 74/75/77/78 qualifiers; AG parliamentary rights.
- **UPPCS depth:** Vice-President; PMO; office/Article mapping; historical President–VP chronology where directly PYQ-backed.

### Demand synthesis

- **Recurring concepts:** nominal vs real executive; CoM responsibility; presidential electoral college/vote value; President–VP contrasts; Cabinet Secretariat; AG status/rights.
- **Recurring traps:** `Parliament` ≠ `Lok Sabha`; President ≠ PM as repository of Union executive power; President ≠ MP though part of Parliament; nominated MPs: President election ✗, VP election ✓; Cabinet Secretary ≠ PM; Cabinet Secretariat ≠ PMO.
- **Qualifier patterns:** “elected”, “nominated”, “both Houses”, “House of the People”, “total membership”, “may reconsider once”, “not entitled to vote”, “limited judicial review”.
- **UPSC vs UPPCS:** UPSC → constitutional fine print + elimination; UPPCS → Article/post mapping, VP, PMO and chronology.
- **Recent reinforcement:** UPSC 2025 directly reinforced **ordinance limits + pardoning power**; UPPCS 2025 reinforced **VP status + Presidential chronology**.

## 3. Base-Note Coverage

- **already strong:** President election, electoral vote value, tenure/impeachment, veto, ordinance, pardon; Vice-President; Art. 74–75; CoM/Cabinet; Cabinet Secretariat. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **partial:** reports laid; Art. 71 fine print; PM-specific Art. 78; Cabinet vs CoM definition; business-rule architecture.
- **missing:** dedicated **Attorney-General** payload; **PMO** distinction/history.
