- Reference sources contain dedicated President/VP/PM/CoM/Cabinet material, while the PYQ reference also separately covers President, VP, CoM and AG. BOTH__POL__BOOK__LAXMIKANTH__20… PRE__POL__PYQ__GHATNACHAKRA__20…

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Constitutional map

| Provision | Payload |
|---|---|
| Art. 52–62 | President |
| Art. 53 | Union executive power vested in President |
| Art. 54–55 | President election + vote-value |
| Art. 61 | impeachment |
| Art. 62 | election on vacancy |
| Art. 63–71 | Vice-President/election disputes |
| Art. 72 | President's clemency |
| Art. 73 | extent of Union executive power |
| Art. 74 | CoM → aid/advice |
| Art. 75 | PM + Ministers; responsibility |
| Art. 76 | Attorney-General |
| Art. 77 | conduct/allocation of Union business |
| Art. 78 | PM's duties towards President |
| Art. 79 | Parliament = President + RS + LS |
| Art. 85–87 | summons/prorogation/address |
| Art. 88 | parliamentary rights of Ministers + AG |
| Art. 111 | Presidential assent |
| Art. 123 | Ordinances |

Constitution places these Union-executive provisions together in Part V, Chapter I, with ordinance power separately under Art. 123. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19632/1/the_constitution_of_india.pdf)

### B. President — election / tenure / removal

| Item | Frozen fact |
|---|---|
| Electoral college | **Elected** LS MPs + **elected** RS MPs + **elected MLAs of States**; Art. 54 Explanation includes Delhi + Puducherry |
| Excluded | nominated MPs; MLCs; nominated legislature members |
| Method | indirect; PR-STV; secret ballot |
| MLA vote value | `State population ÷ elected MLAs ÷ 1000`; remainder ≥500 → +1 |
| MP vote value | total value of MLA votes ÷ total elected MPs; same value for LS & RS MPs |
| Population basis | 1971 Census until figures of first Census taken after 2026 are published |
| Disputes | Supreme Court; decision final |
| Electoral-college vacancy | election cannot be questioned merely because a vacancy exists |
| Election later declared void | acts done **on or before** SC decision remain valid |
| Qualification | citizen; 35+; qualified for LS; no disqualifying office of profit |
| Term | 5 years; continues until successor enters office; re-election permitted |
| Casual vacancy | election ≤6 months; person elected gets **fresh full 5-year term** |
| Oath | CJI; if unavailable → senior-most available SC judge |

The Constitution and ECI confirm the elected-member composition, vote-value architecture and 1971-Census rule. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19632/1/the_constitution_of_india.pdf) Art. 71—not Art. 62—contains the electoral-college-vacancy and void-election safeguards. [India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_DH_62_1008_00005_00005_1561028196113\&filename=the_constitution_of_india.pdf\&type=actfile\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Impeachment — Art. 61**
- ground: **violation of Constitution** only.
- initiated in **either House**.
- ≥14 days' notice; signed by ≥¼ of total membership.
- initiating House: ≥⅔ **total membership**.
- other House investigates; President may appear/be represented.
- second House also requires ≥⅔ **total membership**.
- all MPs of the Houses participate; State legislatures have no role. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19632/1/the_constitution_of_india.pdf)

### C. President — position & parliamentary powers

- **President = constitutional/de jure executive; PM/CoM = real political executive.**
- Art. 74: President acts on CoM advice; may return advice **once** for reconsideration → reconsidered advice binding; advice itself not judicially inquired into. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/06/a714fb6b673825b7170f2f0c979a913c.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Art. 79: President is **part of Parliament but not a member of either House**.
- Art. 87 special address: first session after each LS general election + first session of each year.
- Art. 111:
  - ordinary bill → assent / withhold / return for reconsideration;
  - returned bill passed again → assent cannot be withheld;
  - Money Bill → cannot be returned;
  - Constitution Amendment Bill → assent obligatory;
  - Constitution prescribes **no fixed numerical time limit** for Art. 111 assent.
- Art. 85 powers of summons/prorogation/dissolution operate constitutionally with ministerial advice; prorogation need not await adjournment sine die.

### D. Ordinance — Art. 123

- condition: **both Houses must not simultaneously be in session** + circumstances requiring immediate action.
- same force/effect as Act of Parliament.
- laid before both Houses.
- ceases **6 weeks after reassembly**; if Houses reassemble on different dates → later date.
- may be withdrawn earlier; both Houses may disapprove.
- Parliament's legislative competence limits apply.
- **can:** amend/repeal ordinary Central Act; operate retrospectively.
- **cannot:** abridge FR validly; amend Constitution.
- repeated re-promulgation to bypass legislature → constitutionally impermissible.
- President exercises ordinance power within parliamentary executive structure, not as personal legislation. Art. 123 itself fixes the six-week and competence limits. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19632/1/the_constitution_of_india.pdf)

### E. Pardoning power — Art. 72

President may grant **pardon / reprieve / respite / remission** or **suspend / remit / commute** sentence where:
- Court Martial sentence;
- offence concerns a matter within Union executive power;
- sentence is death. [India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_PY_81_837_00101_00101_1612163968634\&filename=the_constitution_of_india.pdf\&type=actfile\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Elimination layer**
- President acts on **aid/advice of Union CoM**.
- power ≠ appellate judicial power.
- can examine material independently of court's conclusion.
- **limited judicial review** available—e.g. non-application of mind, arbitrariness, mala fides, irrelevant considerations. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2021/31429/31429_2021_9_1501_58322_Judgement_08-Jan-2025.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### F. Prime Minister + Council of Ministers

- **Art. 74:** CoM with PM at head.
- **Art. 75(1):** PM appointed by President; other Ministers appointed by President **on PM's advice**.
- no constitutional requirement that PM be MP **at moment of appointment** → must become member of **either House** within 6 consecutive months.
- Minister may belong to LS **or** RS.
- total Union CoM ≤ **15% of total LS membership**.
- Tenth-Schedule disqualified MP → ministership bar under Art. 75(1B).
- Ministers formally hold office during President's pleasure.
- **collective responsibility = House of the People/Lok Sabha**, not “Parliament” or “both Houses”.
- no-confidence motion → whole ministry.
- President administers oaths of office + secrecy.
- salaries/allowances → Parliament by law.
- PM's resignation/death → CoM falls.

These distinctions are directly fixed by Arts. 74–75. [India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_RJ_83_1130_00003_00003_1696138372158\&filename=the_constitution_of_india.pdf\&type=actfile\&utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Art. 78 — PM → President**
- communicate CoM decisions concerning Union administration + legislative proposals;
- furnish information President calls for;
- if President requires → place before CoM a matter decided by an individual Minister but not considered by CoM. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19151/1/constitution_of_india.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### G. Council of Ministers ≠ Cabinet

| Council of Ministers | Cabinet |
|---|---|
| Constitutional: Arts. 74–75 | expressly defined for Art. 352(3) purposes |
| PM + Cabinet Ministers + Ministers of State etc. | PM + **Ministers of Cabinet rank** |
| larger body | inner/core body |
| collective responsibility constitutionally attaches to CoM | principal practical policy/decision forum |
| size capped at 15% LS | no separate constitutional numerical cap |

### H. Cabinet Secretariat + Business Rules

- functions **directly under PM**.
- administrative head = **Cabinet Secretary**.
- Cabinet Secretary = ex-officio Chairman, Civil Services Board.
- Cabinet Secretariat:
  - secretarial assistance to Cabinet + Cabinet Committees;
  - Cabinet agenda/records/follow-up;
  - inter-ministerial coordination + dispute resolution;
  - administration of **Government of India (Transaction of Business) Rules, 1961** and **Allocation of Business Rules, 1961**.
- AoB + ToB Rules framed under **Art. 77(3)**.
- **allocation of financial resources to Ministries ≠ Cabinet Secretariat function.**
- Art. 77:
  - Union executive action expressed in **name of President**, not PM;
  - President makes rules for convenient transaction of business + allocation among Ministers. [Cabinet Secretariat](https://cabsec.gov.in/aboutus/functions/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### I. Attorney-General for India

- **Art. 76; constitutional office; Union Executive.**
- appointed by President.
- qualification = qualified for appointment as **SC judge**.
- duty: legal advice to Government of India + legal duties referred/assigned by President.
- right of audience in **all courts in India**.
- tenure: **pleasure of President**; Constitution prescribes no impeachment/removal process.
- remuneration: **President determines**, not Parliament.
- Art. 88: may speak/take part in either House, joint sitting and a parliamentary committee of which named → **no vote by virtue of Art. 88**.
- not necessary to be MP.
- constitutional rule does **not** require AG to resign automatically when appointing government resigns; such resignation is conventional, not an Art. 76 command.
- **Solicitor-General ≠ constitutional office.** [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19632/1/the_constitution_of_india.pdf)

### J. Reports / recommendations caused to be laid by President

| Material | Anchor |
|---|---|
| Finance Commission recommendations + explanatory memorandum | Art. 281 |
| CAG Union reports | Art. 151(1) |
| UPSC annual report + memorandum on advice not accepted | Art. 323(1) |
| NCSC report | Art. 338(6) |
| NCST report | Art. 338A(6) |
| NCBC report | Art. 338B(6) |

**Trap:** Public Accounts Committee report is a parliamentary committee report → **not** one of the reports constitutionally caused to be laid by President.

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

### Vice-President

- Arts. **63–71**.
- Art. 64 → ex-officio Chairman, Rajya Sabha.
- **not a member of Rajya Sabha or either House**.
- election:
  - electoral college = **all members of both Houses of Parliament** → elected + nominated;
  - State legislatures excluded;
  - PR-STV + secret ballot.
- qualification: citizen; 35+; qualified for election to **Rajya Sabha**; no disqualifying office of profit.
- term: 5 years; resignation → President.
- removal:
  - **not impeachment**;
  - no constitutional ground specified;
  - initiated **only in Rajya Sabha**;
  - ≥14 days' notice;
  - RS → majority of **all the then members** (effective majority);
  - LS → agreement by ordinary/simple majority.
- when acting as President → does not perform RS Chairman functions.
- VP vacancy → election **as soon as possible**; elected person receives fresh 5-year term.
- election declared void → official acts done **on or before SC decision remain valid**. [Vice President of India](https://vicepresidentofindia.nic.in/the-vice-president-of-india-and-the-constitution/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

**Direct chronology payload**
- Neelam Sanjiva Reddy → R. Venkataraman → K.R. Narayanan → A.P.J. Abdul Kalam.
- **Neelam Sanjiva Reddy** did **not** serve as Vice-President before becoming President; R. Venkataraman, Shankar Dayal Sharma and Zakir Hussain did.

### PMO

- **Prime Minister's Secretariat:** established **15 Aug 1947**.
- renamed **Prime Minister's Office (PMO): 28 Mar 1977**.
- PMO provides secretarial assistance to PM.
- PMO headed by **Principal Secretary to PM**.
- **PMO ≠ Cabinet Secretariat**.
- **Principal Secretary to PM ≠ Cabinet Secretary**. [PM India](https://www.pmindia.gov.in/en/proactive-disclosure-under-section-4-1-b-of-the-rti-act-2005/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist

- Union executive power → **President**, not PM.
- real executive → **PM + CoM**; constitutional repository → President.
- President part of Parliament ≠ President is MP.
- President electoral college: **elected MPs only**; VP electoral college: **elected + nominated MPs**.
- MLCs ≠ Presidential electors.
- MLA vote values vary State-to-State; LS MP vote value = RS MP vote value.
- electoral-college vacancy ≠ ground to invalidate/postpone election.
- Art. **71(4)** ≠ Art. 62 for incomplete-electoral-college rule.
- impeachment: ⅔ **total membership** ≠ ⅔ present and voting.
- CoM responsible to **Lok Sabha** ≠ both Houses/Parliament.
- PM/Minister need not be MP on day of appointment ≠ may remain non-MP indefinitely.
- Cabinet ≠ entire CoM.
- Cabinet does **not** include Ministers of State merely by being Ministers.
- Cabinet Secretariat ≠ PMO.
- Cabinet Secretary ≠ head of PMO.
- President's executive actions expressed in **President's name** ≠ PM's name.
- AoB/ToB Rules → Art. 77(3).
- Cabinet Secretariat secretarial coordination ≠ allocation of financial resources.
- AG qualification = SC judge qualification ≠ must already be SC judge.
- AG remuneration → President ≠ Parliament.
- AG can speak/participate ≠ can vote.
- AG ≠ Solicitor-General constitutionally.
- ordinary Bill return power ≠ Money Bill return power.
- Constitution Amendment Bill → President **must assent**.
- ordinance can amend ordinary Central Act ≠ amend Constitution.
- retrospective ordinance possible ≠ ordinance can violate FR.
- pardon power subject to limited judicial review ≠ wholly immune.
- President's pardon power exercised on CoM advice ≠ personal discretion.
- PAC report ≠ President-laid report.
- VP is RS Chairman ≠ RS member.
- VP removal ≠ Presidential impeachment.

|---|---|---|

[India Code](https://upload.indiacode.nic.in/showfile?actid=AC_DH_62_1008_00005_00005_1561028196113\&filename=the_constitution_of_india.pdf\&type=actfile\&utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| Base: impeachment says “two-thirds majority” without denominator PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… | **≥⅔ of total membership of each House** | Art. 61. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19632/1/the_constitution_of_india.pdf) |
| Base: Cabinet column includes “ministers of states, etc.” PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… | Cabinet = PM + **Ministers of Cabinet rank**; MoS belong to broader CoM | Constitutional Cabinet definition / standard executive distinction. |
| Base electoral-quota expression is malformed PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… | Do not reproduce malformed formula; retain ECI vote-value + PR-STV rules | ECI authoritative procedure. [Election Commission of India](https://www.eci.gov.in/faq/1/2?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| Reference explanation treating Cabinet Secretariat as under PMO | Cabinet Secretariat functions **directly under PM** | Cabinet Secretariat official description. [Cabinet Secretariat](https://cabsec.gov.in/aboutus/functions/?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| `UPPCS-2025-GS1-Q119` gateway key = **A / both statements** | Factual answer = **statement 1 only / option D**: VP is ex-officio RS Chairman but **not RS member** | Arts. 64/66; independent 2025 solutions also record D. [Vice President of India](https://vicepresidentofindia.nic.in/the-vice-president-of-india-and-the-constitution/?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |

[Ministry of Home Affairs](https://www.mha.gov.in/sites/default/files/BharatiyaNagarikSurakshaSanhita_24022024.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |

### OMIT

## 1. Scope

  - Federalism: federal/unitary features; Seventh Schedule; legislative relations; Articles 249, 252, 253, 254; residuary power.

### Demand synthesis
- **Recurring concepts:** Governor discretion + immunity; LC size/status; federal vs unitary features; Parliament entering State List; ISC/Zonal status; President’s Rule consequences.
- **Recurring traps:**
  - Governor for >1 State.
  - 5-year Governor term ≠ secured tenure.
  - LC Chairman elected by Council ≠ nominated by Governor.
  - Assembly dissolution ≠ immediate vacation by Speaker.
  - President’s Rule ≠ automatic Assembly/local-body dissolution.
  - Art. 249 = Rajya Sabha, not Lok Sabha.
  - Art. 253 treaty power ≠ State consent requirement.
  - Federal Union ≠ agreement among States / right to secede.
- **Qualifier patterns:** `not`, `not necessarily`, `only`, `own discretion`, `present and voting`, `as per Constitution`, exact numerical limits.
- **UPSC vs UPPCS:** UPSC → conceptual elimination + fine constitutional wording; UPPCS → article/number/commission/person recall plus direct list-entry questions.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. Constitutional map

| Area | Anchor |
|---|---|
| State Executive | Part VI, Arts. **153–167** |
| Governor | 153–161 |
| State executive power | **162** |
| Aid/advice + discretion | **163** |
| State CoM | **164** |
| State Legislature | Part VI, Arts. **168–212** |
| Creation/abolition of LC | **169** |
| LC composition | **171** |
| Speaker | **178–181** |
| LC Chairman | **182–185** |
| Ordinary bills in bicameral State | **196–197** |
| Money Bills | **198–199** |
| Governor’s assent | **200** |
| Reserved bills | **201** |
| Governor ordinance | **213** |
| Legislative Centre–State relations | Part XI, Arts. **245–255** |
| Administrative relations | Arts. **256–263** |
| Inter-State Council | **263** |
| Emergency provisions | Part XVIII, Arts. **352–360** |
| Governor immunity | **361** |

### B. Governor

- **Art. 153:** Governor for each State; **same person may be Governor of 2+ States**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Art. 155:** appointed by **President**.
- **Art. 156:** holds office during **President’s pleasure**; normal term **5 years**; no constitutional impeachment/removal procedure or specified removal grounds. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Pleasure ≠ arbitrary power: Governor-removal power is subject to constitutional limits/limited judicial review; cannot be exercised arbitrarily, whimsically or capriciously. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/41850.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Art. 160:** contingency in discharge of Governor’s functions → **President makes such provision as he thinks fit**; HC Chief Justice is not an automatic constitutional successor. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Art. 163:** CoM headed by CM aids/advises Governor except where Constitution requires discretion; Constitution expressly envisages gubernatorial discretion. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- High-value discretionary areas:
  - report recommending **Art. 356** proclamation;
  - reservation of appropriate State bills for President;
  - situational discretion in government formation/majority determination → majority ultimately tested on House floor.
- **Art. 200 — ordinary State Bill:** Governor may assent / withhold / return for reconsideration / reserve for President.
  - Money Bill → cannot be returned for reconsideration.
  - If ordinary bill is returned and Legislature passes it again → Governor **shall not withhold assent**.
  - Bill endangering constitutional position/powers of HC → must be reserved for President. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **President cannot suo motu reserve a State bill**; reservation under Arts. 200–201 originates through Governor.
- **Art. 361 immunity:**
  - not answerable to court for exercise/performance of official powers/duties;
  - no criminal proceedings during term;
  - no arrest/imprisonment process during term;
  - civil proceedings for personal acts → **2 months’ prior notice**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Sarkaria—Governor:** eminent person; outsider to State; detached from active politics/recent political involvement; CM consultation recommended.

### C. State CoM + Chief Secretary

- **Art. 164:** CM appointed by Governor; other ministers on CM’s advice; CoM collectively responsible to **Legislative Assembly**.
- **91st Amendment / Art. 164(1A):**
  - total ministers incl. CM ≤ **15%** of Assembly strength;
  - minimum = **12**.
- **Chief Secretary:**
  - senior administrative head of State administration;
  - not a constitutional office with a Constitution-prescribed gubernatorial appointment;
  - **no fixed constitutional tenure**.
  - PYQ elimination: “appointed by Governor” + “fixed tenure” → both rejected in canonical UPSC key.
- Do not import coaching-source wording that converts authentication of State executive action into an independent constitutional appointment power.

### D. State Legislature

**Legislative Council**
- **Art. 169:** Parliament creates/abolishes LC **only after** Assembly resolution:
  - majority of **total membership** +
  - ≥ **2/3 present and voting**.
- Parliament’s resulting law ≠ amendment under Art. 368. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Art. 171:**
  - maximum = **1/3 of Assembly strength**;
  - minimum = **40**;
  - actual strength fixed by Parliament.
  - composition: as nearly as possible:
    - 1/3 local authorities;
    - 1/12 graduates;
    - 1/12 teachers;
    - 1/3 MLAs elect persons not MLAs;
    - **remainder** nominated by Governor.
  - “remainder” ≈ 1/6; use constitutional wording where precision matters. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Permanent House; member term **6 years**; 1/3 retire every **2 years**.
- Chairman + Deputy Chairman → **elected by Council**, not Governor.

**Assembly Speaker**
- elected by Assembly.
- vacates office if ceases to be Assembly member.
- removal → majority of **all the then members** + 14-day notice.
- Assembly dissolution → Speaker **continues until immediately before first meeting of new Assembly**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

**SLA vs SLC**
- Ordinary Bill:
  - first Council delay: max **3 months**;
  - Assembly passes again;
  - second Council delay: max **1 month**;
  - total possible delay = **4 months**, not 6.
- No joint sitting at State level.
- Money Bill:
  - introduced only in Assembly;
  - Council → recommendations only, **14 days**.
- Council cannot permanently veto an Assembly-originated ordinary bill. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

### E. Federal character

**Federal**
- dual polity.
- constitutional division of legislative powers.
- written + supreme Constitution.
- independent judiciary.
- bicameral Union legislature.
- selected federal amendments need State ratification.
- federalism = basic-structure element.

**Unitary/centralising**
- stronger Union List + residuary power with Parliament.
- States have no right to secede; territorial existence/boundaries alterable by Parliament under constitutional procedure.
- single citizenship.
- integrated judiciary.
- Governors appointed by President.
- All-India Services.
- emergency provisions.
- unequal State representation in Rajya Sabha.

**PYQ elimination**
- Indian federation **not formed by an agreement among federating States**.
- Independent judiciary = major indicator of federal character.
- UPPCS attribution: **K.C. Wheare → “quasi-federal”**; retain as attribution, not constitutional terminology.

### F. Legislative relations

| Route for Parliament into State field | Trigger / trap |
|---|---|
| **Art. 249** | Rajya Sabha resolution; ≥ **2/3 present & voting**; national interest; resolution max 1 year at a time |
| **Art. 250** | National Emergency in operation |
| **Art. 252** | **2+ States** pass resolutions requesting Parliament; other States may adopt later |
| **Art. 253** | Implement treaty/agreement/convention/international decision; **State consent not necessary** |

- Art. 249 law → ceases to have effect **6 months after** resolution ceases, except past acts.
- Art. 250 law → ceases **6 months after** Emergency ends, except past acts.
- Art. 252 law applicable to consenting/adopting States; concerned State legislature cannot itself amend/repeal that Parliamentary law.
- Art. 253 overrides normal distribution where needed for international obligations. Arts. 249 and 253 are explicit constitutional exceptions to ordinary State legislative exclusivity. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf) [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Art. 248:** residuary legislative + residuary taxation power → Parliament.
- **Art. 254:** Concurrent-list repugnancy:
  - normally Parliamentary law prevails;
  - State law reserved for + assented to by President may prevail **within that State**;
  - Parliament may later override it.

### G. Seventh-Schedule elimination anchors

- **Inter-State trade and commerce** → Union List.
- **Inter-State quarantine** → Union List.
- “Trade and commerce **within State**” generally State List, subject to specific Concurrent entries → do not equate generic trade with inter-State trade.
- **Economic and social planning** → Concurrent List.
- **Adulteration of foodstuffs and other goods** → Concurrent List.
- **Intestacy and succession** → Concurrent List.
- 42nd Amendment shifted to Concurrent List: **education; forests; weights & measures; protection of wild animals & birds; administration of justice**.

### H. Minor minerals

- MMDR Act:
  - **State Governments** can make rules for grant of concessions in respect of minor minerals.
  - States can make rules to prevent illegal mining.
  - **Central Government** has power to notify what constitutes a “minor mineral”.
- Trap: `State grants concessions but Centre alone frames grant rules` = false. [Ministry of Mines](https://mines.gov.in/admin/download/6433eda82708c1681124776.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) [Ministry of Mines](https://mines.gov.in/admin/download/666a9ae9e4af51718262505.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### I. Inter-governmental coordination

| Body | Status | Anchor |
|---|---|---|
| **Inter-State Council** | Constitutional enabling provision | **Art. 263**; President may establish by order |
| **Zonal Councils** | Statutory | States Reorganisation Act, 1956 |
| **National Security Council** | Executive / non-constitutional | not established by Constitution |

- Art. 263 functions may include:
  - enquire into/advice on inter-State disputes;
  - discuss Union–State/common-interest subjects;
  - recommend better policy/action coordination. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Sarkaria Commission recommended a **permanent ISC**.
- PYQ trap: constitutional mention/provision ≠ every coordinating body being constitutional.

### J. Emergency provisions

| Feature | National Emergency | President’s Rule | Financial Emergency |
|---|---|---|---|
| Article | **352** | **356** | **360** |
| Basis | war / external aggression / armed rebellion | failure of constitutional machinery in State | threat to financial stability/credit of India or part |
| Initial Parliamentary approval | **1 month** | **2 months** | **2 months** |
| Majority | **Special** | Simple | Simple |
| Continuation | 6 months at a time | 6 months at a time | no periodic reapproval |
| Maximum | no fixed max with 6-month approvals | **3 years**, subject to conditions beyond 1 year | no fixed max until revoked |

**Art. 352**
- proclamation on **written decision/advice of Union Cabinet**.
- whole or part of India.
- approval/continuance → majority of total membership + ≥2/3 present & voting. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Parliament may legislate on State List; State legislature itself is **not suspended**.
- Union can give State executive directions on any matter.
- **Art. 358:** Article 19 suspension only when emergency is on **war/external aggression**, not armed rebellion; post-44th protection limited to emergency-related laws/executive action satisfying constitutional conditions.
- **Art. 359:** President may suspend right to move courts for specified Part III rights; **Arts. 20 & 21 excluded**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)

**Art. 356**
- President may act on **Governor’s report or otherwise**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- President may assume State executive functions; State legislative powers become exercisable **by/under authority of Parliament**.
- State Assembly:
  - may be suspended or dissolved;
  - **not automatically dissolved** by proclamation.
- Local bodies → not automatically dissolved.
- State CoM removed; Governor administers on behalf of Union.
- Fundamental Rights → **not suspended merely because Art. 356 operates**.
- Beyond **1 year**: both conditions required:
  - National Emergency operating in whole/part of relevant State; and
  - ECI certifies difficulty in holding Assembly election.
- **S.R. Bommai:** proclamation subject to judicial review; legislative majority ordinarily tested on floor; Art. 356 exceptional, not routine.

**Art. 360**
- threat to financial stability/credit of India or any part. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- Union may direct States to observe financial propriety.
- directions may include reduction of salaries/allowances of persons serving Union/States, including SC/HC judges.
- President may require State Money/financial bills to be reserved for consideration.

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer
- **Article recall**
  - 153 → Governor for each State.
  - 154 → executive power of State vested in Governor.
  - 155 → **appointment**, not removal, of Governor.
  - 156 → term/President’s pleasure.
  - 162 → **extent of executive power of State**.
  - 169 → creation/abolition of Legislative Council.
  - 352 → National Emergency.
- **Numbers**
  - LC ≤ **1/3** Assembly; ≥ **40**.
  - State CoM incl. CM → minimum **12**, maximum **15%** of Assembly.
- **Attributions**
  - K.C. Wheare → quasi-federal description.
  - Sarkaria Commission → Centre–State relations; permanent ISC; Governor-related recommendations.
- **State-legislature distinction:** Legislative Council = permanent/not dissolvable, but **can be abolished by Parliament through Art. 169 route**.
- **Concurrent-list recall:** adulteration of foodstuffs/other goods; intestacy/succession.
- Avoid current Governor names, current Council memberships or State-specific office-holder trivia.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- Governor for each State **≠** different Governor compulsory for every State.
- Governor’s 5-year term **≠** guaranteed 5-year tenure.
- No constitutional removal procedure/grounds for Governor **≠** arbitrary removal immune from judicial review.
- Art. **155 = appointment**, not removal.
- Governor discretion expressly contemplated **≠** every gubernatorial function discretionary.
- Governor may reserve State bill **≠** President can suo motu pick up/reserve it.
- Art. 361 official immunity **≠** State Government immune from challenge.
- Criminal proceedings barred during Governor’s term **≠** permanent criminal immunity.
- LC maximum **1/3 Assembly**, minimum **40**.
- LC Chairman elected by Council **≠** nominated by Governor.
- LC permanent/not dissolvable **≠** cannot be abolished.
- Speaker survives Assembly dissolution **≠** vacates immediately.
- Ordinary Bill LC delay = **3 + 1 = 4 months**, not 6.
- State bicameral deadlock **≠** joint sitting.
- Chief Secretary **≠** constitutional Governor-appointed office; no fixed constitutional tenure.
- Federalism **≠** federation created by agreement of States.
- Indian States **≠** constitutional right to secede.
- Art. 249 → **Rajya Sabha + 2/3 present & voting**, not Lok Sabha.
- Art. 253 treaty implementation **≠** State consent required.
- Residuary powers → Parliament.
- State law with Presidential assent on Concurrent matter can prevail in that State **≠** Parliament permanently excluded.
- Inter-State Council constitutional provision **≠** Zonal Councils constitutional.
- Zonal Councils = statutory **≠** constitutional.
- State has minor-mineral rulemaking powers **≠** Centre alone frames concession rules.
- President’s Rule **≠** automatic dissolution of Assembly/local bodies.
- Art. 356 **≠** suspension of Fundamental Rights.
- Art. 358 → Art. 19 only + war/external aggression **≠** every National Emergency.
- Art. 359 suspends **enforcement** of specified rights **≠** automatic extinction of all FRs.
- Arts. 20 & 21 **cannot** be included in an Art. 359 suspension order.
- National Emergency approval = special majority **≠** President’s Rule/Financial Emergency approval majority.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT
- **Base:** LC ordinary Bill can be delayed maximum **6 months** → **4 months (3 months + 1 month)** → Constitution, Art. 197. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Base:** Governor’s removal under President’s pleasure “not subject to judicial review” → pleasure is not unfettered; arbitrary/capricious removal is subject to limited judicial review → *B.P. Singhal* doctrine. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/41850.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Base:** sudden Governor vacancy → HC Chief Justice automatically takes charge → **Art. 160 leaves contingency arrangement to President**; no automatic constitutional succession by CJI/HC CJ. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Base:** “44th Amendment removed Art. 275A” → wrong article number; **Art. 257A** was the omitted provision. Low-value → exclude from final note.
- **Base:** Governor universally “acts as Chancellor of State universities” → depends on applicable State university law; **not a general constitutional power** → omit from core.
- **Base:** Art. 358 protection phrased as all emergency-era action permanently immune → narrow: post-44th protection applies only within Art. 358 conditions, including emergency-related law/executive action. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/359f70a69695affb9d72f8393102bd2e.pdf)
- **Ghatna Chakra vs canonical PYQ:** explanation for UPSC 2016 Chief Secretary question says Governor appointment; canonical gateway marks both statements false. Final pack therefore uses narrower exam-safe fact: **Chief Secretary is not a Constitution-created gubernatorial appointment; no fixed constitutional tenure**.

### VERIFY
- None blocking.

### OMIT
- Current Governor–State disputes and university-Chancellor controversies.
- Current office-holders.
- Dynamic Zonal Council membership.
- Detailed water-dispute tribunals/international riparian rules.
- Special Category Status.
- Historical emergency counts/dates unless separately demanded.
- Current mineral-royalty judgments; retain durable MMDR competence only.
- Broad descriptive federalism theory not linked to PYQ elimination.
- Dynamic/state-specific Chief Secretary selection conventions beyond canonical demand.

## 8. Supplement Decision
`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

# POLITY & GOVERNANCE → STATES, FEDERALISM & EMERGENCY PROVISIONS → STATE EXECUTIVE & LEGISLATURE — Source Pack v01

**Status:** PASS_WITH_CORRECTIONS  
**taxonomy_id:** `prelims-polity-and-governance-states-federalism-and-emergency-provisions-state-executive-and-legislature`

## 1. Scope

- **Includes:** Governor—appointment, tenure/removal, dual charge, discretion, immunity, Sarkaria recommendation, legislative/ordinance/clemency powers; State Council of Ministers; Chief Secretary; State Legislature structure; Legislative Council composition/status; Assembly Speaker; legislative procedure and privileges needed for PYQs. Taxonomy explicitly centres Governor, Legislative Council size, Assembly Speaker and Chief Secretary; recurring clusters = Governor 7 Q + State legislature 3 Q. PRE-POL__TAXONOMY_GATEWAY
- **Excludes:** Centre–State legislative relations, federalism and President’s Rule machinery as independent topics; High Court organisation; election/reservation architecture; local government; current Governor–State disputes/current office-holders. PRE-POL__TAXONOMY_GATEWAY
- **UPSC depth:** Governor commission recommendations; exact immunity wording; discretion vs aid/advice; institutional fine print; qualifier-heavy State Legislature traps.
- **UPPCS depth:** No separate taxonomy-defined extension; canonical PYQs justify higher article-number/heading recall, Council of Ministers minimum size, Legislative Council size/permanence and Governor provision-mapping.

## 2. PYQ Demand Map

### Demand synthesis

- **Recurring concepts:** Governor’s constitutional position; discretion; tenure/removal; immunity; dual charge; Legislative Council size/permanence; Speaker continuity; Chief Secretary; provision mapping.
- **Recurring traps:** “Governor for each State” ≠ different person for every State; 5-year term ≠ security for full 5 years; no constitutional removal procedure ≠ arbitrary/unreviewable removal; Council ≠ Rajya Sabha in powers; permanent ≠ cannot be abolished.
- **Qualifier patterns:** *same person, not, cannot, during term, first session, fixed tenure, not more than, at least, one-third, two-thirds*.
- **UPSC vs UPPCS:** UPSC → statement-combination, institutional nuance, immunity/discretion/commission traps. UPPCS → Article-number/heading + numerical recall.

## 3. Base-Note Coverage

- **already strong:** `[KEEP/COMPRESS]` Governor Articles 153–161, dual charge, qualifications, Sarkaria, immunity, Article 200/213, CoM, Legislative Council composition, Speaker/Chairman and ordinary-bill procedure. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **partial:** `[COMPRESS]` Chief Secretary; Article 194 legislative privilege; Article 208 procedural-rule distinction; Article 160 vacancy/contingency.
- **missing:** `[ADD]` B.P. Singhal limitation on pleasure doctrine; precise Article 162 heading; explicit Article 361 personal-capacity civil-proceeding qualifier.
- **redundant:** `[OMIT]` long merits/relevance discussion of Legislative Councils; generic Governor controversies; university/VC material; broad political examples.
- **outdated/suspect:** `[CORRECT]` Governor removal “not subject to judicial review”; automatic HC Chief Justice succession; “Governor cannot pardon death sentence”; Legislative Council ordinary-bill delay “6 months”; SLC nominee category “social sciences”; Chief Secretary answer in Ghatnachakra.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### A. State Executive — anchor map

| Article | Core |
|---|---|
| **153** | Governor for each State; **same person may be Governor of 2+ States** |
| **154** | Executive power of State vested in Governor |
| **155** | Governor appointed by **President** by warrant |
| **156** | Holds office during **President’s pleasure**; nominal term **5 years**; resignation → President; continues till successor enters |
| **157** | Citizen of India + **35 years** |
| **158** | No Parliament/State Legislature membership; no office of profit; emoluments/allowances not diminished during term |
| **159** | Oath before HC Chief Justice; in absence, senior-most available HC judge |
| **160** | Unprovided contingency → **President may make provision** |
| **161** | Clemency for offences within State executive field |
| **162** | **“Extent of executive power of State”** |
| **163** | CoM headed by CM aids/advises Governor except constitutionally discretionary functions |
| **164** | CM appointed by Governor; other Ministers on CM advice; collective responsibility → Legislative Assembly |

Articles 153–164 establish these essentials; Article 160 does **not** create automatic succession by the HC Chief Justice. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### B. Governor — PYQ-critical distinctions

- **Dual charge:** Article 153 proviso added by **7th Amendment, 1956** → same Governor for 2+ States. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/7f6bac8249d681944c3218b11ef4d62a.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Removal:** Constitution prescribes **no impeachment/removal procedure or grounds**; Article 156 = pleasure of President. But *B.P. Singhal* limits the pleasure doctrine: removal cannot be arbitrary/whimsical/capricious and remains amenable to limited judicial review. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2011/20706/20706_2011_4_1501_36036_Judgement_20-May-2022.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Constitutional discretion:** Article 163 expressly recognises spheres where Governor acts in discretion. PYQ-tested examples → report concerning Article 356; reservation of specified State Bills for President. Appointment of Ministers generally operates through Article 164/CM advice; State business rules under Article 166 are not a free-standing discretionary power. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- **Bill flow:** State Legislature → **Governor** under Article 200 → if reserved, **President** acts under Article 201. President does **not suo motu pull a State Bill for reservation** without the Governor-reservation stage. Non-Money Bill may be returned; if repassed, Governor “shall not withhold assent.” [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- **Ordinance — Art. 213:** recess power; in bicameral State, unavailable when **both Houses are in session**. Prior President instructions required in the three constitutionally specified categories. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### C. Governor immunity — Article 361

- Not answerable to any court for exercise/performance of constitutional powers/duties.
- **No criminal proceedings whatsoever** instituted or continued during term.
- No process for arrest/imprisonment during term.
- Civil proceedings concerning acts in **personal capacity** during term → only after **2 months’ prior written notice**.
- Immunity is office-holder immunity; does not extinguish proceedings against the Union/State Government. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### D. Sarkaria Commission — PYQ-tested quartet

Governor should be:
1. eminent in some walk of life;
2. from **outside the State**;
3. detached from local politics;
4. not deeply involved in politics generally, particularly in the recent past. [Interstate Council](https://interstatecouncil.gov.in/wp-content/uploads/2015/06/volume2.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### E. State Council of Ministers

- **Art. 164(1A), 91st Amendment:** total Ministers **including CM ≤15%** of total membership of Legislative Assembly; **minimum = 12**.
- CoM collectively responsible to **Legislative Assembly**, not Legislative Council.
- Minister who remains non-member of State Legislature for **6 consecutive months** ceases to be Minister. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### F. Chief Secretary

- Administrative office; **not a constitutional office** created by Part VI.
- Canonical UPSC 2016 demand:  
  - appointed by Governor → **FALSE** for PYQ purposes; selection/appointment rests with State political executive/CM.  
  - fixed tenure → **FALSE**.
- 2nd ARC proposed a selection mechanism involving a panel to the CM/Cabinet and **≥2-year fixed tenure**; Government’s recorded decision says those recommendations were **not accepted** → recommendation ≠ existing general tenure rule. [DARPG](https://www.darpg.gov.in/sites/default/files/decision15.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### G. State Legislature — structure/composition

- **Art. 168:** State Legislature = **Governor + House(s)**.
- **Art. 169:** Legislative Council creation/abolition:
  - State Assembly resolution → majority of **total membership + ≥2/3 present & voting**;
  - Parliament then acts **by law**;
  - such law ≠ constitutional amendment under Art. 368. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

| Feature | Legislative Assembly | Legislative Council |
|---|---|---|
| Constitutional strength | **60–500** ordinarily | **≤1/3 of Assembly; ≥40** |
| Election | Direct | Indirect + nomination |
| Duration | 5 years unless sooner dissolved | **Permanent; not dissolved** |
| Rotation | — | ≈1/3 retire every 2 years |
| Presiding officer | Speaker | Chairman |

Council composition:
- ≈ **1/3** local-authority electorates
- ≈ **1/12** graduates
- ≈ **1/12** teachers
- ≈ **1/3** elected by MLAs from persons **not MLAs**
- remainder ≈ **1/6 nominated by Governor** → literature, science, art, **co-operative movement, social service**
- elected categories → proportional representation by **single transferable vote**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### H. Speaker / Chairman traps

- Speaker + Deputy Speaker elected **by Assembly from among its members**.
- Speaker vacates if ceases Assembly membership.
- Removal → majority of **all the then members** (= effective majority) + **14 days’ notice**.
- Dissolution of Assembly ≠ immediate vacation: Speaker continues **until immediately before first meeting of the new Assembly**.
- Chairman/Deputy Chairman likewise elected by Legislative Council itself; Governor does **not** nominate Chairman.
- Money Bill classification at State level → **Speaker of Assembly; decision final**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### I. State legislative procedure — high-value elimination

| Matter | Rule |
|---|---|
| Ordinary Bill: Council first delay | up to **3 months** |
| Assembly repasses → Council second delay | up to **1 month** |
| Maximum Council delaying power | **4 months (3+1), not 6** |
| State deadlock | **No joint sitting** |
| Bill originating in Council, rejected by Assembly | **dies** |
| Money Bill | cannot originate in Council |
| Council on Money Bill | recommendations only; **14 days** |
| Speaker certification | final |

Articles 197–199 establish Assembly primacy and the 3+1 month rule. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### J. Address, rules, privileges

- **Art. 176:** Governor’s special address → first session after each general election **and** first session of **each year**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- **Art. 208:** each State House makes its own procedure/business rules. For `UPSC-CSE-2019-GS1-Q053`, canonical key treats resort to Lok Sabha practice/rules where State rules are silent as the relevant procedural convention; **do not describe it as the text of Article 208 itself**. Article 208 itself preserves the State House’s rule-making authority. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- **Art. 194:** member immune from court proceedings for anything said or any vote given **in the Legislature/committee**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer

| Recall target | Lock-in |
|---|---|
| Art. 153 | Governor for each State |
| Art. 154 | Executive power of State |
| Art. 155 | **Appointment**, not removal, of Governor |
| Art. 156 | Governor’s term |
| Art. 157 | Qualifications |
| Art. 158 | Conditions of office |
| Art. 161 | Clemency |
| Art. 162 | **Extent of executive power of State** |
| Art. 163 | CoM to aid/advice Governor |
| Art. 164 | State Ministers; **12 minimum / 15% maximum** |
| Art. 168 | State Legislature |
| Art. 169 | Creation/abolition of LC |
| Art. 170 | Assembly composition |
| Art. 171 | Council composition |
| Art. 172 | Duration |
| Art. 176 | Governor’s special address |
| Art. 178–179 | Assembly Speaker/Deputy Speaker |
| Art. 182–183 | Council Chairman/Deputy Chairman |

- **UP-specific durable fact:** Uttar Pradesh has a bicameral State Legislature; no need to preserve changing office-holder trivia.
- Legislative Council = **not dissolvable but abolishable**.
- LC = **≤1/3 Assembly + ≥40**.
- CoM incl. CM = **minimum 12**.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist

- Governor **for each State ≠ separate person for every State**.
- 5-year Governor term **≠ guaranteed 5-year tenure**.
- No constitutional removal procedure **≠ removal immune from judicial review**.
- Article **155 = appointment**; Article **156 = term/pleasure**.
- Article **162 = Extent**, not “Extension”, of executive power.
- Governor discretion **≠ all Governor functions discretionary**.
- President **cannot independently reserve** an unforwarded State Bill.
- Article 361 immunity during term **≠ permanent immunity after office**.
- Criminal immunity **≠ same rule as personal civil suits**.
- Legislative Council permanent **≠ incapable of abolition**.
- LC size **≤1/3 Assembly; minimum 40**.
- Governor nominates part of LC **≠ nominates LC Chairman**.
- Assembly dissolution **≠ Speaker immediately vacates**.
- Council ordinary-Bill delay **3 + 1 = 4 months, not 6**.
- State bicameral deadlock **≠ joint sitting**.
- Legislative Council **≠ Rajya Sabha** in constitutional powers.
- CoM collectively responsible to **Assembly, not Council**.
- Chief Secretary **≠ constitutional office; Governor appointment/fixed tenure traps both false**.
- Governor-nominated LC expertise = **literature, science, art, co-operative movement, social service**.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT

| Base/source fact | Corrected fact | Authority / reason |
|---|---|---|
| Governor’s pleasure tenure “not subject to judicial review” PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… | Pleasure doctrine exists, but arbitrary/whimsical/capricious removal is judicially reviewable | *B.P. Singhal*; SC restatement [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2023/45314/45314_2023_11_1501_60770_Judgement_08-Apr-2025.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| “On sudden vacancy, HC Chief Justice shall hold charge” PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… | No automatic constitutional succession; Art. 160 → **President may make provision** | Constitution [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| Governor = “agent of Centre” | Prefer: constitutional head + vital federal link; may act as special Union representative in limited situations; not employee of Union/agent of ruling party | SC citing *B.P. Singhal* [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/supremecourt/2023/45314/45314_2023_11_1501_60770_Judgement_08-Apr-2025.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| Governor “cannot pardon a death sentence” PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… | Art. 161 power turns on whether State executive power extends to the offence; SC has recognised overlap/concurrent access to Governor and President in death-sentence cases falling within State executive field | SC [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/ropor/rop/all/407692.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| LC can delay ordinary Bill for maximum **6 months** PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… | **4 months maximum = 3 + 1** | Art. 197 [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| Governor LC-nomination list includes “social sciences” | Constitution: **literature, science, art, co-operative movement, social service** | Art. 171(5) [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |
| Ghatnachakra marks UPSC-2016 Chief Secretary question as **(a)** and says Governor appoints CS PRE__POL__PYQ__GHATNACHAKRA__20… | Canonical record = **(d) Neither 1 nor 2**; no fixed general tenure; Governor-appointment statement rejected for PYQ | Canonical gateway + 2nd ARC/Govt decision context [DARPG](https://www.darpg.gov.in/sites/default/files/decision15.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com) |
| UPPCS-2019 Article 162 record/source says **“Extension of Executive Power of State”** | Exact constitutional heading = **“Extent of executive power of State”** | Constitution [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf) |

# Polity & Governance → States, Federalism & Emergency Provisions → Federalism, Centre–State Relations & Emergency — Source Pack v01

**Status:** PASS_WITH_CORRECTIONS  
**taxonomy_id:** `prelims-polity-and-governance-states-federalism-and-emergency-provisions-federalism-centrestate-relations-and-emergency`

## 1. Scope
- **Includes:** Indian federal character; federal vs centralising features; legislative Centre–State relations, especially Articles 249 & 253; exceptional Parliamentary legislation on State List; minor-mineral Centre/State competence; Article 263/Inter-State Council where directly PYQ-driven; National Emergency essentials; President’s Rule; Financial Emergency essentials. Taxonomy explicitly prioritises legislative relations, federalism features, President’s Rule and 2025 State-mineral powers. PRE-POL__TAXONOMY_GATEWAY
- **Excludes:** Governor-specific appointment/discretion/immunity → State Executive node; Seventh-Schedule list memorisation → Schedules/Provision Mapping; Finance Commission/devolution → Constitutional Bodies; local-government “third tier” → Local Government; dynamic mineral-royalty judgments; current political Centre–State disputes.
- **UPSC depth:** qualifier-heavy Articles 249/253/356; federal-feature identification; State vs Union competence over minor minerals; constitutional vs statutory intergovernmental bodies; emergency effects/approval distinctions.
- **UPPCS depth:** article-number recall (352/356/360/263); Sarkaria/Inter-State Council; federalism thinker–description matching; secession/federal-character propositions.

## 2. PYQ Demand Map

  - `UPSC-CSE-2018-GS1-Q053` — Art. 356; State Legislature powers exercisable by/under Parliament

  - `UPPCS-2019-GS1-Q102` — K.C. Wheare → quasi-federal
  - `UPPCS-2020-GS1-Q126` — quasi-federal vs independent judiciary assertion–reason

  - `UPPCS-2024-GS1-Q122` — Article 352 → proclamation of Emergency

**Canonical-record quality:** all IDs above are `FROZEN`, `QUIZ_USABLE`, final/scoreable; no included editable-review record.

### Demand synthesis
- **Recurring concepts:** Art. 249; Art. 253; federal vs unitary/centralising traits; Art. 356 consequences; State–Centre division over minor minerals.
- **Recurring traps:** “State consent required”; “special majority” without specifying formula; automatic Assembly dissolution; Art. 19 suspension under President’s Rule; States’ right to secede; Centre alone makes minor-mineral concession rules.
- **Qualifier patterns:** `necessarily`, `exclusive`, `national interest`, `present and voting`, `constitutional`, `can/may` vs `automatically`.
- **UPSC vs UPPCS:** UPSC → functional/procedural elimination; UPPCS → article/commission/thinker matching + direct constitutional characterization.
- **Recent reinforcement:** minor-mineral competence repeated 2019 → 2025; constitutional status of Inter-State Council tested in 2025.

## 3. Base-Note Coverage
- **already strong:** federal/unitary feature lists; five routes for Parliament into State List; Articles 249/253; Concurrent List repugnancy; Arts. 355/356/365; National/Financial emergency; President’s Rule; Sarkaria + Inter-State Council. PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol… PRE__POL__BASE__SELF__2026__Pol…
- **partial:** Article 252 legal consequence; precise Article 253 wording; Article 356 post-proclamation law continuity; Inter-State Council constitutional-status trap; minor-mineral statutory anchors.
- **missing:** explicit MMDR Act §§3(e), 15 mapping; Article 356 High Court exception; compact three-emergency approval comparison.
- **redundant:** broad commission recommendation catalogues; detailed ISC composition; general administrative-relation material without PYQ linkage.
- **outdated/suspect:** “signed and ratified” limitation under Art. 253; Art. 252 states “lose right to legislate”; “Presidential Acts remain valid forever”; Art. `275A` typo; one-month-after-LS-reconstitution wording; attributing Art. 356 judicial review simply to the 44th Amendment.

## 4. Core Fact Payload

### Federal character
- **Article 1:** India = **Union of States**.
- Indian federation **not created by agreement among States**; States possess **no constitutional right to secede**.
- **Federal features:** dual polity; written + supreme Constitution; constitutional division of powers; rigidity of federal provisions; independent judiciary; bicameralism.
- **Centralising/non-federal features:** strong Centre; residuary power with Parliament; Parliament’s exceptional State-List competence; single Constitution; single citizenship; integrated judiciary; Governors appointed by President; All-India Services; unequal State representation in Rajya Sabha; State boundaries alterable by Parliament; emergency provisions.
- **Essential PYQ distinction:** independent judiciary + constitutional division of power indicate federal character; single citizenship/emergency/strong Centre do not.

### Legislative Centre–State relations
- **Part XI, Chapter I: Arts. 245–255.**
- **Art. 246 + Seventh Schedule:** Union List → Parliament; State List → State legislature; Concurrent List → both; Union law ordinarily prevails on repugnancy.
- **Art. 248:** residuary legislative power, including residuary taxation, → Parliament.

| Route for Parliament over State-field | Trigger | Key limit/trap |
|---|---|---|
| **Art. 249** | Rajya Sabha national-interest resolution | **≥2/3 members present & voting**; resolution ≤1 year, renewable 1 year at a time; resulting law loses effect 6 months after resolution ceases |
| **Art. 250** | National Emergency operative | State legislature does **not** cease to legislate; Parliamentary law prevails during conflict; special law loses effect 6 months after Emergency |
| **Art. 252** | Resolutions by legislatures of ≥2 States | Act applies to requesting States + later adopting States; **State legislature cannot amend/repeal that Parliamentary Act** |
| **Art. 253** | International obligation | Parliament may legislate for treaty/agreement/convention or decision of international conference/association/body; **State consent not constitutionally required** |
| **Arts. 356–357** | President’s Rule | State legislative powers may be exercised by/under authority of Parliament; Parliament may confer law-making power on President/other authority |

Art. 249 expressly uses two-thirds **present and voting**, not the Article 368 “majority of total membership + 2/3 present and voting” formula. Art. 252 preserves amendment/repeal of such Parliamentary legislation to Parliament; Art. 253 is wider than merely a “signed and ratified treaty.” [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

- **Art. 254(2):** State law on Concurrent subject, repugnant to earlier Parliamentary/existing law, may prevail **in that State** if reserved for President + receives assent; Parliament can later add/amend/vary/repeal it. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### Minor minerals — 2019 + 2025 PYQ anchor
- **MMDR Act, 1957, §3(e):** “minor minerals” includes specified minerals such as ordinary sand; **Central Government may notify additional minerals as minor**.
- **§15:** **State Government** may make rules regulating quarry leases/mining leases/other mineral concessions for minor minerals.
- Therefore:
  - State has **rule-making/concession competence** for minor minerals.
  - Centre has **notification/classification power** under §3(e).
  - “State Governments have no rule-making power” = false. [India Code](https://www.indiacode.nic.in/bitstream/123456789/19380/1/mmdr_act%2C1957.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### Inter-State Council
- **Article 263:** President may establish an Inter-State Council if public interest would be served; constitutional functions may include inter-State dispute advice, discussion of common-interest subjects and coordination recommendations. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- Sarkaria Commission recommended a permanent Inter-State Council; Union established ISC by Presidential Order in **1990** under Art. 263. [Ministry of Home Affairs](https://www.mha.gov.in/sites/default/files/REPORT_1990_91_12022021.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- **Status trap:** Inter-State Council → constitutional provision; Zonal Councils → statutory; National Security Council → executive/non-constitutional.

### Emergency map

| Type | Article | Ground | Parliamentary approval | Continuance |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| **National Emergency** | **352** | war / external aggression / armed rebellion; imminent danger sufficient | Both Houses within **1 month**; majority of total membership + ≥2/3 present & voting | 6 months per approval; repeatable |
| **President’s Rule** | **356** | State govt cannot be carried on according to Constitution | Both Houses within **2 months**; simple majority | 6 months per approval; ordinarily max **3 years**; beyond 1 year → extra conditions |
| **Financial Emergency** | **360** | financial stability/credit of India or any part threatened | Both Houses within **2 months**; simple majority | no periodic six-month renewal specified |

The current official Constitution confirms Art. 352’s three grounds, written Cabinet decision, one-month approval and special-majority requirement. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### National Emergency — high-yield effects
- Cabinet decision must be communicated to President **in writing**.
- Can cover whole or part of India; imminent danger sufficient.
- Parliament → State List competence; States continue legislating, subject to Parliamentary predominance.
- Union executive directions → any State matter within constitutional emergency effect.
- President may modify Arts. 268–279 revenue arrangements within constitutional time-limit.
- LS/State Assembly term may be extended by Parliament one year at a time; not beyond 6 months after emergency ends.
- **Art. 358:** Art. 19 effect only where Emergency is based on **war/external aggression**, not armed rebellion.
- **Art. 359:** President may suspend **court enforcement** of specified Part III rights; **Arts. 20 & 21 excluded**; rights themselves ≠ automatically extinguished. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- Emergency approval is **not an exclusive Lok Sabha power**.
- LS has special disapproval mechanism: notice by ≥1/10 total LS membership → special sitting within 14 days; if LS disapproves, President must revoke. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

### President’s Rule
- **Art. 355:** Union duty to protect States against external aggression/internal disturbance + ensure constitutional government.
- **Art. 356:** President may act **on Governor’s report or otherwise**.
- **Art. 365:** failure to comply with Union constitutional directions permits President to hold that constitutional government has failed.
- Consequences:
  - State Council of Ministers may be removed.
  - Assembly may be **suspended or dissolved** → dissolution is **not automatic**.
  - State Legislature’s powers → exercisable by/under authority of Parliament.
  - President **cannot assume High Court powers or suspend constitutional provisions relating to High Courts**. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
  - Fundamental Rights are **not suspended merely because Art. 356 operates**.
  - Laws made under Art. 356/357 continue after President’s Rule until altered/repealed/amended by competent legislature/authority. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- Duration:
  - approval: both Houses within 2 months;
  - continuation: 6-month blocks;
  - max ordinarily 3 years;
  - beyond 1 year: National Emergency must operate in whole India or whole/part of concerned State **and** ECI must certify difficulty in holding Assembly election. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- **S.R. Bommai:** Art. 356 proclamation subject to judicial review; federal character materially constrains arbitrary use of Art. 356. [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/11570.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)

### Financial Emergency
- **Art. 360:** threat to financial stability/credit of India or any part.
- Union may direct States to observe specified canons of financial propriety.
- Direction may require reduction of salaries/allowances of State personnel.
- State Money Bills / Art. 207 financial bills may be required to be reserved for President.
- President may direct reduction in Union-service salaries, including SC/HC judges. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)

## 5. UPPCS Extension Layer
- **Thinker ↔ federal description**
  - K.C. Wheare → **Quasi-federal**
  - Granville Austin → **Co-operative federalism**
  - Morris-Jones → **Bargaining federalism**
  - Ivor Jennings → **Federation with a strong centralising tendency**
- **Article recall:** 249 → RS/national interest; 263 → Inter-State Council; 352 → National Emergency; 356 → President’s Rule; 360 → Financial Emergency.
- **Commission recall:** Sarkaria Commission → Centre–State relations; recommended permanent ISC and sparing use of Art. 356.
- **No State-specific Uttar Pradesh constitutional extension identified** in canonical UPPCS demand.

## 6. PYQ Trap Checklist
- Federation **≠** agreement among Indian States.
- Union of States → States **cannot secede**.
- Federal character → independent judiciary **yes**; single citizenship **no**.
- Art. 249 majority = **2/3 present & voting**, not Article 368 special majority.
- Art. 249 resolution ≠ Parliamentary law: resolution ≤1 year; law survives another 6 months.
- Art. 253 → Parliament can implement international obligations **without State consent**.
- Treaty/agreement/convention/ international-body decision ≠ only “signed + ratified treaty”.
- Art. 252 → State requests Parliament ≠ State can later amend/repeal resulting Parliamentary Act.
- Concurrent State law with Presidential assent can prevail **in that State only**; Parliament can override later.
- Minor minerals: **State rules/concessions** ≠ **Centre notification of what counts as minor mineral**.
- ISC = constitutional provision; Zonal Councils = statutory; NSC = executive.
- National Emergency approval ≠ Lok Sabha alone.
- Art. 352: **war/external aggression/armed rebellion** ≠ internal disturbance.
- Art. 358 = Article 19 + war/external aggression; Art. 359 = enforcement of specified FR; Arts. 20/21 protected.
- President’s Rule ≠ automatic Assembly dissolution.
- President’s Rule ≠ dissolution of local bodies.
- Art. 356 ≠ suspension of Fundamental Rights.
- Art. 356 → State legislative power under Parliament; **High Court powers cannot be assumed**.
- Art. 356 approval = 2 months/simple majority ≠ Art. 352 = 1 month/special majority.
- Financial Emergency = Art. 360 ≠ President’s Rule Art. 356.
- Art. 356 law continues after proclamation ≠ automatically expires with President’s Rule.

## 7. Correction / Verification Register

### CORRECT
- Base: **Art. 253 = international treaty/agreement “signed and ratified”** → **Art. 253 covers treaty, agreement, convention, or decision of international conference/association/body; Constitution does not make the base-note “signed and ratified” phrase the operative test.** [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- Base: **Art. 252 requesting States “lose their rights to legislate on that matter further”** → narrow to: **resulting Parliamentary Act cannot be amended/repealed by the State legislature; Parliament may amend/repeal it in like manner.** [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- Base: **Art. 249 extension by “special majority”** → write precisely **≥2/3 members of Rajya Sabha present and voting**; avoid confusion with Article 368 formula. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- Base: **National Emergency: LS approval “within 1 month of reconstitution”** → **within 30 days from first sitting of reconstituted LS** where RS has meanwhile approved. [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- Base: **Emergency revocation framed as President “on advice of CoM”** → constitutional exam rule: **revocable by subsequent proclamation; President must revoke if LS passes disapproval resolution.**
- Base: **President’s Rule laws “remain valid forever until repealed/amended by State”** → **continue until altered/repealed/amended by a competent legislature or authority.** [Legislative India](https://www.legislative.gov.in/static/uploads/2025/07/88cca69e868e50b217f855be2fb8bdba.pdf)
- Base: **“44th Amendment made President’s satisfaction [Art. 356] subject to judicial review”** → retain safer rule: **Art. 356 proclamation is judicially reviewable; S.R. Bommai is the landmark authority.** [Sci API](https://api.sci.gov.in/jonew/judis/11570.pdf?utm_source=chatgpt.com)
- Base: **Article `275A` removed by 44th Amendment** → typo; **Article 257A** was omitted by the 44th Amendment.
- Base: **“Presidential Acts” / consultation with MPs** → do not use as primary constitutional formulation; use Art. 357 delegation/continuity language.

### VERIFY
- None materially blocking.

### OMIT
- Dynamic claim “Financial Emergency has never been imposed” from frozen payload; not needed to answer identified PYQs.
- Dynamic mineral-royalty judgments/current litigation → taxonomy explicitly excludes.
- Detailed current ISC composition/reconstitution data.
- Broad Sarkaria/Punchhi recommendation catalogue beyond PYQ-relevant distinctions.
- Territorial-nexus case-name mnemonic and “12 nautical miles” base-note aside.
- Historical Emergency proclamations/counts unless later PYQ demand specifically requires them.

## 8. Supplement Decision
`NO_SUPPLEMENT_NEEDED`

Current taxonomy + canonical PYQs + base notes + Laxmikanth cross-check + official Constitution/MMDR/ISC/Supreme Court verification answer all identified demands. Ghatna Chakra supplementation is not required for a material gap.
